# 7. AI AUTONOMY LEVELS
# ============================================================
#
# LEVEL 0 — HUMAN ONLY
# Strategic and legally sensitive decisions.
#
# LEVEL 1 — AI RECOMMENDATION
# AI analyses and recommends.
# CEO approves.
#
# LEVEL 2 — AI PREPARATION
# AI prepares documents, quotes, emails and workflows.
# Human approval required before external execution.
#
# LEVEL 3 — APPROVED AUTOMATION
# AI may execute predefined low-risk workflows.
#
# LEVEL 4 — CONTROLLED AUTONOMY
# AI may execute within strict financial, operational and
# permission limits.
#
# No AI may exceed its permission scope.
#
# ============================================================
# 8. FINANCIAL CONTROLS
# ============================================================
#
# AI must NOT independently:
# - change bank accounts
# - change payment destinations
# - make unrestricted major payments
# - approve major credit
# - sign major contracts
# - transfer ownership
#
# Financial thresholds must be configurable.
#
# Example:
#
# Low-value approved operating expense:
# AI automation allowed.
#
# Medium-value transaction:
# CEO approval required.
#
# High-value transaction:
# CEO + appropriate human verification required.
#

# ============================================================
# 3.3 AI AGENT PERMISSION LEVELS
# ============================================================

# LEVEL 0 — OBSERVE
#
# AI can:
# - read approved data
# - analyse information
# - produce reports
#
# No external action.

# LEVEL 1 — RECOMMEND
#
# AI can:
# - prepare recommendations
# - draft documents
# - prepare quotations
# - prepare messages
#
# Human approval before execution.

# LEVEL 2 — EXECUTE LOW-RISK
#
# AI can perform pre-approved repetitive actions such as:
# - routine notifications
# - internal status updates
# - approved customer updates
# - approved workflow transitions
# - reporting
#
# All activity logged.

# LEVEL 3 — CONTROLLED EXECUTION
#
# AI can execute predefined operational transactions
# within strict limits.
#
# Examples:
# - approved purchase thresholds
# - approved pricing ranges
# - approved logistics workflows
#
# Exceptions automatically escalate.

# LEVEL 4 — HUMAN APPROVAL REQUIRED
#
# Required for:
# - major payments
# - new banking instructions
# - major contracts
# - legal commitments
# - strategic partnerships
# - new countries
# - high-risk customers
# - high-risk suppliers
# - material pricing exceptions
# - ownership matters

# LEVEL 5 — FOUNDER RESERVED
#
# Founder / Chairman / CEO only.
#
# - ownership
# - corporate structure
# - strategic direction
# - acquisition/disposal
# - major investment
# - major financing
# - appointment/removal of senior executives
# - AI authority changes
# - major market expansion
# - ultimate business decisions

# ============================================================
# 3.4 HUMAN-IN-THE-LOOP
# ============================================================

# Every high-risk workflow must contain an approval gate.

# Example:
#
# AI identifies opportunity
#        ↓
# AI analyses opportunity
#        ↓
# AI prepares recommendation
#        ↓
# CEO Dashboard approval
#        ↓
# execution
#        ↓
# audit log
#
# No hidden AI decisions.

# ============================================================
# 3.5 AI AUDIT LOG
# ============================================================

# Every material AI action should record:
#
# - timestamp
# - agent
# - user
# - workflow
# - input
# - decision
# - action
# - approval status
# - outcome
# - exception
# - related customer
# - related supplier
# - related order
# - related shipment
#
# Audit records must be searchable from the CEO Dashboard.

# ============================================================
# 3.6 AI ALERT SYSTEM
# ============================================================

# CRITICAL
# - fraud indicators
# - sanctions/compliance concerns
# - major payment anomaly
# - major shipment failure
# - major customer failure
# - legal/regulatory issue
#
# HIGH
# - serious delay
# - margin collapse
# - supplier failure
# - significant cash-flow issue
#
# MEDIUM
# - unusual operational trend
# - approaching deadline
# - customer issue
#
# LOW
# - routine reminder
# - reporting item
# - minor operational task

# ============================================================
# 3.7 CEO DAILY BRIEF
# ============================================================

# The CEO Dashboard should generate an executive brief
# covering:

# 1. Cash position
# 2. Revenue
# 3. Gross margin
# 4. Orders
# 5. Shipments
# 6. Customers
# 7. Suppliers
# 8. Outstanding invoices
# 9. Risks
# 10. AI actions
# 11. Required approvals
# 12. Opportunities
# 13. Strategic recommendations

# ============================================================
# 3.8 AI DECISION RULE
# ============================================================

# AI should optimise for:
#
# 1. legality
# 2. safety
# 3. compliance
# 4. customer trust
# 5. profitability
# 6. cash preservation
# 7. operational reliability
# 8. scalable growth
#
# AI must never optimise solely for revenue.

# ============================================================
# END PART 3
# ============================================================


# ============================================================
# 5. TRILINK COMMERCIAL ENGINE
# ============================================================

# PURPOSE
#
# Build a scalable AI-operated commercial engine that connects
# buyers, suppliers, logistics providers and trade opportunities
# through the TriLink operating system.

# ============================================================
# 5.1 CORE COMMERCIAL MODEL
# ============================================================

# TriLink operates as a technology-enabled trade and logistics
# coordination business.

# Core value proposition:
#
# - identify demand
# - identify supply
# - match buyer and supplier
# - calculate commercial viability
# - coordinate documentation
# - coordinate logistics
# - track shipment
# - manage communication
# - monitor payment
# - report performance
#
# TriLink should focus on transactions where it can create
# measurable value rather than simply acting as a directory.

# ============================================================
# 5.2 CUSTOMER TYPES
# ============================================================

# PRIMARY CUSTOMER GROUPS
#
# 1. Importers
# 2. Exporters
# 3. Wholesalers
# 4. Distributors
# 5. Retailers
# 6. Manufacturers
# 7. Trading companies
# 8. Logistics companies
# 9. Institutional buyers
# 10. Energy-sector buyers

# ============================================================
# 5.3 SUPPLIER TYPES
# ============================================================

# 1. Manufacturers
# 2. Producers
# 3. Exporters
# 4. Distributors
# 5. Energy suppliers
# 6. Logistics providers
# 7. Freight forwarders
# 8. Warehousing providers
# 9. Customs/documentation specialists
# 10. Local service providers

# ============================================================
# 5.4 COMMERCIAL WORKFLOW
# ============================================================

# STEP 1 — LEAD IDENTIFICATION
#
# AI identifies potential:
# - buyers
# - suppliers
# - logistics partners
# - strategic partners

# STEP 2 — QUALIFICATION
#
# AI collects:
# - company identity
# - country
# - sector
# - products
# - estimated volume
# - buying/selling requirements
# - contact information
# - commercial requirements

# STEP 3 — VERIFICATION
#
# AI checks available business information and flags
# information requiring human verification.

# STEP 4 — MATCHING
#
# AI matches:
#
# buyer
# +
# product
# +
# supplier
# +
# route
# +
# logistics
# +
# commercial terms

# STEP 5 — COST MODEL
#
# AI calculates:
#
# purchase price
# + freight
# + insurance where applicable
# + handling
# + documentation
# + taxes/duties where known
# + operating costs
# + TriLink margin
# = estimated landed cost

# Important:
# AI estimates must clearly distinguish estimates from
# confirmed charges.

# STEP 6 — COMMERCIAL PROPOSAL
#
# AI prepares:
# - quotation
# - commercial proposal
# - pricing explanation
# - delivery estimate
# - assumptions
# - validity period

# STEP 7 — APPROVAL
#
# CEO Dashboard routes the proposal for approval where required.

# STEP 8 — ORDER
#
# Approved commercial agreement becomes an active order.

# STEP 9 — PROCUREMENT
#
# AI coordinates approved supplier workflow.

# STEP 10 — LOGISTICS
#
# AI coordinates shipment workflow.

# STEP 11 — DELIVERY
#
# AI tracks delivery and exceptions.

# STEP 12 — PAYMENT
#
# AI tracks invoices and payment status.
#
# High-risk or major payments require human approval.

# STEP 13 — CLOSE
#
# AI records:
# - revenue
# - costs
# - margin
# - customer result
# - supplier result
# - logistics performance

# STEP 14 — LEARNING
#
# AI analyses the transaction and improves future
# recommendations.

# ============================================================
# 5.5 REVENUE MODEL
# ============================================================

# TriLink may use multiple revenue streams.

# A. TRADE MARGIN
#
# Earn a controlled margin on transactions where TriLink
# takes an appropriate commercial role.

# B. TRANSACTION FEE
#
# Charge a transparent coordination fee for qualifying
# transactions.

# C. LOGISTICS COORDINATION FEE
#
# Charge for logistics coordination where appropriate.

# D. PROCUREMENT FEE
#
# Charge for sourcing/procurement services.

# E. B2B SUBSCRIPTION
#
# Future option for businesses requiring recurring access
# to TriLink services and intelligence.

# F. PREMIUM SERVICES
#
# Examples:
# - priority sourcing
# - dedicated account management
# - advanced reporting
# - market intelligence
# - supply-chain analysis

# ============================================================
# 5.6 PRICING ENGINE
# ============================================================

# The AI pricing engine should consider:
#
# - supplier price
# - quantity
# - currency
# - freight
# - route
# - insurance
# - handling
# - duties/taxes where applicable
# - payment terms
# - expected risk
# - operating cost
# - desired margin
# - market conditions

# The system should produce:
#
# COST
# SELLING PRICE
# GROSS PROFIT
# GROSS MARGIN %
# RISK ADJUSTMENT
# CONFIDENCE SCORE

# ============================================================
# 5.7 MARGIN PROTECTION
# ============================================================

# AI must flag:
#
# - negative-margin transactions
# - unusually low margin
# - unexpected freight increases
# - currency exposure
# - supplier price changes
# - customer price pressure
# - unexpected fees
# - route-cost changes

# AI should recommend:
#
# - revised price
# - alternative supplier
# - alternative route
# - alternative quantity
# - negotiation strategy

# ============================================================
# 5.8 CUSTOMER PIPELINE
# ============================================================

# PIPELINE STAGES
#
# Lead
# ↓
# Qualified
# ↓
# Verified
# ↓
# Opportunity
# ↓
# Proposal
# ↓
# Negotiation
# ↓
# Approved
# ↓
# Contracted
# ↓
# Order
# ↓
# Delivered
# ↓
# Repeat Customer

# ============================================================
# 5.9 CUSTOMER SCORING
# ============================================================

# AI assigns a commercial score based on:
#
# - business legitimacy
# - demand quality
# - expected volume
# - payment reliability
# - strategic fit
# - geographic fit
# - margin potential
# - operational complexity
# - risk indicators

# AI recommendation:
#
# HIGH VALUE
# MEDIUM VALUE
# LOW VALUE
# REVIEW REQUIRED

# ============================================================
# 5.10 SUPPLIER SCORING
# ============================================================

# Supplier score considers:
#
# - legitimacy
# - product quality
# - pricing
# - capacity
# - delivery performance
# - documentation
# - responsiveness
# - payment terms
# - historical performance
# - risk indicators

# Supplier ratings:
#
# A — Strategic
# B — Approved
# C — Conditional
# D — Review
# E — Blocked

# No supplier should be automatically treated as trusted
# solely because AI found it online.

# ============================================================
# 5.11 TRANSACTION RISK ENGINE
# ============================================================

# Every transaction receives a risk assessment.

# RISK FACTORS
#
# - new customer
# - new supplier
# - unusual payment request
# - changed bank details
# - unusual pricing
# - unusual route
# - sanctions concerns
# - missing documentation
# - inconsistent company information
# - unexpected urgency
# - large transaction
# - politically sensitive/high-risk jurisdiction
# - product-specific regulatory concerns

# HIGH-RISK TRANSACTIONS:
#
# STOP AUTOMATION
# ↓
# CREATE ALERT
# ↓
# CEO DASHBOARD REVIEW
# ↓
# HUMAN DECISION
# ↓
# RESUME OR REJECT

# ============================================================
# 5.12 COMMERCIAL DOCUMENT ENGINE
# ============================================================

# AI can prepare drafts for:
#
# - quotation
# - purchase order
# - sales order
# - commercial invoice
# - packing list
# - pro-forma invoice
# - supplier request
# - buyer request
# - shipment instruction
# - delivery confirmation
# - payment reminder
# - account statement

# All documents must contain:
#
# - document ID
# - date
# - parties
# - transaction reference
# - currency
# - terms
# - validity
# - relevant assumptions
#
# Final legal/commercial documents must follow applicable
# law and contractual requirements.

# ============================================================
# 5.13 COMMUNICATION ENGINE
# ============================================================

# AI may draft and, where authorised, send:
#
# - lead outreach
# - quotation emails
# - order confirmations
# - shipment updates
# - delay notifications
# - payment reminders
# - customer service responses
# - supplier communications

# AI must never impersonate a human decision-maker.
#
# Communications should clearly operate on behalf of TriLink.

# ============================================================
# 5.14 CUSTOMER PORTAL
# ============================================================

# Future customer portal should allow authorised customers to:
#
# - view orders
# - view quotations
# - view invoices
# - view shipment status
# - upload documents
# - communicate with TriLink
# - request quotations
# - review delivery status
# - access approved reports

# ============================================================
# 5.15 SUPPLIER PORTAL
# ============================================================

# Supplier portal should allow authorised suppliers to:
#
# - receive enquiries
# - submit quotations
# - confirm availability
# - upload documents
# - confirm orders
# - update shipment status
# - receive approved purchase information

# ============================================================
# 5.16 CEO COMMERCIAL KPIs
# ============================================================

# DASHBOARD KPIs
#
# - total pipeline
# - qualified pipeline
# - conversion rate
# - active customers
# - active suppliers
# - order value
# - shipment value
# - revenue
# - gross profit
# - gross margin
# - average order value
# - customer acquisition cost
# - customer retention
# - repeat-order rate
# - supplier performance
# - shipment success rate
# - payment collection rate
# - outstanding receivables

# ============================================================
# 5.17 AI COMMERCIAL TARGET
# ============================================================

# The goal is NOT to maximise the number of transactions.
#
# The goal is to maximise:
#
# PROFITABLE
# + COMPLIANT
# + RELIABLE
# + REPEATABLE
# + SCALABLE
#
# commercial transactions.

# ============================================================
# 5.18 FIRST MARKET STRATEGY
# ============================================================

# Initial strategic corridor:
#
# NEPAL
#   ↓
# INDIA
#   ↓
# BANGLADESH
#
# Initial focus should remain narrow enough to build reliable
# operating processes before expanding internationally.

# Priority:
#
# 1. validate demand
# 2. validate suppliers
# 3. validate route economics
# 4. complete pilot transactions
# 5. measure margins
# 6. improve AI workflows
# 7. scale repeat transactions
# 8. expand product categories
# 9. expand corridors
# 10. expand countries

# ============================================================
# 5.19 ENERGY BUSINESS
# ============================================================

# TriLink Energy operates as a specialised business unit.
#
# Potential areas:
#
# - energy equipment
# - industrial energy products
# - renewable-energy equipment
# - solar-related products
# - electrical equipment
# - energy procurement
# - energy logistics
# - commercial energy partnerships

# Energy opportunities require additional technical,
# regulatory and safety review.

# AI may identify and analyse opportunities.
#
# Human experts must approve regulated, technically complex,
# safety-critical or legally sensitive transactions.

# ============================================================
# 5.20 COMMERCIAL EXPANSION RULE
# ============================================================

# No new market should be opened simply because AI predicts
# an opportunity.

# Expansion requires:
#
# MARKET CASE
# +
# SUPPLY CASE
# +
# CUSTOMER CASE
# +
# LOGISTICS CASE
# +
# FINANCIAL CASE
# +
# RISK CASE
# +
# COMPLIANCE CASE
# +
# CEO APPROVAL

# ============================================================
# END PART 5
# ============================================================


# ============================================================
# 6. TECHNOLOGY + DATA ARCHITECTURE
# ============================================================

# CORE TECHNOLOGY PRINCIPLE
#
# Platformsify is the technology operating layer for TriLink.
#
# The TriLink CEO Dashboard inside Platformsify is the
# SINGLE executive control centre.
#
# DO NOT BUILD:
# - a second CEO dashboard
# - a competing admin dashboard
# - a separate executive control system
# - duplicate business-management interfaces
#
# All executive information should ultimately be available
# through the Platformsify CEO Dashboard.

# ============================================================
# 6.1 SYSTEM ARCHITECTURE
# ============================================================

# HIGH-LEVEL ARCHITECTURE
#
#                    FOUNDER / CEO
#                         |
#                         v
#              PLATFORMSIFY CEO DASHBOARD
#                         |
#              +----------+----------+
#              |          |          |
#              v          v          v
#           AI CORE    BUSINESS    ALERTS
#              |       SERVICES       |
#              |          |           |
#       +------+----------+-----------+------+
#       |      |          |           |      |
#       v      v          v           v      v
#      CRM   TRADE     LOGISTICS   FINANCE  ENERGY
#       |      |          |           |      |
#       +------+----------+-----------+------+
#                         |
#                         v
#                    DATA LAYER
#                         |
#                         v
#                AUDIT / ANALYTICS
#
# ============================================================
# 6.2 SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH
# ============================================================

# The system must maintain one authoritative record for each:
#
# - customer
# - supplier
# - product
# - quotation
# - order
# - shipment
# - invoice
# - payment
# - contract
# - employee/user
# - AI agent
# - approval
# - alert
#
# Duplicate records should be detected and merged through
# controlled workflows.

# ============================================================
# 6.3 CORE DATA ENTITIES
# ============================================================

# ORGANISATION
#
# Fields:
# - organisation_id
# - legal_name
# - trading_name
# - country
# - address
# - registration_information
# - tax_information
# - contact_information
# - status
# - risk_score
# - created_at
# - updated_at

# CONTACT
#
# - contact_id
# - organisation_id
# - name
# - role
# - email
# - phone
# - status

# PRODUCT
#
# - product_id
# - name
# - category
# - description
# - unit
# - supplier
# - compliance_requirements
# - status

# SUPPLIER
#
# - supplier_id
# - organisation_id
# - supplier_score
# - approval_status
# - payment_terms
# - performance_score
# - risk_status

# CUSTOMER
#
# - customer_id
# - organisation_id
# - customer_score
# - account_status
# - payment_score
# - lifetime_value

# LEAD
#
# - lead_id
# - organisation_id
# - source
# - score
# - owner
# - stage
# - next_action
# - status

# OPPORTUNITY
#
# - opportunity_id
# - customer_id
# - product_id
# - estimated_value
# - estimated_margin
# - probability
# - stage
# - risk_score

# QUOTATION
#
# - quotation_id
# - customer_id
# - opportunity_id
# - currency
# - total_value
# - estimated_cost
# - estimated_margin
# - validity
# - approval_status
# - status

# ORDER
#
# - order_id
# - customer_id
# - supplier_id
# - quotation_id
# - products
# - quantity
# - value
# - currency
# - status
# - approval_status

# SHIPMENT
#
# - shipment_id
# - order_id
# - origin
# - destination
# - route
# - carrier
# - tracking_reference
# - estimated_departure
# - estimated_arrival
# - actual_arrival
# - status
# - exception_status

# INVOICE
#
# - invoice_id
# - customer_id
# - order_id
# - amount
# - currency
# - issue_date
# - due_date
# - status
# - payment_status

# PAYMENT
#
# - payment_id
# - invoice_id
# - amount
# - currency
# - payment_date
# - method
# - status
# - approval_reference

# CONTRACT
#
# - contract_id
# - parties
# - contract_type
# - start_date
# - end_date
# - value
# - status
# - renewal_status
# - approval_status

# APPROVAL
#
# - approval_id
# - object_type
# - object_id
# - requested_by
# - approved_by
# - decision
# - reason
# - timestamp

# AI ACTION
#
# - ai_action_id
# - agent_id
# - workflow_id
# - action_type
# - risk_level
# - input_reference
# - output_reference
# - approval_required
# - approval_status
# - execution_status
# - timestamp

# ============================================================
# 6.4 IDENTITY + ACCESS CONTROL
# ============================================================

# Every user and AI agent must have a defined identity.

# ACCESS PRINCIPLE:
#
# LEAST PRIVILEGE
#
# Users and AI agents receive only the permissions necessary
# to perform their approved tasks.

# ============================================================
# 6.5 USER ROLES
# ============================================================

# FOUNDER / CHAIRMAN / CEO
#
# Ultimate business authority.

# EXECUTIVE
#
# Access determined by assigned responsibilities.

# FINANCE
#
# Finance-related access only.

# OPERATIONS
#
# Operational workflows only.

# SALES
#
# CRM and sales workflows.

# LOGISTICS
#
# Shipment and logistics workflows.

# COMPLIANCE
#
# Risk and compliance workflows.

# SUPPORT
#
# Customer-support workflows.

# AI AGENT
#
# Machine identity with explicitly defined permissions.

# READ-ONLY
#
# Reporting and information access only.

# ============================================================
# 6.6 AI PERMISSION MODEL
# ============================================================

# Every AI agent must have:
#
# - agent_id
# - purpose
# - permitted_data
# - permitted_actions
# - maximum_transaction_value
# - approval_requirements
# - escalation_rules
# - operating_status
#
# No AI agent receives unrestricted access by default.

# ============================================================
# 6.7 SECRETS MANAGEMENT
# ============================================================

# API keys, database credentials, payment credentials and
# authentication secrets must NEVER be hard-coded into source
# files.

# Use environment variables and secure secret storage.

# NEVER commit:
#
# - API keys
# - passwords
# - private keys
# - database passwords
# - payment secrets
# - authentication tokens
#
# to Git.

# ============================================================
# 6.8 ENVIRONMENT SEPARATION
# ============================================================

# DEVELOPMENT
#
# Used for building and testing.

# STAGING
#
# Used for final pre-production testing.

# PRODUCTION
#
# Live TriLink business environment.

# Production credentials must never be copied into development
# environments unnecessarily.

# ============================================================
# 6.9 DEPLOYMENT PRINCIPLE
# ============================================================

# Deployment must follow:
#
# CODE
# ↓
# TEST
# ↓
# BUILD
# ↓
# SECURITY CHECK
# ↓
# STAGING
# ↓
# ROUTE TEST
# ↓
# APPROVAL
# ↓
# PRODUCTION
#
# Never deploy untested changes directly into production.

# ============================================================
# 6.10 BACKUP STRATEGY
# ============================================================

# Maintain:
#
# - database backups
# - configuration backups
# - source-control history
# - deployment records
# - audit logs
# - critical business documents
#
# Backups must be tested periodically.
#
# A backup is not considered reliable until restoration has
# been tested.

# ============================================================
# 6.11 DISASTER RECOVERY
# ============================================================

# The business must be able to recover from:
#
# - application failure
# - database failure
# - cloud outage
# - accidental deletion
# - compromised credentials
# - deployment failure
# - third-party service outage
#
# Recovery objectives should be defined as the platform matures.

# ============================================================
# 6.12 OBSERVABILITY
# ============================================================

# Monitor:
#
# - application uptime
# - API errors
# - database health
# - AI failures
# - workflow failures
# - payment failures
# - shipment exceptions
# - authentication failures
# - unusual traffic
# - infrastructure costs
#
# Critical alerts must reach the CEO Dashboard.

# ============================================================
# 6.13 AI SAFETY ARCHITECTURE
# ============================================================

# AI requests should pass through controlled tools/workflows.
#
# AI should NOT receive unrestricted database or infrastructure
# access simply because it is an AI system.

# TOOL ACCESS SHOULD BE:
#
# - scoped
# - authenticated
# - logged
# - rate-limited
# - permission-checked
# - reversible where possible

# ============================================================
# 6.14 AI OUTPUT VALIDATION
# ============================================================

# Before an AI-generated output becomes a business action:
#
# 1. validate format
# 2. validate required fields
# 3. validate permissions
# 4. validate business rules
# 5. validate financial limits
# 6. validate risk conditions
# 7. require approval where applicable
#
# AI-generated text must not automatically be treated as fact.

# ============================================================
# 6.15 HUMAN APPROVAL SERVICE
# ============================================================

# The CEO Dashboard should provide a central approval queue.

# Example:
#
# APPROVAL REQUEST
#
# Type: Major Purchase
# Supplier: Example Supplier
# Value: £XX,XXX
# Estimated Margin: XX%
# Risk: Medium
# AI Recommendation: Approve
#
# ACTION:
# [APPROVE]
# [REJECT]
# [REQUEST MORE INFORMATION]

# Every decision is recorded.

# ============================================================
# 6.16 NOTIFICATION SYSTEM
# ============================================================

# Notifications should be categorised:
#
# CRITICAL
# HIGH
# MEDIUM
# LOW
#
# Users should not be overwhelmed with routine alerts.
#
# AI should consolidate non-critical notifications into useful
# summaries.

# ============================================================
# 6.17 CEO DASHBOARD HOME
# ============================================================

# The main CEO Dashboard should prioritise:
#
# 1. TODAY
# 2. CASH
# 3. REVENUE
# 4. PROFIT
# 5. ACTIVE ORDERS
# 6. SHIPMENTS
# 7. RISKS
# 8. APPROVALS
# 9. AI ACTIVITY
# 10. OPPORTUNITIES
#
# The Founder should understand the company position quickly
# without navigating through multiple management systems.

# ============================================================
# 6.18 AI ACTIVITY CENTRE
# ============================================================

# CEO Dashboard should display:
#
# - AI tasks completed
# - AI tasks pending
# - AI recommendations
# - AI actions executed
# - AI actions blocked
# - escalations
# - exceptions
# - failed workflows
#
# The Founder should always be able to see what the AI is doing.

# ============================================================
# 6.19 AI KILL SWITCH
# ============================================================

# The CEO Dashboard must provide the ability to suspend
# automated AI execution.

# EMERGENCY ACTION:
#
# STOP AI EXECUTION
#
# This should:
#
# - stop new autonomous actions
# - preserve data
# - preserve audit logs
# - allow human operation
# - notify authorised administrators
#
# Critical human-controlled systems should remain accessible.

# ============================================================
# 6.20 AI AGENT LIFECYCLE
# ============================================================

# CREATE
# ↓
# TEST
# ↓
# SECURITY REVIEW
# ↓
# LIMITED PERMISSION
# ↓
# PILOT
# ↓
# PERFORMANCE REVIEW
# ↓
# APPROVED
# ↓
# PRODUCTION
# ↓
# MONITOR
# ↓
# RETIRE / UPDATE

# No new AI agent should automatically receive production
# authority.

# ============================================================
# 6.21 DATA QUALITY ENGINE
# ============================================================

# AI should identify:
#
# - duplicate companies
# - duplicate contacts
# - missing fields
# - invalid data
# - inconsistent addresses
# - inconsistent currencies
# - stale records
# - conflicting commercial information

# Data quality alerts should appear in the CEO Dashboard.

# ============================================================

# ============================================================

# AUTOMATED REPORTS
#
# DAILY:
# - CEO brief
# - critical alerts
# - cash summary
# - operations summary
#
# WEEKLY:
# - revenue
# - margin
# - pipeline
# - customer performance
# - supplier performance
# - logistics performance
# - AI performance
#
# MONTHLY:
# - management accounts
# - profitability
# - cash-flow analysis
# - strategic KPIs
# - risk report
# - growth report

# ============================================================
# 6.23 TECHNOLOGY COST CONTROL
# ============================================================

# AI must monitor technology costs including:
#
# - AI API usage
# - cloud usage
# - database usage
# - storage
# - email
# - communications
# - payment processing
# - third-party services
#
# Unexpected increases should generate alerts.

# ============================================================

# External services may be used when they provide clear
# business value.

# Before production use:
#

# - review reliability
# - establish fallback where necessary

# Avoid unnecessary vendor duplication.

# ============================================================
# 7. AI OPERATING MODEL
# ============================================================

## 7.1 AI-FIRST PRINCIPLE

TriLink Global is designed to operate as an AI-first business.

AI is responsible for approved operational workflows across:

- trade
- logistics
- energy
- customer operations
- supplier operations
- document processing
- quotations
- CRM
- reporting
- financial monitoring
- compliance monitoring
- business intelligence
- forecasting
- internal support
- executive reporting

The AI system must never be treated as an unrestricted decision-maker.

AI operates within defined permissions, financial limits, approval rules,
compliance rules and audit requirements.

The Founder / Chairman / CEO remains the ultimate authority.

## 7.2 PLATFORMSIFY CEO DASHBOARD

Platformsify CEO Dashboard is the SINGLE executive control centre.

The dashboard must provide one unified view of:

- company performance
- revenue
- orders
- shipments
- customers
- suppliers
- opportunities
- quotations
- invoices
- payments
- cash position
- energy projects
- logistics activity
- trade activity
- AI activity
- alerts
- risks
- compliance
- approvals
- strategic KPIs

No separate TriLink CEO management dashboard should be created.

If another internal interface is required, it must operate as a module
inside the Platformsify-controlled architecture rather than becoming
a competing executive control centre.

## 7.3 CEO HOME SCREEN

The CEO Dashboard home screen should display:

1. TODAY'S EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
2. REVENUE
3. GROSS MARGIN
4. CASH POSITION
5. ACTIVE ORDERS
6. ACTIVE SHIPMENTS
7. OPEN OPPORTUNITIES
8. CUSTOMER PIPELINE
9. SUPPLIER PIPELINE
10. ENERGY PROJECT PIPELINE
11. AI ACTIONS
12. APPROVALS REQUIRED
13. HIGH-RISK ALERTS
14. COMPLIANCE ALERTS
15. TOP PRIORITIES

The CEO should be able to understand the business position
without opening multiple systems.

## 7.4 AI COMMAND CENTRE

The CEO Dashboard must contain an AI Command Centre.

The CEO can issue approved commands such as:

- "Give me today's business report."
- "Show all delayed shipments."
- "Find our highest-value opportunities."
- "Show unpaid invoices."
- "Identify customers at risk."
- "Prepare a quotation."
- "Prepare a supplier comparison."
- "Analyse this shipment."
- "Show this month's profit."
- "Show energy projects requiring attention."
- "Prepare my executive briefing."

AI responses must include:

- result
- source data
- timestamp
- confidence where applicable
- recommended action
- approval requirement where applicable

## 7.5 AI EXECUTIVE REPORT

AI should automatically prepare an executive report containing:

### Financial

- revenue
- gross profit
- outstanding invoices
- overdue payments
- major expenses
- cash position
- forecast

### Commercial

- new leads
- qualified opportunities
- quotations
- wins
- losses
- conversion rate
- pipeline value

### Operations

- active orders
- shipments
- delays
- exceptions
- supplier performance
- customer service issues

### Energy

- active energy opportunities
- project stage
- estimated value
- supplier status
- customer status
- project risks

### AI

- actions completed
- actions awaiting approval
- failed actions
- exceptions
- system alerts

### CEO PRIORITIES

AI must finish every executive report with:

1. What needs attention now
2. What can wait
3. What AI recommends
4. What requires CEO approval

## 7.6 AI AGENT STRUCTURE

TriLink should use specialised AI agents operating under
one central governance framework.

### CEO AGENT

Purpose:

Executive intelligence and decision support.

Responsibilities:

- executive summaries
- KPI analysis
- strategic recommendations
- business forecasting
- priority management

The CEO Agent cannot override human approval requirements.

### TRADE AGENT

Responsibilities:

- supplier discovery
- buyer discovery
- product matching
- quotation preparation
- trade opportunity analysis
- document preparation
- trade workflow monitoring

### LOGISTICS AGENT

Responsibilities:

- shipment tracking
- route monitoring
- ETA monitoring
- exception detection
- logistics cost analysis
- delivery reporting

### ENERGY AGENT

Responsibilities:

- energy opportunity identification
- project pipeline management
- supplier matching
- project documentation
- project economics
- milestone monitoring

### SALES AGENT

Responsibilities:

- lead qualification
- CRM updates
- follow-up scheduling
- proposal preparation
- sales forecasting
- customer communications within approved rules

### FINANCE AGENT

Responsibilities:

- invoice monitoring
- payment status
- cash-flow forecasting
- financial reporting
- margin analysis
- anomaly detection

The Finance Agent must NOT independently move money.

### PROCUREMENT AGENT

Responsibilities:

- supplier comparison
- procurement analysis
- quotation comparison
- purchase recommendations
- supplier performance monitoring

### COMPLIANCE AGENT

Responsibilities:

- document checks
- sanctions/compliance screening workflows
- missing-document detection
- regulatory reminders
- transaction risk flags

High-risk decisions must be escalated to a human.

### CUSTOMER SUPPORT AGENT

Responsibilities:

- customer enquiries
- order status
- shipment status
- FAQs
- ticket classification
- escalation

### DOCUMENT AGENT

Responsibilities:

- generate approved business documents
- extract information
- classify documents
- identify missing information
- prepare drafts
- maintain document records

## 7.7 AI PERMISSION LEVELS

Every AI agent must operate under one of four permission levels.

### LEVEL 0 — READ ONLY

AI may:

- analyse data
- produce reports
- make recommendations

AI may not change records.

### LEVEL 1 — LOW-RISK AUTOMATION

AI may:

- update CRM records
- classify documents
- send approved notifications
- schedule approved follow-ups
- update operational statuses

### LEVEL 2 — APPROVAL REQUIRED

AI may prepare:

- contracts
- purchase orders
- quotations
- payment requests
- supplier agreements
- customer proposals

A human must approve before execution.

### LEVEL 3 — CEO ONLY

Requires Founder / Chairman / CEO approval:

- major contracts
- major payments
- bank changes
- ownership changes
- strategic partnerships
- high-risk transactions
- new-country expansion
- major supplier/customer onboarding
- changes to AI permissions
- changes to company structure

## 7.8 AI AUDIT LOG

Every AI action must be recorded.

Required fields:

- timestamp
- agent
- user/requester
- action
- data used
- decision
- confidence
- permission level
- approval status
- resulting action
- error/exception
- final outcome

The CEO must be able to search the AI audit history.

## 7.9 HUMAN OVERRIDE

The Founder / Chairman / CEO must have an immediate override capability.

CEO controls must include:

- pause AI agent
- disable automation
- revoke permission
- require manual approval
- stop workflow
- review audit history
- restore approved workflow

No AI agent may prevent the CEO from stopping its activity.

## 7.10 AI FAILURE MANAGEMENT

If an AI agent:

- loses access to required data
- produces inconsistent results
- detects uncertainty
- encounters a compliance issue
- encounters a financial anomaly
- encounters conflicting instructions
- exceeds its permission level

the workflow must stop or escalate.

AI must never silently continue a high-risk workflow.

## 7.11 AI DATA SECURITY

The system must enforce:

- role-based access
- least-privilege permissions
- secure authentication
- encrypted connections
- protected credentials
- secret management
- audit logging
- environment separation
- backup procedures
- controlled API access

Secrets must never be committed to Git.

Production credentials must never be stored directly inside source code.

## 7.12 AI BUSINESS PRINCIPLE

The target operating model is:

HUMAN STRATEGY
        ↓
PLATFORMSIFY CEO DASHBOARD
        ↓
AI GOVERNANCE
        ↓
SPECIALISED AI AGENTS
        ↓
AUTOMATED OPERATIONS
        ↓
AUDIT + EXCEPTIONS
        ↓
CEO OVERSIGHT

The goal is not "AI with unlimited control."

The goal is:

AI AUTOMATION + HUMAN GOVERNANCE + FULL VISIBILITY.


# ============================================================
# 8. TRADE + LOGISTICS OPERATING SYSTEM
# ============================================================

## 8.1 CORE COMMERCIAL MODEL

TriLink Global will operate as an integrated AI-first trade and
logistics platform.

The business connects:

SUPPLIERS
    ↓
TRILINK TRADE
    ↓
LOGISTICS
    ↓
CUSTOMERS / BUYERS

The system should allow TriLink to manage the commercial journey
from opportunity through delivery.

Primary initial corridor:

NEPAL → INDIA → BANGLADESH

The architecture must remain flexible enough to support additional
countries and trade corridors later.

## 8.2 TRADE LIFECYCLE

Every trade opportunity should follow a controlled lifecycle:

1. Lead identified
2. Buyer identified
3. Supplier identified
4. Product identified
5. Product information collected
6. Compliance requirements checked
7. Supplier quotation requested
8. Logistics cost estimated
9. Landed cost calculated
10. Margin calculated
11. Customer quotation prepared
12. CEO/customer approval obtained where required
13. Purchase/order confirmed
14. Supplier confirmation
15. Logistics arranged
16. Documents collected
17. Shipment dispatched
18. Shipment monitored
19. Customs/document exceptions monitored
20. Delivery completed
21. Invoice issued
22. Payment monitored
23. Transaction closed
24. Profit recorded
25. Supplier/customer performance recorded

AI should monitor every stage.

## 8.3 OPPORTUNITY MANAGEMENT

Each opportunity must contain:

- opportunity ID
- customer
- country
- supplier
- product
- quantity
- estimated purchase price
- estimated logistics cost
- estimated duties/taxes where applicable
- estimated landed cost
- proposed selling price
- expected gross margin
- currency
- expected delivery time
- compliance status
- risk status
- current stage
- owner
- AI activity
- approval status

AI should automatically identify opportunities that require attention.

## 8.4 SUPPLIER MANAGEMENT

Supplier profiles must include:

- legal name
- country
- address
- contact details
- products
- certifications
- documents
- pricing
- payment terms
- lead time
- minimum order quantity
- historical performance
- delivery performance
- quality performance
- compliance status
- risk score
- active/inactive status

AI should compare suppliers based on approved criteria.

Supplier selection for material/high-value transactions requires
appropriate human approval.

## 8.5 CUSTOMER MANAGEMENT

Customer profiles must include:

- legal name
- country
- contact
- business type
- products of interest
- purchase history
- quotation history
- payment history
- credit/risk information where legally available
- preferred delivery terms
- relationship status
- opportunity value
- customer risk flags

AI may manage routine customer communications within approved rules.

Sensitive or commercially material communications should be
reviewable by management.

## 8.6 PRODUCT CATALOGUE

Every traded product should have a structured record.

Required fields:

- SKU/product ID
- product name
- category
- origin
- supplier
- specifications
- packaging
- unit
- MOQ
- purchase price
- target selling price
- currency
- estimated logistics cost
- estimated landed cost
- expected margin
- compliance requirements
- supporting documents
- status

AI should be able to search and compare products instantly.

## 8.7 PRICING ENGINE

The pricing engine should calculate:

PURCHASE COST
+
LOGISTICS
+
INSURANCE WHERE APPLICABLE
+
DUTIES/TAXES WHERE APPLICABLE
+
OTHER APPROVED COSTS
=
LANDED COST

Then:

LANDED COST
+
TARGET GROSS PROFIT
=
TARGET SELLING PRICE

The system should display:

- cost
- selling price
- gross profit
- gross margin %
- currency
- assumptions

AI must clearly identify estimates versus confirmed costs.

## 8.8 QUOTATION ENGINE

AI may prepare quotations using approved templates.

A quotation should contain:

- quotation number
- date
- customer
- product
- quantity
- unit price
- total price
- currency
- Incoterms where applicable
- estimated delivery
- validity period
- payment terms
- exclusions
- assumptions
- company details

The quotation must pass required approval rules before being
sent where applicable.

## 8.9 ORDER MANAGEMENT

Every confirmed order must receive a unique order ID.

Order states:

NEW
→ CONFIRMED
→ SUPPLIER CONFIRMED
→ PROCUREMENT
→ READY FOR DISPATCH
→ IN TRANSIT
→ ARRIVED
→ DELIVERED
→ INVOICED
→ PAID
→ CLOSED

Exceptions must be represented separately rather than hidden inside
normal statuses.

## 8.10 LOGISTICS CONTROL

The logistics system should track:

- shipment ID
- order ID
- supplier
- customer
- origin
- destination
- carrier
- route
- container/vehicle details where applicable
- booking
- departure
- ETA
- actual arrival
- customs status
- documentation status
- delivery status
- logistics cost
- exception status

AI should monitor shipment progress and identify delays.

## 8.11 EXCEPTION ENGINE

AI should automatically flag:

- shipment delays
- missing documents
- unexpected cost increases
- supplier delays
- customer delays
- payment delays
- compliance issues
- unusual pricing
- margin deterioration
- data inconsistencies

Each exception receives:

- severity
- reason
- affected transaction
- recommended action
- responsible party
- status
- timestamp

Severity levels:

LOW
MEDIUM
HIGH
CRITICAL

Critical exceptions must be escalated.

## 8.12 DOCUMENT CONTROL

The document system must support approved business documents including:

- quotations
- purchase orders
- invoices
- packing lists
- commercial invoices
- shipping documents
- certificates
- supplier documents
- customer documents
- contracts
- compliance records

Each document should have:

- document ID
- document type
- related transaction
- version
- creation date
- uploader/creator
- approval status
- expiry date where applicable
- verification status

AI may extract information from documents but must not assume
unverified information is correct.

## 8.13 FINANCE INTEGRATION

Every transaction must connect commercial activity to financial data.

The system should calculate:

- revenue
- cost of goods
- logistics cost
- gross profit
- gross margin
- outstanding receivables
- supplier payables
- transaction profitability

AI should identify transactions where expected margins are
materially different from actual margins.

## 8.14 CUSTOMER PAYMENT WORKFLOW

Invoice lifecycle:

DRAFT
→ APPROVAL
→ ISSUED
→ DUE
→ OVERDUE
→ PARTIALLY PAID
→ PAID
→ CLOSED

AI can:

- monitor due dates
- send approved reminders
- flag overdue invoices
- produce receivables reports

AI cannot independently alter banking information or transfer
company funds.

## 8.15 SUPPLIER PAYMENT WORKFLOW

Supplier payment lifecycle:

PAYMENT REQUEST
→ VALIDATION
→ APPROVAL
→ PAYMENT
→ RECONCILIATION
→ CLOSED

Controls should include:

- supplier identity verification
- invoice matching
- purchase/order matching
- duplicate detection
- approval threshold
- audit trail

Bank/payment execution must remain subject to appropriate
financial controls.

## 8.16 PROFITABILITY DASHBOARD

CEO Dashboard must show:

- revenue
- gross profit
- gross margin
- profit by order
- profit by customer
- profit by supplier
- profit by country
- profit by corridor
- profit by product
- logistics cost
- cost variance

AI should highlight the most profitable and least profitable
commercial activity.

## 8.17 TRADE CORRIDOR INTELLIGENCE

For each corridor, TriLink should track:

- origin
- transit countries
- destination
- transport mode
- typical lead time
- logistics cost
- customs requirements
- documentation
- supplier availability
- customer demand
- operational risks
- historical performance

AI should compare corridors and recommend alternatives when
operationally appropriate.

## 8.18 CORRIDOR EXPANSION

New corridors should only be activated after evaluation of:

- market demand
- supplier availability
- customer demand
- transport feasibility
- regulatory requirements
- compliance requirements
- expected margin
- operational capability
- financial risk

The Founder / Chairman / CEO retains final approval for
strategic corridor expansion.

## 8.19 BUSINESS INTELLIGENCE

AI should continuously analyse:

- sales trends
- customer demand
- product demand
- supplier performance
- shipping performance
- margins
- cash flow
- operational bottlenecks

The output should be presented through the Platformsify CEO Dashboard.

## 8.20 OPERATING PRINCIPLE

TriLink should not operate as disconnected departments.

The target architecture is:

LEAD
 ↓
OPPORTUNITY
 ↓
CUSTOMER + SUPPLIER
 ↓
PRODUCT
 ↓
PRICE
 ↓
ORDER
 ↓
LOGISTICS
 ↓
DELIVERY
 ↓
INVOICE
 ↓
PAYMENT
 ↓
PROFIT
 ↓
INTELLIGENCE

Every stage feeds the next stage.

Every important event feeds the CEO Dashboard.

Every AI action is auditable.


# ============================================================
# 9. TRILINK ENERGY BUSINESS
# ============================================================

## 9.1 ENERGY DIVISION

TriLink Energy is a strategic business division of TriLink Global.

Its purpose is to identify, develop, coordinate and manage
commercial energy opportunities using the same AI-first operating
model as the wider TriLink business.

TriLink Energy must operate through the Platformsify CEO Dashboard.

No separate CEO dashboard is permitted.

## 9.2 ENERGY BUSINESS MODEL

TriLink Energy may operate across approved activities including:

- energy equipment trading
- renewable-energy equipment
- solar solutions
- battery/storage solutions
- power equipment
- industrial energy solutions
- energy infrastructure opportunities
- energy project sourcing
- supplier/customer matching
- project coordination
- commercial energy partnerships

Specific activities must be activated only after appropriate
commercial, technical, legal and regulatory review.

## 9.3 ENERGY OPPORTUNITY PIPELINE

Every energy opportunity must have a structured record.

Required fields:

- project ID
- project name
- customer
- customer country
- project country
- project location
- project type
- estimated capacity where applicable
- technology
- equipment requirements
- supplier
- estimated project value
- estimated costs
- expected gross margin
- project timeline
- funding status
- technical status
- documentation status
- compliance status
- risk level
- current stage
- responsible owner
- CEO approval status

## 9.4 ENERGY PROJECT STAGES

STANDARD PROJECT LIFECYCLE:

LEAD
→ QUALIFIED
→ TECHNICAL REVIEW
→ COMMERCIAL REVIEW
→ SUPPLIER REVIEW
→ CUSTOMER REVIEW
→ PROPOSAL
→ NEGOTIATION
→ APPROVAL
→ CONTRACT
→ PROCUREMENT
→ LOGISTICS
→ IMPLEMENTATION
→ COMMISSIONING
→ HANDOVER
→ WARRANTY / SUPPORT
→ CLOSED

AI must monitor the project continuously.

## 9.5 ENERGY AI AGENT

The Energy Agent should:

- identify opportunities
- organise project information
- compare suppliers
- analyse equipment options
- prepare project summaries
- calculate preliminary commercial models
- identify missing documentation
- monitor project milestones
- flag delays
- prepare management reports
- assist with customer communications
- prepare draft proposals

The Energy Agent must not independently make engineering,
legal, financial or regulatory decisions outside its permissions.

## 9.6 TECHNICAL VALIDATION

AI-generated technical recommendations are preliminary.

Where a project requires professional engineering, certification,
technical sign-off or regulated activity, qualified professionals
must review and approve the relevant work.

AI must clearly distinguish:

CONFIRMED
ESTIMATED
ASSUMED
REQUIRES VALIDATION

## 9.7 ENERGY SUPPLIER NETWORK

Supplier records should contain:

- company name
- country
- products
- technology
- certifications
- technical documentation
- warranty
- capacity
- lead times
- pricing
- payment terms
- previous performance
- compliance status
- risk rating

AI should create supplier comparison reports.

## 9.8 ENERGY CUSTOMER PIPELINE

Potential customers may include:

- businesses
- industrial customers
- commercial property operators
- infrastructure operators
- distributors
- project developers
- approved institutional customers
- other qualified commercial buyers

The CRM should track:

- project requirements
- budget
- timeline
- technical requirements
- decision makers
- procurement stage
- proposal status
- contract status

## 9.9 ENERGY PROJECT ECONOMICS

Every serious project must have a financial model.

The model should consider:

- equipment cost
- transport
- insurance where applicable
- duties/taxes where applicable
- installation
- engineering
- labour
- project management
- financing costs
- contingency
- warranty/support
- other approved costs

Then calculate:

TOTAL PROJECT COST

EXPECTED CONTRACT VALUE

EXPECTED GROSS PROFIT

EXPECTED GROSS MARGIN

The dashboard should identify which values are estimates.

## 9.10 ENERGY RISK MANAGEMENT

AI must identify:

- supplier risk
- technical risk
- customer credit risk
- project delay risk
- regulatory risk
- documentation risk
- currency risk
- logistics risk
- cost-overrun risk
- contract risk

Each risk must have:

- severity
- probability where available
- impact
- mitigation
- owner
- status

## 9.11 ENERGY DOCUMENT MANAGEMENT

Energy projects may require:

- technical specifications
- quotations
- proposals
- equipment datasheets
- certifications
- warranties
- contracts
- purchase orders
- invoices
- shipping documents
- project plans
- installation records
- commissioning records
- acceptance documents

All documents must be linked to the relevant project.

## 9.12 ENERGY MILESTONE MONITORING

Each project should contain milestones.

Example:

MILESTONE 1
Opportunity qualification

MILESTONE 2
Technical requirements

MILESTONE 3
Supplier confirmation

MILESTONE 4
Commercial proposal

MILESTONE 5
Customer approval

MILESTONE 6
Contract

MILESTONE 7
Procurement

MILESTONE 8
Shipment

MILESTONE 9
Installation

MILESTONE 10
Commissioning

MILESTONE 11
Handover

AI should automatically flag overdue milestones.

## 9.13 ENERGY CEO VIEW

The CEO Dashboard must provide:

- total energy pipeline
- pipeline by country
- pipeline by project type
- pipeline by customer
- pipeline by supplier
- expected revenue
- expected gross profit
- expected margin
- projects at risk
- delayed projects
- projects requiring approval
- upcoming milestones

## 9.14 ENERGY PROCUREMENT

AI may prepare procurement comparisons.

The comparison should include:

- supplier
- equipment
- price
- specification
- lead time
- warranty
- certification
- payment terms
- logistics
- total estimated cost
- risk

The system should recommend the strongest commercial option,
but material procurement decisions must follow the approved
authority matrix.

## 9.15 ENERGY CONTRACT CONTROL

AI may prepare contract drafts using approved templates.

Contracts must be reviewed according to:

- value
- risk
- jurisdiction
- project type
- regulatory requirements
- company policy

Material contracts require appropriate human approval.

## 9.16 ENERGY REVENUE MODEL

Potential revenue models may include:

### TRADE MARGIN

TriLink purchases equipment and resells it at an approved margin.

### PROJECT MARGIN

TriLink coordinates a project and earns an agreed project margin.

### COMMISSION

TriLink earns a commission for introducing or coordinating
approved suppliers and customers.

### SERVICE FEES

TriLink may charge approved project-management or coordination
fees.

### LONG-TERM SUPPORT

Where commercially and legally appropriate, TriLink may provide
approved support or coordination services.

The actual model for each project must be clearly documented.

## 9.17 ENERGY QUALITY CONTROL

Before commercial completion, the system should confirm:

- required documents present
- agreed specifications met
- delivery completed
- installation/commissioning status confirmed where applicable
- customer acceptance obtained
- warranty information recorded
- invoice issued
- payment status recorded

## 9.18 ENERGY CUSTOMER EXPERIENCE

Customers should be able to receive controlled updates on:

- project status
- shipment
- milestones
- documents
- approvals
- estimated completion
- outstanding requirements

Customer-facing information must only expose authorised data.

## 9.19 ENERGY EXPANSION

TriLink may expand into additional energy markets after reviewing:

- demand
- supplier quality
- regulatory requirements
- project economics
- logistics
- financing
- operational capability
- risk

Expansion requires executive approval.

## 9.20 ENERGY OPERATING PRINCIPLE

TriLink Energy must use the same unified architecture:

CUSTOMER
 ↓
PROJECT
 ↓
TECHNICAL REQUIREMENTS
 ↓
SUPPLIER
 ↓
COMMERCIAL MODEL
 ↓
APPROVAL
 ↓
CONTRACT
 ↓
PROCUREMENT
 ↓
LOGISTICS
 ↓
IMPLEMENTATION
 ↓
COMMISSIONING
 ↓
HANDOVER
 ↓
REVENUE
 ↓
PROFIT
 ↓
AI INTELLIGENCE

All material activity must be visible through the Platformsify
CEO Dashboard.


# ============================================================
# 10. FINANCE, GOVERNANCE, RISK & CONTROL SYSTEM
# ============================================================

## 10.1 FINANCIAL CONTROL PRINCIPLE

TriLink Global must maintain strict separation between:

- AI analysis
- AI recommendations
- human approval
- financial execution
- financial reconciliation

AI can prepare and analyse financial activity.

AI must not have unrestricted authority to move company money.

## 10.2 CEO FINANCIAL DASHBOARD

Platformsify CEO Dashboard must provide a consolidated financial
view containing:

- revenue
- gross profit
- gross margin
- operating costs
- accounts receivable
- accounts payable
- cash position
- cash-flow forecast
- outstanding invoices
- overdue invoices
- supplier liabilities
- committed expenditure
- pipeline value
- forecast revenue
- forecast profit
- currency exposure
- financial exceptions

## 10.3 TRANSACTION CONTROL

Every material transaction should have:

- transaction ID
- customer/supplier
- related order/project
- amount
- currency
- purpose
- supporting documentation
- approval status
- payment status
- reconciliation status
- audit history

## 10.4 AUTHORITY MATRIX

### AI

AI may:

- analyse
- calculate
- compare
- prepare
- classify
- report
- recommend
- perform approved low-risk actions

### OPERATIONS

Authorised operational staff may approve actions within
their delegated limits.

### SENIOR MANAGEMENT

Senior management may approve material commercial activity
within formally delegated limits.

### FOUNDER / CHAIRMAN / CEO

The Founder / Chairman / CEO retains final authority for:

- strategic commitments
- major contracts
- major payments
- bank changes
- ownership matters
- major partnerships
- high-risk transactions
- major investments
- new-country expansion
- new business divisions
- major AI permissions
- material changes to company structure

## 10.5 FOUR-EYES CONTROL

For appropriate high-risk transactions, the system should require
two-person approval.

Examples:

- high-value payments
- bank detail changes
- sensitive supplier changes
- major contracts
- exceptional discounts
- unusual refunds
- high-risk customer onboarding

The exact thresholds must be configurable.

## 10.6 PAYMENT SECURITY

Payment workflows should include:

1. Request
2. Document validation
3. Supplier/customer validation
4. Duplicate check
5. Order/invoice matching
6. Risk check
7. Approval
8. Payment execution
9. Reconciliation
10. Audit completion

AI may assist every stage except unrestricted financial execution.

## 10.7 BANK DETAIL CHANGES

Changes to supplier or customer banking information are
high-risk events.

The system should:

- flag the change
- record the previous details
- record the new details
- require verification
- require appropriate approval
- record the approver
- maintain an audit trail

AI must not silently update payment details.

## 10.8 FRAUD DETECTION

AI should identify unusual patterns such as:

- duplicate invoices
- unusual payment amounts
- sudden supplier bank changes
- unusual discounts
- unexpected margin changes
- repeated refunds
- unusual transaction timing
- suspicious account activity
- mismatched documentation

Alerts must be visible on the CEO Dashboard.

## 10.9 CASH-FLOW INTELLIGENCE

AI should prepare:

- 7-day forecast
- 30-day forecast
- 60-day forecast
- 90-day forecast

Forecasts should identify:

- expected receipts
- expected payments
- committed costs
- expected project expenditure
- projected cash balance
- potential shortfalls
- major financial risks

Forecasts must clearly identify assumptions.

## 10.10 PROFITABILITY CONTROL

Every completed trade/project should contribute to profitability
analysis.

Required calculations:

Revenue
- direct costs
- logistics costs
- approved transaction costs
=
Gross Profit

Gross Profit / Revenue
=
Gross Margin

AI should compare:

- quoted margin
- expected margin
- actual margin

Material deviations must be flagged.

## 10.11 BUDGET CONTROL

The system should support:

- annual budget
- monthly budget
- department/division budget
- project budget
- actuals
- variance
- forecast

CEO Dashboard should show:

BUDGET
VS
ACTUAL
VS
FORECAST

## 10.12 RISK REGISTER

TriLink must maintain a central risk register.

Risk categories:

- financial
- operational
- supplier
- customer
- logistics
- legal
- regulatory
- compliance
- cybersecurity
- technology
- reputational
- strategic
- country
- currency
- project

Each risk must include:

- risk ID
- description
- category
- probability
- impact
- severity
- mitigation
- owner
- status
- review date

## 10.13 RISK SCORING

A simple initial framework:

LOW
MEDIUM
HIGH
CRITICAL

The system may calculate:

RISK SCORE =
PROBABILITY × IMPACT

The scoring model must remain configurable.

## 10.14 CRITICAL RISK

Critical risks must trigger:

- immediate dashboard alert
- AI escalation
- workflow suspension where appropriate
- human review
- recorded resolution

AI must not conceal critical risks.

## 10.15 COMPLIANCE CONTROL

TriLink must maintain appropriate compliance workflows for
each jurisdiction and transaction.

The system should track:

- required licences
- certifications
- permits
- trade documents
- customer/supplier verification
- applicable restrictions
- expiry dates
- regulatory obligations

AI may identify missing or expired information.

Qualified humans remain responsible for regulated decisions.

## 10.16 SANCTIONS / RESTRICTED-PARTY SCREENING

Where required, the business should use appropriate current
screening sources and procedures.

AI may:

- collect information
- run approved screening workflows
- flag possible matches
- organise results

AI must not treat a possible match as automatically confirmed.

Potential matches require appropriate review.

## 10.17 DATA GOVERNANCE

TriLink must maintain:

- data ownership
- data classification
- access controls
- retention rules
- deletion procedures
- audit logs
- backup procedures
- recovery procedures

Sensitive business data must only be accessible to authorised
users and services.

## 10.18 CYBERSECURITY

The production system must use:

- secure authentication
- strong access control
- least privilege
- protected secrets
- secure API authentication
- HTTPS
- dependency management
- logging
- monitoring
- backup
- incident response
- environment separation

Production secrets must never be committed to Git.

## 10.19 ENVIRONMENT SEPARATION

At minimum:

DEVELOPMENT
STAGING
PRODUCTION

Changes should be tested before production deployment.

Production database and credentials must not be casually reused
in development.

## 10.20 BACKUP AND RECOVERY

The system must maintain:

- database backups
- configuration backups
- document backups
- deployment recovery procedures
- disaster recovery documentation

Recovery procedures must be tested periodically.

## 10.21 INCIDENT MANAGEMENT

Security or operational incidents must follow:

DETECT
→ CLASSIFY
→ CONTAIN
→ INVESTIGATE
→ RESOLVE
→ RECOVER
→ REVIEW
→ PREVENT RECURRENCE

AI should assist with detection and reporting.

Human leadership controls major incident decisions.

## 10.22 AUDIT TRAIL

Material system events must be auditable.

Audit records should include:

- timestamp
- actor
- AI agent if applicable
- action
- previous value
- new value
- reason
- approval
- result
- related transaction

Audit records should not be casually editable or deleted.

## 10.23 GOVERNANCE DASHBOARD

CEO Dashboard must show:

- pending approvals
- high-risk transactions
- compliance exceptions
- financial exceptions
- AI exceptions
- security alerts
- overdue actions
- major contract status
- critical projects
- critical suppliers
- critical customers

## 10.24 CEO DAILY CONTROL

The CEO should receive one consolidated daily briefing.

It should answer:

1. What happened?
2. What changed?
3. What made money?
4. What lost money?
5. What is delayed?
6. What is at risk?
7. What requires approval?
8. What is AI doing?
9. What should the CEO do today?

## 10.25 GOVERNANCE PRINCIPLE

The operating model is:

AI SPEED
+
HUMAN ACCOUNTABILITY
+
FINANCIAL CONTROL
+
COMPLIANCE
+
AUDITABILITY

This prevents the business from becoming dependent on
uncontrolled automation.
