# ============================================================
# PART 13 — COMMERCIAL ENGINE, TRADE & REVENUE MODEL
# ============================================================

# ============================================================
# 13.1 COMMERCIAL OBJECTIVE
# ============================================================
#
# TriLink Global must be designed as a commercially scalable,
# asset-light, AI-first international trade and logistics group.
#
# The objective is to generate sustainable revenue and margin
# through:
#
# - international trade
# - sourcing
# - procurement
# - logistics coordination
# - distribution
# - energy opportunities
# - business services
# - technology-enabled operations
#
# AI improves speed, research, coordination and decision support.
#
# AI does not create artificial revenue or guarantee transactions.
#
# ============================================================
# 13.2 INITIAL TRADE CORRIDOR
# ============================================================
#
# INITIAL STRATEGIC CORRIDOR:
#
# NEPAL
#   ↓
# INDIA
#   ↓
# BANGLADESH
#
# The corridor should be treated as the initial commercial
# focus rather than a permanent geographic limitation.
#
# Expansion decisions must be based on:
#
# - customer demand
# - supplier availability
# - route economics
# - regulatory requirements
# - political/country risk
# - logistics reliability
# - achievable margins
# - working-capital requirements
#
# ============================================================
# 13.3 TRI-LINK TRADE
# ============================================================
#
# TriLink Trade acts as the commercial sourcing and trade engine.
#
# Core activities:
#
# - buyer identification
# - supplier identification
# - product sourcing
# - quotation management
# - procurement coordination
# - trade documentation
# - transaction coordination
# - customer service
# - margin management
#
# TriLink should avoid unnecessary inventory exposure during the
# early stage.
#
# ============================================================
# 13.4 ASSET-LIGHT STRATEGY
# ============================================================
#
# Initial strategy:
#
# CUSTOMER DEMAND
#       ↓
# SUPPLIER MATCH
#       ↓
# COMMERCIAL QUOTE
#       ↓
# APPROVED ORDER
#       ↓
# PROCUREMENT
#       ↓
# LOGISTICS
#       ↓
# DELIVERY
#       ↓
# PAYMENT
#
# The company should prioritise transactions where demand is
# established before taking significant inventory risk.
#
# ============================================================
# 13.5 REVENUE STREAMS
# ============================================================
#
# REVENUE STREAM 1 — TRADE MARGIN
#
# Purchase products at an approved commercial cost and sell to
# qualified customers at a profitable price.
#
# REVENUE STREAM 2 — SOURCING FEES
#
# Charge customers for sourcing and procurement services where
# commercially appropriate.
#
# REVENUE STREAM 3 — LOGISTICS COORDINATION
#
# Charge for logistics coordination, documentation,
# consolidation or related services where legally permitted.
#
# REVENUE STREAM 4 — DISTRIBUTION
#
# Develop distribution relationships in selected markets.
#
# REVENUE STREAM 5 — ENERGY SERVICES / PROJECT MARGINS
#
# Generate revenue from qualifying energy products, services,
# project development or commercial partnerships.
#
# REVENUE STREAM 6 — BUSINESS SERVICES
#
# Where appropriate, provide trade-related business services
# such as market intelligence, sourcing support and coordination.
#
# ============================================================
# 13.6 COMMERCIAL UNIT ECONOMICS
# ============================================================
#
# Every transaction should calculate:
#
# SALES PRICE
# - PRODUCT COST
# - PROCUREMENT COST
# - LOGISTICS COST
# - INSURANCE WHERE APPLICABLE
# - DUTIES/TAXES WHERE APPLICABLE
# - PAYMENT/FINANCIAL COST
# - OTHER DIRECT COSTS
# = GROSS PROFIT
#
# The system should additionally calculate:
#
# GROSS MARGIN %
# CONTRIBUTION MARGIN
# WORKING CAPITAL REQUIREMENT
# PAYMENT RISK
# TRANSACTION RISK
#
# ============================================================
# 13.7 AI PRICING ASSISTANT
# ============================================================
#
# AI should recommend pricing using:
#
# - supplier price
# - historic transaction prices
# - market information
# - logistics costs
# - target margin
# - customer segment
# - order size
# - payment terms
# - risk
#
# AI recommendations must show assumptions.
#
# Pricing below approved minimum margins requires human approval.
#
# ============================================================
# 13.8 CUSTOMER SEGMENTS
# ============================================================
#
# PRIORITY CUSTOMER GROUPS:
#
# 1. Importers
# 2. Distributors
# 3. Wholesalers
# 4. Retail groups
# 5. Manufacturers
# 6. Construction/industrial buyers
# 7. Energy-sector customers
# 8. Institutional/commercial buyers where appropriate
#
# AI should score customer fit and commercial potential.
#
# ============================================================
# 13.9 SUPPLIER SEGMENTS
# ============================================================
#
# PRIORITY SUPPLIERS:
#
# - manufacturers
# - authorised distributors
# - wholesalers
# - exporters
# - energy equipment providers
# - logistics partners
# - specialist service providers
#
# Supplier selection must consider:
#
# - price
# - quality
# - capacity
# - reliability
# - certification
# - compliance
# - payment terms
# - delivery time
# - country risk
#
# ============================================================
# 13.10 TRANSACTION WORKFLOW
# ============================================================
#
# STEP 1 — MARKET IDENTIFICATION
#
# AI identifies demand or commercial opportunity.
#
# STEP 2 — CUSTOMER QUALIFICATION
#
# AI researches and scores the potential customer.
#
# STEP 3 — PRODUCT/SERVICE MATCH
#
# AI identifies suitable suppliers and solutions.
#
# STEP 4 — COST CALCULATION
#
# Calculate complete landed/transaction cost.
#
# STEP 5 — MARGIN ANALYSIS
#
# Verify minimum approved margin.
#
# STEP 6 — QUOTATION
#
# Generate customer quotation.
#
# STEP 7 — NEGOTIATION
#
# AI assists with approved negotiation parameters.
#
# STEP 8 — APPROVAL
#
# Material commitments require appropriate human approval.
#
# STEP 9 — ORDER
#
# Create approved order.
#
# STEP 10 — PROCUREMENT
#
# Coordinate supplier fulfilment.
#
# STEP 11 — LOGISTICS
#
# Coordinate movement and documentation.
#
# STEP 12 — DELIVERY
#
# Confirm delivery.
#
# STEP 13 — INVOICING
#
# Issue/track approved invoice.
#
# STEP 14 — PAYMENT
#
# Monitor receivable/payment status.
#
# STEP 15 — PROFIT MEASUREMENT
#
# Record actual transaction economics.
#
# ============================================================
# 13.11 CUSTOMER PAYMENT POLICY
# ============================================================
#
# AI should assess:
#
# - payment history
# - customer risk
# - order size
# - credit exposure
# - country risk
# - proposed payment terms
#
# High-risk credit exposure requires human approval.
#
# Where commercially practical, TriLink should favour payment
# structures that reduce working-capital risk.
#
# ============================================================
# 13.12 SUPPLIER PAYMENT POLICY
# ============================================================
#
# AI should compare:
#
# - supplier terms
# - deposit requirements
# - payment milestones
# - currency exposure
# - transaction risk
# - delivery risk
#
# AI must never independently change bank account details.
#
# Bank-detail changes require independent verification and
# authorised human approval.
#
# ============================================================
# 13.13 WORKING CAPITAL STRATEGY
# ============================================================
#
# Initial principle:
#
# PROTECT CASH.
#
# Avoid:
#
# - unnecessary inventory
# - speculative purchasing
# - excessive customer credit
# - uncontrolled deposits
# - unverified suppliers
# - transactions with unclear economics
#
# Prioritise:
#
# - confirmed demand
# - predictable margins
# - controlled payment terms
# - reliable logistics
# - low operational complexity
#
# ============================================================
# 13.14 TRADE DOCUMENTATION
# ============================================================
#
# AI may prepare/check document workflows including:
#
# - commercial invoices
# - purchase orders
# - quotations
# - packing information
# - shipping documentation
# - certificates
# - customs-related documentation
# - contracts
# - delivery records
#
# AI-generated documents must be reviewed where legally or
# commercially required.
#
# ============================================================
# 13.15 LOGISTICS PARTNERS
# ============================================================
#
# TriLink should maintain a qualified network of:
#
# - freight forwarders
# - customs specialists
# - transport providers
# - warehouses
# - consolidation providers
# - inspection providers
# - insurance providers
#
# Partner performance must be tracked.
#
# ============================================================
# 13.16 ROUTE INTELLIGENCE
# ============================================================
#
# AI should compare routes based on:
#
# - total cost
# - transit time
# - reliability
# - border requirements
# - documentation requirements
# - capacity
# - risk
#
# Route recommendations must be verified before execution.
#
# ============================================================
# 13.17 ENERGY COMMERCIAL ENGINE
# ============================================================
#
# TriLink Energy should initially operate through opportunities
# that can be commercially validated without excessive capital.
#
# Potential areas may include:
#
# - energy equipment sourcing
# - renewable-energy equipment
# - solar-related products
# - power infrastructure opportunities
# - industrial energy solutions
# - project procurement
# - technical/commercial partnerships
#
# Specific products and services must be validated for:
#
# - market demand
# - certification
# - technical requirements
# - regulatory requirements
# - supplier capability
# - project economics
#
# ============================================================
# 13.18 ENERGY PROJECT PIPELINE
# ============================================================
#
# OPPORTUNITY
# ↓
# QUALIFICATION
# ↓
# TECHNICAL REVIEW
# ↓
# COMMERCIAL REVIEW
# ↓
# SUPPLIER/PARTNER SELECTION
# ↓
# PROPOSAL
# ↓
# CONTRACT
# ↓
# PROCUREMENT
# ↓
# IMPLEMENTATION
# ↓
# COMMISSIONING/DELIVERY
# ↓
# CLOSEOUT
#
# Technical and safety-critical decisions require qualified
# human professionals.
#
# ============================================================
# 13.19 CROSS-SELLING ENGINE
# ============================================================
#
# AI should identify opportunities to sell additional services
# to existing customers.
#
# Examples:
#
# TRADE CUSTOMER
# → LOGISTICS
# → PROCUREMENT
# → DISTRIBUTION
# → ENERGY OPPORTUNITY
#
# LOGISTICS CUSTOMER
# → TRADE
# → SOURCING
# → PROCUREMENT
#
# ENERGY CUSTOMER
# → EQUIPMENT
# → PROCUREMENT
# → LOGISTICS
#
# ============================================================
# 13.20 CUSTOMER RETENTION
# ============================================================
#
# AI should monitor:
#
# - repeat order timing
# - customer inactivity
# - declining order volume
# - complaints
# - payment behaviour
# - service performance
#
# AI should recommend retention actions.
#
# ============================================================
# 13.21 COMMERCIAL RISK GATES
# ============================================================
#
# NO TRANSACTION SHOULD PROCEED AUTOMATICALLY IF:
#
# - customer identity is uncertain
# - supplier identity is uncertain
# - required documentation is missing
# - sanctions/compliance screening raises concerns
# - margin is below approved threshold
# - payment risk is excessive
# - legal status is unclear
# - route is materially unsafe/unreliable
# - transaction economics cannot be verified
#
# Such cases must be escalated.
#
# ============================================================
# 13.22 COMMERCIAL DASHBOARD
# ============================================================
#
# CEO Dashboard should show:
#
# - total sales
# - gross profit
# - gross margin
# - pipeline
# - confirmed orders
# - active shipments
# - receivables
# - supplier exposure
# - customer exposure
# - working capital
# - transaction risk
# - top opportunities
# - top customers
# - top suppliers
#
# ============================================================
# 13.23 COMMERCIAL GROWTH MODEL
# ============================================================
#
# PHASE 1
# Validate corridor and first transactions.
#
# PHASE 2
# Establish repeat customers and reliable suppliers.
#
# PHASE 3
# Increase transaction volume while protecting margins.
#
# PHASE 4
# Expand products and services.
#
# PHASE 5
# Expand into additional countries/routes.
#
# PHASE 6
# Build strategic distribution and energy partnerships.
#
# ============================================================
# 13.24 SCALE PRINCIPLE
# ============================================================
#
# DO NOT SCALE LOSSES.
#
# Every major expansion should demonstrate:
#
# - customer demand
# - positive unit economics
# - operational capability
# - manageable risk
# - sufficient working capital
# - compliance readiness
#
# ============================================================
# 13.25 COMMERCIAL NORTH STAR
# ============================================================
#
# FIND DEMAND
# ↓
# FIND SUPPLY
# ↓
# PRICE CORRECTLY
# ↓
# CONTROL RISK
# ↓
# EXECUTE RELIABLY
# ↓
# DELIVER VALUE
# ↓
# COLLECT CASH
# ↓
# MEASURE PROFIT
# ↓
# REPEAT
# ↓
# SCALE
#
# ============================================================
# END OF PART 13
# ============================================================


# ============================================================
# PART 14 — LEGAL, COMPLIANCE, KYC/KYB & RISK CONTROL
# ============================================================

# ============================================================
# 14.1 COMPLIANCE-FIRST PRINCIPLE
# ============================================================
#
# TriLink Global must be designed so that commercial growth
# cannot bypass legal, regulatory or compliance controls.
#
# AI assists with compliance.
# AI does not replace qualified legal, regulatory, customs,
# tax, engineering or other professional advice.
#
# ============================================================
# 14.2 THREE-LAYER CONTROL MODEL
# ============================================================
#
# LAYER 1 — AI SCREENING
#
# AI identifies potential issues.
#
# LAYER 2 — SYSTEM CONTROL
#
# The platform blocks or escalates transactions according to
# configured rules.
#
# LAYER 3 — HUMAN AUTHORITY
#
# Founder / Chairman / CEO or authorised professionals make
# decisions where human judgement or legal authority is required.
#
# ============================================================
# 14.3 KYC — KNOW YOUR CUSTOMER
# ============================================================
#
# Before material commercial activity, TriLink should collect
# appropriate customer information, subject to applicable law.
#
# Possible information includes:
#
# - legal company name
# - registration information
# - registered address
# - operating address
# - beneficial ownership information where required
# - authorised representatives
# - business activity
# - country
# - payment information
# - relevant licences/certifications
# - transaction purpose
#
# ============================================================
# 14.4 KYB — KNOW YOUR BUSINESS
# ============================================================
#
# Business counterparties should be verified using appropriate
# reliable sources.
#
# AI may assist with:
#
# - company research
# - registration checks
# - document comparison
# - identity matching
# - ownership research
# - anomaly detection
#
# AI results must be treated as screening support until verified.
#
# ============================================================
# 14.5 SUPPLIER DUE DILIGENCE
# ============================================================
#
# Before approving strategic suppliers, review where appropriate:
#
# - company existence
# - ownership
# - trading history
# - product capability
# - certifications
# - references
# - payment details
# - sanctions exposure
# - adverse information
# - regulatory requirements
#
# High-risk suppliers require escalation.
#
# ============================================================
# 14.6 CUSTOMER DUE DILIGENCE
# ============================================================
#
# Customer review should consider:
#
# - identity
# - ownership
# - business activity
# - intended transaction
# - country
# - payment behaviour
# - sanctions/compliance concerns
# - unusual transaction patterns
#
# ============================================================
# 14.7 SANCTIONS SCREENING
# ============================================================
#
# TriLink must maintain a process for screening relevant
# counterparties and transactions against applicable sanctions
# and restricted-party requirements.
#
# Screening should be performed using authoritative and
# appropriately maintained sources.
#
# A potential match must be escalated for review.
#
# AI must not automatically declare a person/company innocent
# or guilty solely from a name match.
#
# ============================================================
# 14.8 ANTI-FRAUD CONTROL
# ============================================================
#
# AI should identify:
#
# - unusual payment requests
# - sudden bank-detail changes
# - duplicate invoices
# - abnormal pricing
# - unusual customer behaviour
# - unusual supplier behaviour
# - suspicious account changes
# - inconsistent documentation
#
# Suspicious activity must be escalated.
#
# ============================================================
# 14.9 BANK-DETAIL CHANGE CONTROL
# ============================================================
#
# BANK-DETAIL CHANGES ARE HIGH RISK.
#
# AI may detect and flag changes.
#
# AI must NOT independently approve changes to:
#
# - supplier bank accounts
# - customer bank accounts
# - TriLink bank accounts
# - payment destinations
#
# Independent verification and authorised human approval are
# required.
#
# ============================================================
# 14.10 PAYMENT CONTROL
# ============================================================
#
# AI may prepare payment information and identify upcoming
# obligations.
#
# AI must not independently execute material company payments
# without an approved payment-control process.
#
# Payment controls should include:
#
# - beneficiary verification
# - invoice verification
# - duplicate detection
# - approval limits
# - transaction records
# - audit logs
#
# ============================================================
# 14.11 CONTRACT CONTROL
# ============================================================
#
# AI may:
#
# - prepare contract drafts from approved templates
# - summarise contracts
# - identify unusual clauses
# - compare versions
# - track obligations
# - track renewal dates
# - flag missing information
#
# AI-generated legal material must be reviewed by an appropriate
# human professional where required.
#
# ============================================================
# 14.12 CONTRACT APPROVAL
# ============================================================
#
# Material contracts must pass appropriate approval before
# becoming binding.
#
# The system should record:
#
# - contract owner
# - counterparty
# - value
# - currency
# - start date
# - end date
# - renewal date
# - obligations
# - approval status
# - signatures
# - related documents
#
# ============================================================
# 14.13 CUSTOMS CONTROL
# ============================================================
#
# International trade workflows must account for applicable
# customs requirements.
#
# AI may assist with:
#
# - document preparation
# - product classification research
# - shipment data checks
# - missing-document detection
# - customs workflow tracking
#
# Final classification and regulatory determinations should be
# verified by appropriately qualified persons where necessary.
#
# ============================================================
# 14.14 PRODUCT COMPLIANCE
# ============================================================
#
# Products must be reviewed for applicable:
#
# - safety requirements
# - import requirements
# - export requirements
# - labelling requirements
# - certifications
# - technical standards
# - food requirements
# - energy requirements
# - environmental requirements
#
# Product-specific rules vary by country and product category.
#
# ============================================================
# 14.15 ENERGY COMPLIANCE
# ============================================================
#
# Energy projects require additional controls for:
#
# - technical standards
# - electrical safety
# - equipment certification
# - engineering requirements
# - environmental requirements
# - construction requirements
# - permits
# - professional sign-off
#
# AI cannot replace qualified engineering or safety professionals.
#
# ============================================================
# 14.16 DATA PROTECTION
# ============================================================
#
# TriLink must implement appropriate data-protection controls.
#
# The platform should support:
#
# - access control
# - data minimisation
# - retention policies
# - deletion workflows
# - audit logs
# - secure transmission
# - secure storage
# - incident handling
#
# Applicable privacy laws must be assessed for each relevant
# market and business activity.
#
# ============================================================
# 14.17 AI DATA GOVERNANCE
# ============================================================
#
# Business data used by AI must be controlled.
#
# The system should distinguish:
#
# - public information
# - internal information
# - confidential information
# - highly sensitive information
#
# AI agents must only access data required for their authorised
# workflow.
#
# ============================================================
# 14.18 AI HALLUCINATION CONTROL
# ============================================================
#
# AI-generated information must not automatically become a
# business fact.
#
# Important outputs should be labelled:
#
# VERIFIED FACT
# UNVERIFIED INFORMATION
# AI ESTIMATE
# AI RECOMMENDATION
# HUMAN APPROVED
#
# ============================================================
# 14.19 SOURCE VERIFICATION
# ============================================================
#
# For material external information, AI should identify:
#
# - source
# - date
# - relevance
# - confidence
#
# High-impact decisions should use reliable primary or
# authoritative sources wherever possible.
#
# ============================================================
# 14.20 COUNTRY RISK
# ============================================================
#
# AI should monitor:
#
# - political risk
# - regulatory changes
# - trade restrictions
# - sanctions
# - currency risk
# - logistics disruption
# - natural-disaster risk
# - market instability
#
# High-risk developments should trigger CEO alerts.
#
# ============================================================
# 14.21 TRANSACTION RISK SCORE
# ============================================================
#
# Every material transaction should receive a risk assessment.
#
# FACTORS:
#
# - customer risk
# - supplier risk
# - country risk
# - payment risk
# - product risk
# - logistics risk
# - compliance risk
# - margin risk
#
# RATING:
#
# LOW
# MEDIUM
# HIGH
# CRITICAL
#
# ============================================================
# 14.22 RISK ESCALATION
# ============================================================
#
# LOW:
# Automated workflow permitted where authorised.
#
# MEDIUM:
# Management review.
#
# HIGH:
# CEO or designated senior approval.
#
# CRITICAL:
# Transaction blocked pending authorised review.
#
# ============================================================
# 14.23 COMPLIANCE AUDIT TRAIL
# ============================================================
#
# The CEO Dashboard should record:
#
# - screening performed
# - date/time
# - data/source used
# - result
# - reviewer
# - decision
# - escalation
# - supporting documents
#
# ============================================================
# 14.24 COMPLIANCE CALENDAR
# ============================================================
#
# The platform should track:
#
# - licences
# - certificates
# - insurance
# - contracts
# - renewals
# - filings
# - regulatory deadlines
# - supplier documents
# - customer documents
#
# AI should issue advance alerts.
#
# ============================================================
# 14.25 INSURANCE CONTROL
# ============================================================
#
# Relevant business insurance requirements should be assessed
# before undertaking material operations.
#
# The system should track:
#
# - policy
# - insurer
# - coverage
# - limits
# - expiry
# - renewal
# - claims
#
# ============================================================
# 14.26 PROFESSIONAL ADVISERS
# ============================================================
#
# TriLink should maintain access to appropriate professional
# advisers as required, potentially including:
#
# - accountant
# - tax adviser
# - solicitor
# - customs/trade specialist
# - insurance adviser
# - compliance specialist
# - engineering professionals
# - cybersecurity professionals
#
# AI coordinates information but does not replace these
# professionals where their expertise or formal authority is
# required.
#
# ============================================================
# 14.27 INCIDENT MANAGEMENT
# ============================================================
#
# The platform should support incidents involving:
#
# - fraud
# - cybersecurity
# - data exposure
# - shipment disruption
# - regulatory issues
# - supplier failure
# - customer disputes
# - payment problems
# - safety concerns
#
# Each incident should have:
#
# - severity
# - owner
# - status
# - timeline
# - actions
# - evidence
# - resolution
#
# ============================================================
# 14.28 COMPLIANCE PRINCIPLE
# ============================================================
#
# NO CUSTOMER
# WITHOUT APPROPRIATE DUE DILIGENCE
#
# NO SUPPLIER
# WITHOUT APPROPRIATE DUE DILIGENCE
#
# NO MATERIAL TRANSACTION
# WITHOUT APPROPRIATE RISK REVIEW
#
# NO MATERIAL PAYMENT
# WITHOUT APPROPRIATE AUTHORISATION
#
# NO HIGH-RISK ACTIVITY
# WITHOUT HUMAN ESCALATION
#
# ============================================================
# 14.29 CEO COMPLIANCE VIEW
# ============================================================
#
# The CEO Dashboard should show:
#
# - open compliance issues
# - pending KYC/KYB
# - high-risk counterparties
# - sanctions alerts
# - contract approvals
# - licence/certificate expiries
# - payment anomalies
# - fraud alerts
# - country-risk alerts
# - incidents
#
# ============================================================
# 14.30 COMPLIANCE NORTH STAR
# ============================================================
#
# VERIFY
# ↓
# SCREEN
# ↓
# ASSESS
# ↓
# APPROVE
# ↓
# EXECUTE
# ↓
# AUDIT
# ↓
# MONITOR
#
# ============================================================
# END OF PART 14
# ============================================================


# ============================================================
# PART 15 — AI OPERATIONS, CONTROL & EXECUTION
# ============================================================
#
# TriLink Global will operate as an AI-first business, with
# Platformsify CEO Dashboard acting as the SINGLE executive
# technology control centre.
#
# The objective is maximum automation without removing human
# accountability.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 15.1 AI OPERATING MODEL
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# AI handles approved operational workflows including:
#
# - lead capture
# - customer qualification
# - supplier discovery
# - supplier comparison
# - quotation preparation
# - RFQ management
# - document classification
# - shipment tracking
# - logistics status updates
# - customer communications
# - supplier communications
# - invoice preparation
# - payment-status monitoring
# - financial reporting
# - KPI reporting
# - risk flagging
# - compliance reminders
# - market intelligence
# - energy opportunity analysis
# - management reporting
# - task creation
# - workflow escalation
#
# AI must never bypass permissions, financial controls,
# compliance requirements or Founder/CEO approval thresholds.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 15.2 CEO DASHBOARD
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# Platformsify CEO Dashboard is the SINGLE management interface.
#
# It must provide the Founder / Chairman / CEO with:
#
# EXECUTIVE OVERVIEW
# - revenue
# - gross margin
# - net position
# - cash position
# - outstanding receivables
# - outstanding payables
# - active deals
# - active shipments
# - active customers
# - active suppliers
# - operational alerts
# - risk alerts
# - AI actions
# - pending approvals
#
# COMMERCIAL CONTROL
# - leads
# - opportunities
# - quotations
# - orders
# - contracts
# - customers
# - suppliers
# - pipeline value
# - conversion rate
#
# LOGISTICS CONTROL
# - shipments
# - routes
# - cargo status
# - milestones
# - delays
# - exceptions
# - estimated arrival
# - delivery confirmation
#
# FINANCIAL CONTROL
# - invoices
# - payments
# - receivables
# - payables
# - margins
# - transaction history
# - financial exceptions
#
# AI CONTROL
# - AI activity log
# - AI recommendations
# - AI approvals
# - AI exceptions
# - AI confidence
# - AI escalations
# - AI permission status
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 15.3 AI AUTHORITY LEVELS
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# LEVEL 0 — OBSERVE
# AI may read approved information and generate analysis.
#
# LEVEL 1 — RECOMMEND
# AI may recommend actions but cannot execute them.
#
# LEVEL 2 — EXECUTE LOW RISK
# AI may execute pre-approved operational actions.
#
# LEVEL 3 — EXECUTE WITH APPROVAL
# AI prepares the complete action and requests CEO approval.
#
# LEVEL 4 — HUMAN ONLY
# AI cannot execute the action.
#
# LEVEL 4 includes:
# - ownership changes
# - banking changes
# - major financial commitments
# - major contracts
# - legal decisions
# - regulatory submissions
# - high-risk transactions
# - strategic acquisitions
# - new-country expansion
# - senior executive appointments
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 15.4 AI DECISION PIPELINE
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# Every material AI action follows:
#
# INPUT
# ↓
# VALIDATION
# ↓
# BUSINESS RULE CHECK
# ↓
# RISK CHECK
# ↓
# AI DECISION
# ↓
# PERMISSION CHECK
# ↓
# EXECUTION OR CEO APPROVAL
# ↓
# AUDIT LOG
# ↓
# KPI UPDATE
#
# No material action may bypass this process.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 15.5 HUMAN ESCALATION
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# AI must immediately escalate when:
#
# - information conflicts
# - required documentation is missing
# - customer identity is uncertain
# - supplier legitimacy is uncertain
# - payment information changes unexpectedly
# - margins fall below approved thresholds
# - shipment risk increases materially
# - compliance risk appears
# - fraud indicators appear
# - contractual terms materially change
# - AI confidence falls below the configured threshold
#
# The CEO Dashboard must display the escalation clearly.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 15.6 AUDITABILITY
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# Every AI-controlled workflow must record:
#
# - timestamp
# - user/system identity
# - AI agent
# - input
# - decision
# - confidence
# - rule applied
# - approval status
# - action taken
# - result
# - exception, if any
#
# Audit records must be immutable or protected against unauthorised
# modification.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 15.7 AI AGENT STRUCTURE
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# TriLink should use specialised AI agents rather than one
# unrestricted AI agent.
#
# CEO / Executive Agent
# └── oversees executive reporting and escalation
#
# Commercial Agent
# └── leads, customers, quotations and sales pipeline
#
# Procurement Agent
# └── suppliers, RFQs and purchasing workflows
#
# Logistics Agent
# └── shipments, routes, milestones and exceptions
#
# Finance Agent
# └── invoices, receivables, payables and financial reporting
#
# Compliance Agent
# └── documentation, regulatory checks and alerts
#
# Energy Agent
# └── energy opportunities, projects and market intelligence
#
# Intelligence Agent
# └── market research, competitor intelligence and forecasting
#
# Support Agent
# └── customer communication and service workflows
#
# Agents must operate inside defined permissions.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 15.8 SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# Platformsify CEO Dashboard must not create duplicate business
# records in separate management systems.
#
# Each core entity should have one authoritative record:
#
# CUSTOMER
# SUPPLIER
# LEAD
# OPPORTUNITY
# QUOTE
# ORDER
# CONTRACT
# SHIPMENT
# INVOICE
# PAYMENT
# PROJECT
# AI ACTION
# APPROVAL
# RISK EVENT
#
# All dashboards, reports and AI agents must reference the
# authoritative records.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 15.9 CEO COMMAND CENTRE
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# The CEO Dashboard should make the business understandable
# within seconds.
#
# PRIORITY 1:
# What needs my attention?
#
# PRIORITY 2:
# What is making money?
#
# PRIORITY 3:
# What is at risk?
#
# PRIORITY 4:
# What is AI doing?
#
# PRIORITY 5:
# What decisions require my approval?
#
# PRIORITY 6:
# What opportunities should we pursue?
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 15.10 DAILY AI EXECUTIVE BRIEF
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# AI should prepare an executive brief containing:
#
# 1. Revenue
# 2. Gross margin
# 3. Cash position
# 4. New customers
# 5. New suppliers
# 6. New opportunities
# 7. Active shipments
# 8. Delayed shipments
# 9. Financial exceptions
# 10. Compliance exceptions
# 11. Major risks
# 12. AI actions completed
# 13. AI actions awaiting approval
# 14. Recommended CEO decisions
#
# The Founder / Chairman / CEO remains the final decision-maker.
#
# ============================================================
# END PART 15
# ============================================================


# ============================================================
# PART 16 — COMMERCIAL ENGINE & REVENUE MODEL
# ============================================================
#
# TriLink Global must be designed as a commercially scalable,
# AI-first trade, logistics and energy platform.
#
# The business should prioritise profitable transactions,
# repeat customers, reliable suppliers and controlled growth.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 16.1 CORE REVENUE STREAMS
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# TriLink may generate revenue through:
#
# 1. Trade margins
# 2. Procurement margins
# 3. Logistics coordination fees
# 4. Freight / shipment management fees
# 5. Brokerage / agency commissions
# 6. Sourcing fees
# 7. Supplier-side service fees where commercially appropriate
# 8. Customer-side service fees where commercially appropriate
# 9. Energy project development fees
# 10. Energy equipment / solution margins
# 11. Consulting and trade intelligence services
# 12. Technology-enabled service fees
#
# Revenue streams must be separately tracked in the CEO Dashboard.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 16.2 COMMERCIAL PRIORITY
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# The commercial engine follows:
#
# FIND DEMAND
# ↓
# QUALIFY CUSTOMER
# ↓
# IDENTIFY SUPPLY
# ↓
# VERIFY SUPPLIER
# ↓
# CALCULATE LANDED COST
# ↓
# CALCULATE RISK
# ↓
# CALCULATE MARGIN
# ↓
# GENERATE QUOTE
# ↓
# CEO / AUTHORIZED APPROVAL
# ↓
# CUSTOMER CONFIRMATION
# ↓
# ORDER
# ↓
# LOGISTICS
# ↓
# DELIVERY
# ↓
# INVOICE
# ↓
# PAYMENT
# ↓
# REPEAT ORDER
#
# AI should automate each approved stage.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 16.3 CUSTOMER ACQUISITION
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# AI should identify and qualify potential:
#
# - importers
# - exporters
# - distributors
# - wholesalers
# - retailers
# - manufacturers
# - institutional buyers
# - energy customers
# - logistics partners
# - strategic partners
#
# AI may score opportunities using:
#
# - country
# - industry
# - purchasing capacity
# - product demand
# - transaction size
# - expected margin
# - payment reliability
# - regulatory complexity
# - logistics complexity
# - strategic value
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 16.4 CUSTOMER SCORING
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# Every commercial lead should receive a score.
#
# EXAMPLE:
#
# A — HIGH PRIORITY
# Strong demand + verified business + attractive margin.
#
# B — PRIORITY
# Good potential but requires additional verification.
#
# C — WATCH
# Possible opportunity but insufficient information.
#
# D — REJECT / HOLD
# High risk, poor economics or insufficient legitimacy.
#
# AI must explain why a lead received its score.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 16.5 SUPPLIER STRATEGY
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# Suppliers should be evaluated using:
#
# - legal identity
# - operating history
# - product capability
# - certifications
# - production capacity
# - pricing
# - minimum order quantity
# - lead time
# - payment terms
# - export capability
# - logistics capability
# - reputation
# - sanctions / compliance screening where applicable
#
# AI may shortlist suppliers.
#
# Final approval for high-value or high-risk suppliers remains
# subject to authorised human approval.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 16.6 LANDED COST ENGINE
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# Every trade quotation must calculate total expected landed cost.
#
# LANDed COST SHOULD CONSIDER:
#
# product cost
# + packaging
# + origin handling
# + documentation
# + freight
# + insurance
# + customs-related costs
# + duties / taxes where applicable
# + destination handling
# + warehousing where applicable
# + local delivery
# + financing cost where applicable
# + contingency
#
# The system must never calculate selling price from product cost
# alone when material additional costs are expected.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 16.7 PRICING ENGINE
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# AI should generate recommended pricing based on:
#
# - landed cost
# - target gross margin
# - market price
# - competitor pricing
# - order volume
# - customer value
# - payment terms
# - logistics risk
# - currency risk
# - supplier reliability
# - urgency
#
# AI must show:
#
# COST
# RECOMMENDED PRICE
# GROSS PROFIT
# GROSS MARGIN %
# KEY ASSUMPTIONS
# RISKS
#
# Exceptional discounts require approval according to configured
# authority limits.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 16.8 QUOTATION ENGINE
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# AI may prepare professional quotations containing:
#
# - customer details
# - supplier / origin
# - product description
# - quantity
# - unit price
# - total price
# - currency
# - Incoterms where applicable
# - estimated lead time
# - delivery assumptions
# - validity period
# - payment terms
# - exclusions
# - applicable conditions
#
# AI must not invent commercial terms.
#
# Missing information must be marked as:
#
# "REQUIRES CONFIRMATION"
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 16.9 ORDER MANAGEMENT
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# Once a customer accepts an approved quotation:
#
# QUOTE
# ↓
# CUSTOMER ACCEPTANCE
# ↓
# ORDER CREATION
# ↓
# SUPPLIER CONFIRMATION
# ↓
# PAYMENT / CREDIT CHECK
# ↓
# PRODUCTION / PROCUREMENT
# ↓
# SHIPMENT
# ↓
# DELIVERY
# ↓
# INVOICE
# ↓
# PAYMENT
#
# Every transition must be recorded.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 16.10 REPEAT BUSINESS ENGINE
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# TriLink should prioritise recurring revenue.
#
# AI should identify:
#
# - repeat purchasing patterns
# - reorder dates
# - seasonal demand
# - declining customer activity
# - high-value customers
# - cross-selling opportunities
# - supplier alternatives
#
# AI may recommend:
#
# - reorder reminders
# - revised quotations
# - volume pricing
# - alternative products
# - alternative suppliers
# - logistics optimisation
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 16.11 CUSTOMER RETENTION
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# Customer experience must be measurable.
#
# Track:
#
# - delivery performance
# - quotation response time
# - issue resolution time
# - repeat-order rate
# - customer profitability
# - complaints
# - payment behaviour
# - customer lifetime value
#
# The CEO Dashboard should highlight customers requiring attention.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 16.12 COMMERCIAL KPIs
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# CORE KPIs:
#
# - total revenue
# - gross profit
# - gross margin %
# - pipeline value
# - qualified opportunities
# - quote-to-order conversion
# - average order value
# - customer acquisition cost
# - customer lifetime value
# - repeat-order rate
# - supplier response time
# - quotation response time
# - delivery success rate
# - overdue receivables
#
# AI should continuously monitor these metrics and identify trends.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 16.13 COMMERCIAL ALERTS
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# The CEO Dashboard must alert the Founder / Chairman / CEO when:
#
# - a high-value opportunity appears
# - a major customer is at risk
# - margin falls below threshold
# - a supplier price changes materially
# - logistics costs increase materially
# - a customer payment becomes overdue
# - an unusually large transaction appears
# - fraud indicators appear
# - a strategic opportunity is identified
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 16.14 COMMERCIAL PRINCIPLE
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# TriLink should NOT pursue revenue at any cost.
#
# The priority is:
#
# PROFITABLE
# + VERIFIED
# + COMPLIANT
# + DELIVERABLE
# + REPEATABLE
# + SCALABLE
#
# Growth must strengthen the company rather than create uncontrolled
# financial or operational exposure.
#
# ============================================================
# END PART 16
# ============================================================


# ============================================================
# PART 17 — TRADE, LOGISTICS & SUPPLY-CHAIN ENGINE
# ============================================================
#
# TriLink Global will operate a technology-enabled trade and
# logistics coordination engine designed to connect verified
# buyers, suppliers and logistics partners.
#
# The initial strategic corridor is:
#
# NEPAL → INDIA → BANGLADESH
#
# The architecture must remain flexible enough to support
# additional corridors and countries after validation.
#
# ============================================================
# 17.1 TRADE OPERATING MODEL
# ============================================================
#
# TriLink does not need to own every physical asset.
#
# The preferred model is an asset-light, technology-led network
# using verified:
#
# - suppliers
# - manufacturers
# - exporters
# - freight forwarders
# - customs specialists
# - warehouses
# - distributors
# - local delivery providers
# - financial/payment partners
#
# TriLink coordinates the commercial transaction and logistics
# workflow while maintaining control through the CEO Dashboard.
#
# ============================================================
# 17.2 TRADE CORRIDOR MODEL
# ============================================================
#
# Every corridor must be represented as a structured route.
#
# ROUTE:
#
# ORIGIN
# ↓
# SUPPLIER
# ↓
# ORIGIN HANDLING
# ↓
# EXPORT PROCESS
# ↓
# TRANSIT COUNTRY / HUB
# ↓
# BORDER / CUSTOMS
# ↓
# DESTINATION
# ↓
# WAREHOUSE / DISTRIBUTION
# ↓
# CUSTOMER
#
# Each stage must have:
#
# - responsible party
# - expected date
# - actual date
# - documentation status
# - cost
# - risk status
# - exception status
#
# ============================================================
# 17.3 SHIPMENT RECORD
# ============================================================
#
# Every shipment must have a unique shipment ID.
#
# The record should include:
#
# - shipment ID
# - order ID
# - customer
# - supplier
# - origin
# - destination
# - cargo description
# - quantity
# - weight
# - volume
# - transport mode
# - carrier / logistics provider
# - tracking reference
# - expected departure
# - actual departure
# - expected arrival
# - actual arrival
# - customs status
# - insurance status where applicable
# - delivery status
# - shipment cost
# - exception status
#
# ============================================================
# 17.4 AI LOGISTICS AGENT
# ============================================================
#
# The Logistics Agent monitors active shipments and identifies:
#
# - delays
# - missed milestones
# - route changes
# - unusual transit times
# - documentation gaps
# - customs exceptions
# - supplier delays
# - carrier delays
# - delivery failures
#
# AI should automatically create an exception when a defined
# threshold is breached.
#
# ============================================================
# 17.5 SHIPMENT STATUS MODEL
# ============================================================
#
# PLANNED
# ↓
# BOOKED
# ↓
# READY FOR COLLECTION
# ↓
# COLLECTED
# ↓
# IN TRANSIT
# ↓
# BORDER / CUSTOMS
# ↓
# DESTINATION
# ↓
# OUT FOR DELIVERY
# ↓
# DELIVERED
# ↓
# CLOSED
#
# Exceptions must be represented separately rather than hidden
# inside normal status values.
#
# ============================================================
# 17.6 EXCEPTION MANAGEMENT
# ============================================================
#
# Examples:
#
# EXCEPTION — SUPPLIER DELAY
# AI identifies production delay.
#
# EXCEPTION — DOCUMENT MISSING
# Required document has not been received.
#
# EXCEPTION — BORDER DELAY
# Shipment remains at border beyond expected threshold.
#
# EXCEPTION — COST CHANGE
# Logistics cost materially exceeds estimate.
#
# EXCEPTION — CUSTOMER DELIVERY FAILURE
# Delivery cannot be completed.
#
# AI should:
#
# 1. detect
# 2. classify
# 3. estimate impact
# 4. recommend action
# 5. notify responsible party
# 6. escalate according to severity
# 7. record the resolution
#
# ============================================================
# 17.7 LOGISTICS PARTNER MANAGEMENT
# ============================================================
#
# Logistics partners must be scored using:
#
# - reliability
# - coverage
# - pricing
# - transit time
# - documentation quality
# - claims history
# - communication
# - capacity
# - compliance
# - customer feedback
#
# AI may recommend the best partner based on the shipment.
#
# Final approval rules must be configurable.
#
# ============================================================
# 17.8 ROUTE OPTIMISATION
# ============================================================
#
# AI should compare available routes using:
#
# - total landed cost
# - transit time
# - reliability
# - border complexity
# - capacity
# - cargo requirements
# - insurance requirements
# - risk
#
# Cheapest route must NOT automatically be selected.
#
# The objective is:
#
# BEST TOTAL COMMERCIAL OUTCOME
#
# rather than:
#
# LOWEST FREIGHT PRICE
#
# ============================================================
# 17.9 DOCUMENT CONTROL
# ============================================================
#
# Shipment documentation should be centrally associated with the
# shipment record.
#
# Examples may include:
#
# - commercial invoice
# - packing list
# - transport documents
# - certificates
# - origin documentation
# - customs documentation
# - insurance documentation
# - delivery confirmation
#
# AI can classify documents and identify missing information.
#
# AI must not fabricate documents or regulatory information.
#
# ============================================================
# 17.10 CUSTOMS & REGULATORY SAFETY
# ============================================================
#
# Customs and regulatory requirements vary by product, country
# and transaction.
#
# The system must therefore treat regulatory requirements as
# dynamic information requiring verification.
#
# AI may:
#
# - identify potentially applicable requirements
# - flag missing documents
# - prepare checklists
# - identify questions for customs specialists
#
# AI must escalate uncertain or high-risk regulatory matters to
# an appropriately qualified human or professional service.
#
# ============================================================
# 17.11 INVENTORY VISIBILITY
# ============================================================
#
# Where TriLink manages inventory or receives inventory data,
# the system should track:
#
# - available quantity
# - reserved quantity
# - incoming quantity
# - damaged quantity
# - reorder threshold
# - expected replenishment
#
# AI may forecast demand and recommend replenishment.
#
# ============================================================
# 17.12 DEMAND FORECASTING
# ============================================================
#
# AI should analyse:
#
# - historical orders
# - seasonality
# - customer behaviour
# - market trends
# - supplier lead times
# - price changes
# - logistics constraints
#
# Output:
#
# DEMAND FORECAST
# + CONFIDENCE
# + KEY DRIVERS
# + RISKS
#
# Forecasts are recommendations and must not be presented as
# guaranteed future outcomes.
#
# ============================================================
# 17.13 SUPPLY-CHAIN RISK SCORE
# ============================================================
#
# Each material transaction should receive a risk score based on:
#
# - supplier risk
# - customer risk
# - country risk
# - route risk
# - regulatory risk
# - payment risk
# - currency risk
# - concentration risk
# - operational risk
#
# HIGH-RISK transactions require escalation.
#
# ============================================================
# 17.14 SUPPLIER CONCENTRATION
# ============================================================
#
# TriLink should avoid unnecessary dependence on one supplier,
# carrier or logistics route.
#
# AI should identify:
#
# - single-source products
# - supplier concentration
# - route concentration
# - carrier concentration
#
# and recommend alternatives where commercially justified.
#
# ============================================================
# 17.15 CUSTOMER DELIVERY EXPERIENCE
# ============================================================
#
# Customers should receive appropriate status information without
# needing to contact TriLink for routine updates.
#
# Approved automated communications may include:
#
# - order confirmation
# - shipment confirmation
# - dispatch notification
# - milestone update
# - delay notification
# - estimated arrival update
# - delivery confirmation
#
# AI must use verified shipment data only.
#
# ============================================================
# 17.16 LOGISTICS KPIs
# ============================================================
#
# CEO Dashboard must track:
#
# - on-time shipment %
# - on-time delivery %
# - average transit time
# - average logistics cost
# - cost variance
# - shipment exception rate
# - documentation error rate
# - customs delay rate
# - carrier performance
# - supplier performance
# - customer delivery satisfaction
#
# ============================================================
# 17.17 CORRIDOR EXPANSION
# ============================================================
#
# New corridors must pass a controlled validation process:
#
# MARKET DEMAND
# ↓
# LEGAL / REGULATORY REVIEW
# ↓
# SUPPLY AVAILABILITY
# ↓
# LOGISTICS FEASIBILITY
# ↓
# UNIT ECONOMICS
# ↓
# PILOT TRANSACTIONS
# ↓
# PERFORMANCE REVIEW
# ↓
# CEO APPROVAL
# ↓
# SCALE
#
# AI may recommend expansion.
#
# Founder / Chairman / CEO approves strategic expansion.
#
# ============================================================
# 17.18 CORE PRINCIPLE
# ============================================================
#
# TriLink's logistics advantage should come from:
#
# VISIBILITY
# + SPEED
# + VERIFIED PARTNERS
# + DATA
# + AI
# + EXCEPTION MANAGEMENT
# + COMMERCIAL DISCIPLINE
#
# The business should build trust by making every transaction
# easier to understand, track and manage.
#
# ============================================================
# END PART 17
# ============================================================


# ============================================================
# PART 18 — AI BUSINESS INTELLIGENCE & EXECUTIVE CONTROL
# ============================================================
#
# TriLink Global will operate as an AI-first business.
#
# Platformsify CEO Dashboard is the SINGLE executive control
# centre for the entire TriLink organisation.
#
# The Founder / Chairman / CEO remains the ultimate authority.
#
# ============================================================
# 18.1 — EXECUTIVE COMMAND CENTRE
# ============================================================
#
# The CEO Dashboard must provide one consolidated view of:
#
# - Company performance
# - Revenue
# - Gross profit
# - Operating costs
# - Cash flow
# - Customers
# - Suppliers
# - Orders
# - Shipments
# - Trade opportunities
# - Logistics activity
# - Energy opportunities
# - Risks
# - Compliance
# - AI activity
# - Pending approvals
# - Strategic recommendations
#
# The objective is to allow the Founder / Chairman / CEO to
# understand the condition of the company from one system.
#
# ============================================================
# 18.2 — AI BUSINESS INTELLIGENCE
# ============================================================
#
# AI should continuously analyse authorised business data and
# identify:
#
# - growth opportunities
# - declining performance
# - unusual costs
# - margin problems
# - delayed transactions
# - supplier risks
# - customer risks
# - logistics problems
# - cash-flow risks
# - high-value opportunities
#
# AI recommendations must show the reasoning, relevant data and
# confidence/priority where practical.
#
# ============================================================
# 18.3 — CEO DAILY BRIEF
# ============================================================
#
# The CEO Dashboard should generate a concise executive briefing
# containing:
#
# 1. What happened
# 2. What is happening
# 3. What needs attention
# 4. What AI recommends
# 5. What requires CEO approval
# 6. Major financial movements
# 7. Major operational risks
# 8. Major opportunities
#
# ============================================================
# 18.4 — KPI CONTROL
# ============================================================
#
# Core KPIs:
#
# - Revenue
# - Gross margin
# - Net operating result
# - Cash position
# - Accounts receivable
# - Accounts payable
# - Order volume
# - Shipment volume
# - On-time delivery
# - Customer conversion
# - Supplier performance
# - Lead conversion
# - Opportunity pipeline
#
# KPIs must be traceable to their underlying business records.
#
# ============================================================
# 18.5 — AI ALERT PRIORITY
# ============================================================
#
# ALERT LEVEL 1 — INFORMATION
# Routine information requiring no immediate action.
#
# ALERT LEVEL 2 — ATTENTION
# Operational issue that should be reviewed.
#
# ALERT LEVEL 3 — IMPORTANT
# Issue likely to affect revenue, cost, customer or delivery.
#
# ALERT LEVEL 4 — CRITICAL
# Significant financial, legal, security, compliance or business
# risk requiring rapid human intervention.
#
# ============================================================
# 18.6 — OPPORTUNITY ENGINE
# ============================================================
#
# AI should identify potential:
#
# - buyers
# - suppliers
# - products
# - trade corridors
# - logistics routes
# - energy projects
# - partnerships
# - new markets
#
# Each opportunity should be scored using appropriate factors
# such as:
#
# - estimated value
# - margin potential
# - market demand
# - operational complexity
# - counterparty risk
# - regulatory complexity
# - capital requirement
# - strategic fit
#
# AI recommendations are advisory unless explicitly authorised
# for automated execution.
#
# ============================================================
# 18.7 — DECISION SUPPORT
# ============================================================
#
# AI should prepare decisions rather than hide decisions.
#
# Recommended decision format:
#
# DECISION
# WHY IT MATTERS
# DATA
# OPTIONS
# RISKS
# RECOMMENDATION
# REQUIRED APPROVAL
# NEXT ACTION
#
# ============================================================
# 18.8 — SINGLE SOURCE OF EXECUTIVE TRUTH
# ============================================================
#
# The Platformsify CEO Dashboard is the executive source of
# truth for TriLink.
#
# Other systems may provide specialised operational functions,
# but they must not create a competing CEO command centre.
#
# ============================================================
# 18.9 — DATA QUALITY
# ============================================================
#
# AI must not knowingly present incomplete or unverified data
# as confirmed information.
#
# Where information is missing, stale or uncertain, the system
# should identify that limitation.
#
# Critical decisions should require appropriate verification.
#
# ============================================================
# 18.10 — CEO APPROVAL QUEUE
# ============================================================
#
# The CEO Dashboard must maintain a central approval queue for
# actions requiring Founder / Chairman / CEO approval.
#
# Each approval should display:
#
# - requested action
# - requesting AI agent
# - business reason
# - financial impact
# - risk
# - supporting information
# - deadline
# - recommended decision
#
# ============================================================
# 18.11 — EXECUTIVE PRINCIPLE
# ============================================================
#
# The objective is NOT to make the Founder manually operate
# every part of TriLink.
#
# The objective is to give the Founder / Chairman / CEO maximum
# visibility and control while allowing AI to operate approved
# workflows automatically.
#
# ONE COMPANY
# ONE EXECUTIVE CONTROL CENTRE
# ONE CEO DASHBOARD
# AI-FIRST OPERATIONS
# HUMAN ULTIMATE AUTHORITY
#
# ============================================================


# ============================================================
# PART 19 — AI OPERATING MODEL & AUTONOMY CONTROL
# ============================================================
#
# TriLink Global will operate as an AI-first business while
# maintaining human governance, financial controls, legal
# compliance, security and operational accountability.
#
# Platformsify CEO Dashboard remains the SINGLE executive
# control centre for TriLink.
#
# No second CEO dashboard.
# No competing executive management system.
#
# ============================================================
# 19.1 — AI OPERATING PRINCIPLE
# ============================================================
#
# AI should automate repetitive, approved operational work.
#
# AI must never receive unrestricted authority by default.
#
# Every AI capability must have:
#
# - defined purpose
# - defined owner
# - defined permissions
# - defined data access
# - defined financial limits where applicable
# - audit logging
# - escalation rules
# - human override
#
# ============================================================
# 19.2 — AI AGENT STRUCTURE
# ============================================================
#
# TRI-LINK EXECUTIVE AI
# - KPI analysis
# - CEO briefings
# - strategic recommendations
# - exception detection
#
# TRI-LINK TRADE AI
# - buyer research
# - supplier research
# - product research
# - RFQ preparation
# - quote comparison
# - opportunity scoring
#
# TRI-LINK LOGISTICS AI
# - shipment monitoring
# - route analysis
# - delivery monitoring
# - ETA analysis
# - logistics exception detection
#
# TRI-LINK ENERGY AI
# - energy-market research
# - project opportunity research
# - supplier/project screening
# - opportunity scoring
# - project preparation
#
# TRI-LINK FINANCE AI
# - revenue monitoring
# - margin monitoring
# - invoice monitoring
# - cash-flow forecasting
# - anomaly detection
#
# TRI-LINK CUSTOMER AI
# - lead qualification
# - customer follow-up
# - CRM preparation
# - customer-service triage
# - communication drafting
#
# TRI-LINK SUPPLIER AI
# - supplier research
# - supplier onboarding preparation
# - supplier performance monitoring
# - documentation checking
#
# TRI-LINK COMPLIANCE AI
# - document checks
# - missing-document alerts
# - compliance workflow preparation
# - risk flagging
# - escalation
#
# TRI-LINK SECURITY AI
# - security monitoring
# - suspicious-activity detection
# - access review support
# - incident escalation
#
# ============================================================
# 19.3 — AI AUTONOMY LEVELS
# ============================================================
#
# LEVEL 0 — OBSERVE
#
# AI may:
# - read authorised information
# - analyse information
# - produce recommendations
#
# No operational changes.
#
# LEVEL 1 — ASSIST
#
# AI may:
# - draft emails
# - draft documents
# - prepare reports
# - prepare quotes
# - prepare tasks
#
# Human approval required for external execution unless
# explicitly pre-approved.
#
# LEVEL 2 — CONTROLLED AUTOMATION
#
# AI may perform predefined low-risk internal workflows.
#
# Examples:
# - update internal records
# - categorise leads
# - create internal tasks
# - generate routine reports
# - update approved operational statuses
#
# LEVEL 3 — SUPERVISED COMMERCIAL ACTION
#
# AI may perform predefined commercial workflows within
# approved limits.
#
# Examples:
# - standard customer follow-up
# - standard supplier communication
# - approved RFQ communication
# - routine operational communication
#
# LEVEL 4 — HUMAN APPROVAL
#
# Human approval is mandatory for:
#
# - major contracts
# - major purchases
# - significant payments
# - unusual pricing
# - banking changes
# - legal commitments
# - regulatory submissions
# - high-value commitments
# - high-risk suppliers
# - major partnerships
# - new-country expansion
#
# LEVEL 5 — FOUNDER / CHAIRMAN / CEO ONLY
#
# Reserved for decisions involving:
#
# - ownership
# - strategic direction
# - major corporate restructuring
# - ultimate financial authority
# - major capital commitments
# - major strategic partnerships
# - AI governance changes
#
# ============================================================
# 19.4 — FINANCIAL AI SAFETY
# ============================================================
#
# AI must not have unrestricted access to company funds.
#
# Financial automation must use:
#
# - transaction limits
# - approved counterparties
# - approval thresholds
# - audit logs
# - anomaly detection
# - human escalation
# - separation of duties where appropriate
#
# AI must not independently change:
#
# - bank ownership
# - bank beneficiaries
# - payment credentials
# - company ownership
# - shareholder information
# - financial authority
#
# ============================================================
# 19.5 — AI COMMUNICATION CONTROL
# ============================================================
#
# ROUTINE COMMUNICATION
# AI may send where the workflow has been explicitly approved.
#
# COMMERCIAL COMMUNICATION
# AI may prepare or send only within authorised permissions.
#
# LEGAL / REGULATORY COMMUNICATION
# Human approval required.
#
# HIGH-RISK OR REPUTATIONAL COMMUNICATION
# Human approval required.
#
# CEO / CHAIRMAN COMMUNICATION
# AI may prepare drafts, but final authority remains with the
# Founder / Chairman / CEO unless explicitly delegated.
#
# ============================================================
# 19.6 — AI AUDIT TRAIL
# ============================================================
#
# Material AI actions should record:
#
# - timestamp
# - AI agent
# - user/owner
# - requested action
# - relevant data
# - recommendation
# - action taken
# - permission level
# - approval status
# - result
# - escalation status
#
# Audit records must be protected against unauthorised changes.
#
# ============================================================
# 19.7 — AI DECISION LOOP
# ============================================================
#
# DETECT
#     ↓
# ANALYSE
#     ↓
# CLASSIFY RISK
#     ↓
# CHECK PERMISSIONS
#     ↓
# ACT OR REQUEST APPROVAL
#     ↓
# VERIFY RESULT
#     ↓
# LOG ACTION
#     ↓
# ESCALATE IF REQUIRED
#
# ============================================================
# 19.8 — CEO EXCEPTION MANAGEMENT
# ============================================================
#
# The CEO Dashboard should prioritise:
#
# - delayed shipments
# - failed payments
# - unusual margins
# - supplier failures
# - customer complaints
# - compliance problems
# - missing documents
# - suspicious transactions
# - important deadlines
# - major opportunities
# - material cash-flow changes
#
# AI should reduce operational noise and escalate matters that
# genuinely require executive attention.
#
# ============================================================
# 19.9 — HUMAN OVERRIDE
# ============================================================
#
# The Founder / Chairman / CEO must be able to:
#
# - pause an AI agent
# - revoke permissions
# - approve an action
# - reject an action
# - change thresholds
# - disable an automation
# - review audit history
# - require human approval
# - restore approved operation
#
# Critical automated workflows must have an emergency
# disable/shutdown mechanism.
#
# ============================================================
# 19.10 — AI GOVERNANCE RULE
# ============================================================
#
# AI operates the workflows delegated to it.
#
# The Founder / Chairman / CEO owns the business decisions.
#
# Platformsify CEO Dashboard remains the SINGLE executive
# control centre.
#
# TriLink must not create a second CEO dashboard.
#
# ============================================================
# 19.11 — OPERATING PRINCIPLE
# ============================================================
#
# MAXIMUM AI AUTOMATION
# +
# CONTROLLED PERMISSIONS
# +
# COMPLETE AUDITABILITY
# +
# HUMAN OVERRIDE
# +
# FOUNDER / CEO AUTHORITY
#
# ============================================================

# ============================================================
# PART 23 — AI GOVERNANCE, SECURITY, AUDIT & CEO CONTROL
# ============================================================

# PURPOSE
#
# This section defines the final governance layer for TriLink
# Global's AI-first operating model.
#
# Platformsify CEO Dashboard remains the SINGLE executive
# control centre.
#
# No independent AI system may override the Founder / Chairman
# / CEO or create a competing executive control centre.

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 23.1 CEO AUTHORITY
# ------------------------------------------------------------

# Founder / Chairman / CEO:
# Meheraj Uddin
#
# Ultimate authority remains with the Founder / Chairman / CEO.
#
# AI recommendations are not automatically equal to CEO decisions.
#
# The CEO can:
# - approve
# - reject
# - pause
# - modify
# - delegate
# - revoke
# - override
# - shut down
# any AI-controlled workflow.

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 23.2 AI PERMISSION LEVELS
# ------------------------------------------------------------

# LEVEL 0 — OBSERVE
#
# AI may:
# - read approved operational data
# - analyse information
# - identify opportunities
# - produce reports
# - detect anomalies
#
# AI may NOT execute transactions.

# LEVEL 1 — ASSIST
#
# AI may:
# - draft emails
# - draft quotations
# - prepare supplier/customer records
# - prepare logistics documents
# - prepare internal reports
#
# Human approval required before external commitment.

# LEVEL 2 — CONTROLLED AUTOMATION
#
# AI may execute pre-approved low-risk workflows.
#
# Examples:
# - lead classification
# - CRM updates
# - routine notifications
# - shipment status updates
# - document generation
# - internal task creation
# - routine reconciliation
#
# Actions must remain logged and reversible where practical.

# LEVEL 3 — EXECUTIVE APPROVAL
#
# AI prepares:
# - contracts
# - major quotations
# - high-value transactions
# - strategic partnerships
# - major supplier decisions
# - major customer decisions
#
# CEO approval required before execution.

# LEVEL 4 — CEO ONLY
#
# The following remain exclusively controlled by the Founder /
# Chairman / CEO:
#
# - ownership changes
# - company sale
# - acquisition
# - major borrowing
# - bank account changes
# - payment-account changes
# - major capital expenditure
# - major legal commitments
# - regulatory decisions
# - new-country expansion
# - changes to AI authority
# - deletion of critical business data
# - permanent shutdown
#
# AI may advise but cannot independently execute these decisions.

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 23.3 SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH
# ------------------------------------------------------------

# The Platformsify CEO Dashboard is the executive source of truth.
#
# It should consolidate:
#
# - company KPIs
# - revenue
# - gross margin
# - pipeline
# - customers
# - suppliers
# - shipments
# - energy opportunities
# - cash position
# - receivables
# - payables
# - risks
# - AI activity
# - alerts
# - approvals
# - compliance status
# - strategic objectives
#
# Do not build a separate TriLink CEO dashboard.
#
# TriLink-specific screens, permissions and data may exist inside
# the Platformsify CEO Dashboard, but executive control remains
# centralised.

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 23.4 AI AUDIT TRAIL
# ------------------------------------------------------------

# Every material AI action must record:
#
# - timestamp
# - AI agent/workflow
# - user/system initiating action
# - action requested
# - action performed
# - data/source used
# - permission level
# - approval status
# - resulting change
# - error status
#
# Audit records should be tamper-resistant and retained according
# to applicable legal and business requirements.

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 23.5 HUMAN APPROVAL GATES
# ------------------------------------------------------------

# Mandatory CEO approval gates:
#
# GATE A
# Strategic decisions
#
# GATE B
# High-value financial commitments
#
# GATE C
# Binding legal agreements
#
# GATE D
# High-risk suppliers/customers
#
# GATE E
# Regulatory/compliance exceptions
#
# GATE F
# New-country expansion
#
# GATE G
# AI permission escalation
#
# GATE H
# Security-critical changes

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 23.6 AI SAFETY RULES
# ------------------------------------------------------------

# AI must NOT:
#
# - fabricate customers
# - fabricate suppliers
# - fabricate prices
# - fabricate shipment information
# - fabricate financial results
# - fabricate regulatory approvals
# - impersonate the CEO without authorisation
# - approve its own restricted actions
# - bypass approval gates
# - disable audit logging
# - delete evidence of actions
# - expose confidential information without permission
# - make unsupported legal claims
# - make unsupported financial guarantees
#
# If required information is unavailable:
#
# AI must report:
# "INSUFFICIENT VERIFIED DATA"
#
# rather than inventing an answer.

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 23.7 SECURITY MODEL
# ------------------------------------------------------------

# Apply:
#
# - least-privilege access
# - role-based permissions
# - strong authentication
# - secret management
# - encrypted connections
# - encrypted sensitive data where appropriate
# - environment separation
# - production/development separation
# - secure API authentication
# - rate limiting
# - input validation
# - output validation
# - security logging
# - backup and recovery procedures
#
# Secrets must never be committed to Git.
#
# .env files containing secrets must remain outside source control.

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 23.8 ROLE MODEL
# ------------------------------------------------------------

# CEO
# Full executive authority.
#
# AI OPERATIONS
# Executes approved automated workflows.
#
# FINANCE
# Financial visibility and controlled financial workflows.
#
# COMMERCIAL
# Customers, sales and quotations.
#
# LOGISTICS
# Shipments, routes and operational coordination.
#
# ENERGY
# Energy-sector opportunities and projects.
#
# COMPLIANCE
# Regulatory and documentation oversight.
#
# TECHNOLOGY
# Platformsify integration and technical operations.
#
# AUDITOR
# Read-only audit and reporting access.

# No role receives more authority than required.

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 23.9 AI AGENT ARCHITECTURE
# ------------------------------------------------------------

# TriLink may use specialised AI agents, but all agents report
# through the central Platformsify operating architecture.
#
# Suggested agents:
#
# TRI-COMMERCIAL
# Sales, leads, customers and quotations.
#
# TRI-SUPPLY
# Supplier discovery, comparison and procurement support.
#
# TRI-LOGISTICS
# Shipment coordination, route intelligence and status monitoring.
#
# TRI-ENERGY
# Energy opportunity analysis and project intelligence.
#
# TRI-FINANCE
# Financial analysis, reconciliation support and forecasting.
#
# TRI-COMPLIANCE
# Documentation, regulatory monitoring and risk flags.
#
# TRI-CEO
# Executive briefing, KPI analysis and decision support.
#
# Important:
# These agents are operational specialists.
# They do NOT become separate executive dashboards.

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 23.10 CEO DAILY BRIEF
# ------------------------------------------------------------

# The CEO Dashboard should provide a concise daily briefing:
#
# 1. Cash position
# 2. Revenue
# 3. Gross margin
# 4. New customers
# 5. Open opportunities
# 6. Active shipments
# 7. Supplier issues
# 8. Customer issues
# 9. Energy opportunities
# 10. Compliance alerts
# 11. AI actions
# 12. Items requiring CEO approval
# 13. Critical risks
# 14. Recommended actions
#
# Recommendations must clearly distinguish:
#
# VERIFIED FACT
# AI ANALYSIS
# AI RECOMMENDATION
# CEO DECISION REQUIRED

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 23.11 EMERGENCY STOP
# ------------------------------------------------------------

# The CEO Dashboard must provide an emergency AI control.
#
# CEO can:
#
# - pause all AI execution
# - pause individual agents
# - revoke permissions
# - disable automated transactions
# - force human approval
#
# Emergency stop must not delete audit records.

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 23.12 BUSINESS CONTINUITY
# ------------------------------------------------------------

# TriLink must maintain:
#
# - regular backups
# - recovery procedures
# - disaster recovery documentation
# - incident response procedures
# - credential recovery
# - service dependency inventory
# - critical supplier/customer contact records
#
# Critical operations should have a documented manual fallback.

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 23.13 AI PERFORMANCE MONITORING
# ------------------------------------------------------------

# Track:
#
# - AI accuracy
# - failed workflows
# - rejected recommendations
# - approval rates
# - false alerts
# - execution errors
# - processing time
# - cost per workflow
# - customer-impacting errors
#
# Poor-performing AI workflows must automatically move toward
# human-review mode until corrected.

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 23.14 DATA QUALITY
# ------------------------------------------------------------

# Critical decisions should use verified data wherever possible.
#
# Data should be classified:
#
# VERIFIED
# CONFIRMED
# UNVERIFIED
# ESTIMATED
# AI-GENERATED
#
# AI-generated information must not silently become verified
# business information.

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 23.15 CEO DECISION QUEUE
# ------------------------------------------------------------

# Platformsify CEO Dashboard should maintain a central queue:
#
# PENDING APPROVAL
# APPROVED
# REJECTED
# DEFERRED
# ESCALATED
# EXECUTED
#
# Each decision should show:
#
# - recommendation
# - reason
# - financial impact
# - risk
# - supporting evidence
# - deadline
# - requested action

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 23.16 FINAL GOVERNANCE PRINCIPLE
# ------------------------------------------------------------

# TRI-LINK IS AI-FIRST.
#
# AI performs the work.
# Platformsify provides the technology operating layer.
# The CEO Dashboard provides executive visibility and control.
#
# AI does not replace corporate authority.
#
# AI increases speed, intelligence, automation and scalability
# while the Founder / Chairman / CEO retains ultimate authority.

# ============================================================
# END OF PART 23
# ============================================================


# ============================================================
# PART 24 — COMMERCIAL ENGINE, REVENUE & AI OPERATIONS
# ============================================================

# PURPOSE
#
# This section defines how TriLink Global turns its AI-first
# infrastructure into a functioning commercial business.
#
# The objective is to create a scalable system that can:
#
# - find opportunities
# - qualify customers
# - identify suppliers
# - build quotations
# - coordinate trade
# - coordinate logistics
# - manage energy opportunities
# - monitor transactions
# - measure profitability
# - retain customers
#
# All executive visibility remains inside the
# Platformsify CEO Dashboard.

# ============================================================
# 24.1 COMMERCIAL BUSINESS MODEL
# ============================================================

# TriLink Global operates as an AI-first trade, logistics and
# energy coordination business.
#
# Core commercial pillars:
#
# 1. TRADE
# 2. LOGISTICS
# 3. ENERGY
# 4. PROCUREMENT
# 5. BUSINESS-TO-BUSINESS SERVICES
#
# Initial corridor:
#
