# Nepal
#   ↓
# India
#   ↓
# Bangladesh
#
# The model is designed to expand into additional corridors
# after the initial operating model is proven.

# ============================================================
# 24.2 TRADE ENGINE
# ============================================================

# TriLink Trade connects:
#
# SUPPLIERS
#      ↓
# TRI-LINK COMMERCIAL ENGINE
#      ↓
# BUYERS
#
# AI may assist with:
#
# - supplier discovery
# - product matching
# - buyer discovery
# - market research
# - price comparison
# - quotation preparation
# - margin analysis
# - documentation
# - follow-up
#
# AI must not invent supplier capability, pricing or stock.

# ============================================================
# 24.3 CUSTOMER ACQUISITION
# ============================================================

# Customer sources:
#
# - direct B2B outreach
# - inbound website enquiries
# - referrals
# - trade networks
# - distributors
# - wholesalers
# - manufacturers
# - institutional buyers
# - strategic partnerships
#
# AI can:
#
# 1. identify potential customers
# 2. enrich permitted business information
# 3. score prospects
# 4. classify industry
# 5. identify likely requirements
# 6. prepare outreach drafts
# 7. schedule approved follow-ups
#
# Human/CEO approval is required where the action creates a
# significant commercial or legal commitment.

# ============================================================
# 24.4 CUSTOMER PIPELINE
# ============================================================

# Every customer opportunity should move through defined stages:
#
# LEAD
# ↓
# QUALIFIED
# ↓
# REQUIREMENT IDENTIFIED
# ↓
# SUPPLIER / SOLUTION MATCHED
# ↓
# QUOTATION PREPARED
# ↓
# CUSTOMER REVIEW
# ↓
# NEGOTIATION
# ↓
# ORDER CONFIRMED
# ↓
# FULFILMENT
# ↓
# COMPLETED
# ↓
# REPEAT / RETENTION
#
# Lost opportunities should be recorded with a reason.

# ============================================================
# 24.5 CUSTOMER SCORING
# ============================================================

# AI may score prospects using approved factors:
#
# - estimated demand
# - purchasing frequency
# - geographic fit
# - product fit
# - payment reliability indicators
# - strategic value
# - expected margin
# - operational complexity
# - compliance risk
#
# Score categories:
#
# A — HIGH PRIORITY
# B — GOOD OPPORTUNITY
# C — DEVELOP
# D — LOW PRIORITY
# E — DO NOT PURSUE
#
# AI recommendations remain subject to business rules and
# human oversight.

# ============================================================
# 24.6 SUPPLIER ENGINE
# ============================================================

# Supplier lifecycle:
#
# DISCOVERY
# ↓
# SCREENING
# ↓
# VERIFICATION
# ↓
# COMMERCIAL REVIEW
# ↓
# APPROVED SUPPLIER
# ↓
# ACTIVE
# ↓
# PERFORMANCE REVIEW
#
# Supplier records should include:
#
# - legal/business name
# - country
# - products/services
# - capacity
# - lead time
# - commercial terms
# - documentation
# - certifications where relevant
# - payment terms
# - reliability
# - risk rating
# - performance history

# ============================================================
# 24.7 SUPPLIER RISK
# ============================================================

# AI should flag:
#
# - incomplete information
# - inconsistent company information
# - unusual pricing
# - missing documentation
# - suspicious payment requests
# - sudden bank-detail changes
# - delivery failures
# - repeated quality problems
#
# High-risk suppliers must be escalated.

# ============================================================
# 24.8 QUOTATION ENGINE
# ============================================================

# AI can prepare draft quotations using verified data.
#
# Quote calculation may include:
#
# PRODUCT COST
# + LOGISTICS
# + INSURANCE
# + DUTIES/TAXES WHERE APPLICABLE
# + HANDLING
# + FINANCING COST
# + RISK BUFFER
# + TRI-LINK MARGIN
# = CUSTOMER PRICE
#
# Every quote must preserve the underlying calculation.
#
# No hidden margin manipulation.

# ============================================================
# 24.9 MARGIN CONTROL
# ============================================================

# Every transaction should calculate:
#
# REVENUE
# - PRODUCT COST
# - LOGISTICS COST
# - OTHER DIRECT COSTS
# = GROSS PROFIT
#
# GROSS PROFIT / REVENUE
# = GROSS MARGIN %
#
# AI should flag transactions where:
#
# - margin is below target
# - cost assumptions are missing
# - logistics cost is uncertain
# - currency exposure is significant
# - payment risk is elevated

# ============================================================
# 24.10 LOGISTICS ENGINE
# ============================================================

# TriLink Logistics coordinates approved shipments.
#
# Shipment lifecycle:
#
# ORDER
# ↓
# BOOKING
# ↓
# DOCUMENTATION
# ↓
# ORIGIN
# ↓
# TRANSIT
# ↓
# BORDER / CUSTOMS
# ↓
# DESTINATION
# ↓
# DELIVERY
# ↓
# CLOSED
#
# AI may monitor status and identify delays.

# ============================================================
# 24.11 ROUTE INTELLIGENCE
# ============================================================

# AI may analyse:
#
# - route options
# - estimated transit time
# - cost
# - border requirements
# - transport modes
# - risk
# - delays
# - supplier location
# - customer location
#
# The system should recommend the best available route based
# on cost, speed, reliability and risk.

# ============================================================
# 24.12 ENERGY ENGINE
# ============================================================

# TriLink Energy operates as a specialist commercial pillar.
#
# AI may identify:
#
# - energy projects
# - suppliers
# - infrastructure opportunities
# - equipment requirements
# - project partners
# - commercial opportunities
#
# Energy projects require additional due diligence because of
# their financial, technical and regulatory complexity.

# ============================================================
# 24.13 ENERGY PROJECT PIPELINE
# ============================================================

# ENERGY LEAD
# ↓
# INITIAL SCREEN
# ↓
# TECHNICAL REVIEW
# ↓
# COMMERCIAL REVIEW
# ↓
# PARTNER / SUPPLIER IDENTIFICATION
# ↓
# PROJECT PROPOSAL
# ↓
# DUE DILIGENCE
# ↓
# APPROVAL
# ↓
# CONTRACT
# ↓
# EXECUTION
# ↓
# MONITORING
#
# AI assists throughout the pipeline but does not replace
# qualified professional, legal, financial or technical review.

# ============================================================
# 24.14 PROCUREMENT
# ============================================================

# AI procurement workflow:
#
# CUSTOMER REQUIREMENT
# ↓
# PRODUCT/SERVICE IDENTIFICATION
# ↓
# SUPPLIER SEARCH
# ↓
# SUPPLIER COMPARISON
# ↓
# COST ANALYSIS
# ↓
# RISK CHECK
# ↓
# QUOTATION
# ↓
# APPROVAL
# ↓
# PURCHASE
#
# Supplier selection should consider total value, not simply
# lowest price.

# ============================================================
# 24.15 DOCUMENT AUTOMATION
# ============================================================

# AI may prepare drafts of:
#
# - quotations
# - purchase orders
# - invoices
# - commercial summaries
# - shipment summaries
# - supplier comparison reports
# - customer reports
# - internal briefing documents
#
# Legal documents require appropriate review before becoming
# binding.

# ============================================================
# 24.16 FINANCIAL OPERATING MODEL
# ============================================================

# CEO Dashboard should show:
#
# - sales
# - revenue
# - gross profit
# - gross margin
# - outstanding invoices
# - receivables
# - payables
# - operating expenses
# - cash position
# - forecast
# - transaction pipeline
#
# AI may forecast cash and revenue using approved data.
#
# Forecasts must clearly be labelled as forecasts.

# ============================================================
# 24.17 PAYMENT CONTROL
# ============================================================

# AI must NOT independently change:
#
# - bank details
# - beneficiary details
# - payment accounts
# - payment approval rules
#
# Bank-detail changes require independent verification.
#
# High-value payments require the appropriate human approval.

# ============================================================
# 24.18 CUSTOMER CREDIT CONTROL
# ============================================================

# Before offering material credit:
#
# - verify customer identity
# - assess payment risk
# - assess transaction size
# - assess trading history where available
# - establish approved credit terms
#
# AI can recommend limits.
#
# Final approval follows company policy.

# ============================================================
# 24.19 REVENUE STREAMS
# ============================================================

# TriLink may generate revenue from:
#
# 1. TRADE MARGIN
# 2. PROCUREMENT MARGIN
# 3. LOGISTICS COORDINATION FEES
# 4. SOURCING FEES
# 5. B2B SERVICE FEES
# 6. PROJECT / ENERGY ADVISORY FEES
# 7. PARTNERSHIP / COMMERCIAL ARRANGEMENT FEES
# 8. RECURRING ENTERPRISE SERVICES
#
# Revenue streams must be legally and commercially validated
# before launch.

# ============================================================
# 24.20 CUSTOMER RETENTION
# ============================================================

# After successful fulfilment:
#
# AI may:
#
# - request feedback
# - identify repeat demand
# - forecast next order
# - prepare follow-up
# - identify cross-selling opportunities
# - identify logistics optimisation opportunities
#
# The objective is to turn one-off transactions into repeat
# commercial relationships.

# ============================================================
# 24.21 AI SALES ASSISTANT
# ============================================================

# AI Sales Assistant responsibilities:
#
# - lead research
# - qualification
# - CRM updates
# - follow-up drafting
# - opportunity scoring
# - quote preparation
# - pipeline forecasting
# - customer summaries
#
# It must never misrepresent TriLink's capabilities.

# ============================================================
# 24.22 AI PROCUREMENT ASSISTANT
# ============================================================

# Responsibilities:
#
# - supplier research
# - product matching
# - supplier comparison
# - cost analysis
# - lead-time analysis
# - risk flagging
# - procurement recommendations

# ============================================================
# 24.23 AI LOGISTICS ASSISTANT
# ============================================================

# Responsibilities:
#
# - shipment monitoring
# - ETA tracking
# - delay alerts
# - document status
# - route analysis
# - exception management
# - customer shipment updates

# ============================================================
# 24.24 AI FINANCE ASSISTANT
# ============================================================

# Responsibilities:
#
# - revenue reporting
# - margin analysis
# - receivables monitoring
# - payables monitoring
# - cash forecasting
# - anomaly detection
# - management reporting
#
# It does not replace qualified accounting professionals.

# ============================================================
# 24.25 AI CEO ASSISTANT
# ============================================================

# The AI CEO Assistant provides:
#
# DAILY:
# - executive briefing
#
# WEEKLY:
# - performance review
#
# MONTHLY:
# - management report
#
# QUARTERLY:
# - strategic review
#
# The CEO Assistant should answer:
#
# "What happened?"
# "Why did it happen?"
# "What is changing?"
# "What is the risk?"
# "What should we do?"
# "What requires my approval?"

# ============================================================
# 24.26 KPI FRAMEWORK
# ============================================================

# CORE KPIs:
#
# Revenue
# Gross Profit
# Gross Margin
# Number of Active Customers
# New Customers
# Repeat Customers
# Qualified Leads
# Conversion Rate
# Average Order Value
# Supplier Reliability
# Shipment On-Time Rate
# Customer Satisfaction
# Receivables Days
# Cash Runway
# AI Automation Rate
# AI Error Rate
# Open Risks
# Pending CEO Decisions

# ============================================================
# 24.27 AI AUTOMATION TARGET
# ============================================================

# Target:
#
# AUTOMATE AS MUCH LOW-RISK OPERATIONAL WORK AS PRACTICABLE.
#
# AI should reduce repetitive human administration.
#
# Human attention should focus on:
#
# - strategy
# - relationships
# - negotiations
# - high-value decisions
# - legal matters
# - financial control
# - risk
# - leadership
#
# "100% AI-RUN" means AI-first operational execution where
# technically and legally appropriate, NOT removal of human
# accountability.

# ============================================================
# 24.28 EXECUTIVE ESCALATION
# ============================================================

# AI must escalate when:
#
# - confidence is low
# - information conflicts
# - risk is high
# - financial exposure is material
# - legal interpretation is required
# - regulatory uncertainty exists
# - customer/supplier identity is unclear
# - transaction data is incomplete
# - an unusual pattern is detected
#
# Escalation should create a visible CEO Dashboard task.

# ============================================================
# 24.29 COMMERCIAL CONTROL LOOP
# ============================================================

# Every commercial transaction follows:
#
# DISCOVER
# ↓
# QUALIFY
# ↓
# VERIFY
# ↓
# PRICE
# ↓
# APPROVE
# ↓
# EXECUTE
# ↓
# MONITOR
# ↓
# COMPLETE
# ↓
# MEASURE
# ↓
# LEARN
# ↓
# IMPROVE
#
# AI continuously improves recommendations from verified
# historical performance.

# ============================================================
# 24.30 MASTER OPERATING PRINCIPLE
# ============================================================

# TriLink Global should operate as:
#
# AI-FIRST
# DATA-DRIVEN
# AUTOMATED
# AUDITABLE
# PROFIT-FOCUSED
# RISK-AWARE
# CUSTOMER-CENTRIC
# SCALABLE
#
# Platformsify CEO Dashboard remains the single executive
# control centre.
#
# The Founder / Chairman / CEO remains the ultimate authority.
#
# ============================================================
# END OF PART 24
# ============================================================


# ============================================================
# PART 25 — TECHNOLOGY ARCHITECTURE & PLATFORM INTEGRATION
# ============================================================

# PURPOSE
#
# This section defines the technical architecture for TriLink
# Global and its relationship with Platformsify.
#
# CORE RULE:
#
# Platformsify = TECHNOLOGY OPERATING LAYER
# TriLink = COMMERCIAL BUSINESS
# Platformsify CEO Dashboard = SINGLE EXECUTIVE CONTROL CENTRE
#
# No duplicate CEO dashboard is permitted.

# ============================================================
# 25.1 TECHNOLOGY PRINCIPLES
# ============================================================

# The TriLink technology platform must be:
#
# - modular
# - secure
# - scalable
# - observable
# - API-first
# - AI-first
# - mobile-ready
# - cloud-ready
# - fault-tolerant
# - auditable
#
# Architecture should avoid unnecessary complexity.
#
# Build only what is required for the current business stage,
# while keeping the architecture ready for expansion.

# ============================================================
# 25.2 SYSTEM OF RECORD
# ============================================================

# The platform must maintain authoritative records for:
#
# - customers
# - suppliers
# - products
# - services
# - opportunities
# - quotations
# - orders
# - shipments
# - invoices
# - payments
# - projects
# - AI actions
# - approvals
# - risks
# - users
#
# Duplicate records should be avoided.

# ============================================================
# 25.3 PLATFORM LAYERS
# ============================================================

# LAYER 1 — USER INTERFACE
#
# - CEO Dashboard
# - operational interfaces
# - customer-facing interfaces
# - supplier interfaces
# - mobile interfaces where required
#
# LAYER 2 — APPLICATION
#
# - CRM
# - trade management
# - procurement
# - logistics
# - energy
# - finance
# - compliance
# - reporting
#
# LAYER 3 — AI
#
# - AI agents
# - classification
# - forecasting
# - recommendations
# - document processing
# - workflow automation
#
# LAYER 4 — DATA
#
# - operational database
# - analytics data
# - audit logs
# - document storage
#
# LAYER 5 — INTEGRATIONS
#
# - payment systems
# - communications
# - logistics services
# - external data providers
# - business services
#
# LAYER 6 — SECURITY
#
# - authentication
# - authorisation
# - secrets
# - logging
# - monitoring
# - backup

# ============================================================
# 25.4 PLATFORMSIFY INTEGRATION
# ============================================================

# Platformsify remains the central technology platform.
#
# TriLink-specific business logic may be implemented as modules,
# applications, services or workflows within the Platformsify
# architecture.
#
# The CEO Dashboard must provide access to:
#
# - TriLink KPIs
# - commercial pipeline
# - operational status
# - financial indicators
# - AI activity
# - approvals
# - risk
# - alerts
#
# Do not create:
#
# "TriLink CEO Dashboard"
#
# as a separate competing executive system.
#
# Instead:
#
# "TriLink Executive View"
#
# may exist inside the Platformsify CEO Dashboard.

# ============================================================
# 25.5 APPLICATION STRUCTURE
# ============================================================

# Recommended logical modules:
#
# /commercial
# /customers
# /suppliers
# /trade
# /procurement
# /logistics
# /energy
# /finance
# /compliance
# /ai
# /approvals
# /analytics
# /documents
# /notifications
# /audit
#
# Modules should communicate through defined interfaces.

# ============================================================
# 25.6 API-FIRST ARCHITECTURE
# ============================================================

# Core functionality should be accessible through secure APIs.
#
# API principles:
#
# - authentication required
# - authorisation required
# - input validation
# - rate limiting
# - structured errors
# - versioning
# - logging
# - auditability
#
# Sensitive operations must require appropriate permission.

# ============================================================
# 25.7 DATABASE
# ============================================================

# The primary database should support:
#
# - relational business records
# - transactions
# - relationships
# - auditability
# - reliable backups
# - secure access
#
# Recommended conceptual entities:
#
# USERS
# ORGANISATIONS
# CUSTOMERS
# SUPPLIERS
# PRODUCTS
# SERVICES
# LEADS
# OPPORTUNITIES
# QUOTES
# ORDERS
# SHIPMENTS
# PROJECTS
# INVOICES
# PAYMENTS
# APPROVALS
# AI_ACTIONS
# RISKS
# DOCUMENTS
#
# Database design must preserve referential integrity.

# ============================================================
# 25.8 DATA CLASSIFICATION
# ============================================================

# DATA TYPES:
#
# PUBLIC
# INTERNAL
# CONFIDENTIAL
# RESTRICTED
# HIGHLY RESTRICTED
#
# Access must be based on business need.
#
# Sensitive information must not be exposed to AI agents unless
# the agent has explicit permission.

# ============================================================
# 25.9 AI MODEL LAYER
# ============================================================

# AI models should be treated as replaceable components.
#
# The application must not depend unnecessarily on a single
# model implementation.
#
# AI provider/model configuration should be centrally controlled.
#
# AI workflows should define:
#
# - purpose
# - inputs
# - permissions
# - expected output
# - validation
# - escalation
# - logging
#
# Model changes must not silently change business permissions.

# ============================================================
# 25.10 AI AGENT RUNTIME
# ============================================================

# Each agent should have:
#
# - unique identity
# - defined role
# - permission scope
# - tool access
# - data access
# - execution limits
# - logging
# - escalation rules
#
# Example:
#
# TRI-COMMERCIAL
# can access commercial records.
#
# TRI-LOGISTICS
# can access shipment records.
#
# TRI-FINANCE
# can analyse financial information.
#
# No agent receives unrestricted system access by default.

# ============================================================
# 25.11 TOOL PERMISSIONS
# ============================================================

# AI tools should be categorised:
#
# READ
# WRITE
# EXECUTE
# APPROVE
# ADMIN
#
# Default:
#
# READ > WRITE > EXECUTE > APPROVE > ADMIN
#
# Higher permissions require explicit authorisation.

# ============================================================
# 25.12 WORKFLOW ENGINE
# ============================================================

# Automated workflows should follow:
#
# TRIGGER
# ↓
# VALIDATE
# ↓
# AI ANALYSIS
# ↓
# POLICY CHECK
# ↓
# APPROVAL CHECK
# ↓
# EXECUTE
# ↓
# VERIFY
# ↓
# LOG
# ↓
# NOTIFY
#
# Failed validation must stop execution.

# ============================================================
# 25.13 EVENT ARCHITECTURE
# ============================================================

# Important business events may include:
#
# customer.created
# lead.created
# opportunity.created
# quote.created
# quote.approved
# order.created
# shipment.created
# shipment.delayed
# invoice.created
# payment.received
# payment.overdue
# risk.detected
# approval.required
# approval.completed
# ai.action.completed
#
# Events should be traceable.

# ============================================================
# 25.14 NOTIFICATION ENGINE
# ============================================================

# Notifications may be delivered through approved channels.
#
# Categories:
#
# INFORMATION
# WARNING
# ACTION REQUIRED
# CRITICAL
#
# CEO notifications should prioritise:
#
# - financial risk
# - major operational disruption
# - security incidents
# - compliance problems
# - important customer problems
# - important supplier problems
# - decisions requiring CEO approval

# ============================================================
# 25.15 DASHBOARD DESIGN
# ============================================================

# Platformsify CEO Dashboard should provide:
#
# EXECUTIVE OVERVIEW
# COMMERCIAL
# TRADE
# LOGISTICS
# ENERGY
# FINANCE
# AI OPERATIONS
# RISKS
# APPROVALS
# COMPLIANCE
# SETTINGS
#
# The CEO should be able to understand the entire business
# without opening multiple management systems.

# ============================================================
# 25.16 ROLE-BASED DASHBOARDS
# ============================================================

# Operational views may be customised by role.
#
# CEO:
# full executive visibility
#
# COMMERCIAL:
# customers, opportunities, quotes
#
# LOGISTICS:
# shipments, routes, exceptions
#
# FINANCE:
# financial records and reporting
#
# ENERGY:
# projects and opportunities
#
# COMPLIANCE:
# documentation and risk
#
# AI OPERATIONS:
# agents, workflows, errors
#
# These are role views, NOT separate executive platforms.

# ============================================================
# 25.17 AUTHENTICATION
# ============================================================

# Production authentication should support:
#
# - secure passwords where applicable
# - multi-factor authentication
# - session management
# - account recovery
# - device/session visibility
# - account lockout/risk controls
#
# CEO access should receive the strongest available protection.

# ============================================================
# 25.18 AUTHORISATION
# ============================================================

# Every sensitive API request must verify:
#
# WHO
# WHAT
# WHICH RESOURCE
# WHICH ACTION
# WHICH PERMISSION
#
# Never rely solely on front-end restrictions.
#
# Authorisation must be enforced server-side.

# ============================================================
# 25.19 SECRET MANAGEMENT
# ============================================================

# Secrets include:
#
# - API keys
# - database credentials
# - payment credentials
# - authentication secrets
# - encryption keys
# - service credentials
#
# Rules:
#
# NEVER commit secrets to Git.
# NEVER place production secrets in public source.
# NEVER expose secrets to browser clients unnecessarily.
#
# Production secrets should use secure environment/secret
# management.

# ============================================================
# 25.20 ENVIRONMENT SEPARATION
# ============================================================

# Maintain separate environments:
#
# DEVELOPMENT
# STAGING
# PRODUCTION
#
# Development data must not accidentally become production data.
#
# Production credentials must never be used casually in local
# development.

# ============================================================
# 25.21 GIT & SOURCE CONTROL
# ============================================================

# All production code must be version controlled.
#
# Rules:
#
# - meaningful commits
# - protected main branch where appropriate
# - pull-request review for critical changes
# - no secrets
# - tagged releases
# - rollback capability
#
# Production deployment should reference a known commit/version.

# ============================================================
# 25.22 CI/CD
# ============================================================

# Deployment pipeline:
#
# CODE
# ↓
# LINT
# ↓
# TEST
# ↓
# BUILD
# ↓
# SECURITY CHECK
# ↓
# STAGING
# ↓
# SMOKE TEST
# ↓
# PRODUCTION
#
# Failed critical checks should prevent deployment.

# ============================================================
# 25.23 PRODUCTION DEPLOYMENT
# ============================================================

# Production infrastructure should provide:
#
# - HTTPS
# - secure DNS
# - CDN where appropriate
# - API protection
# - monitoring
# - logs
# - backups
# - rollback
#
# Cloudflare may be used as the edge/network/security layer
# where appropriate.
#
# Deployment configuration must remain reproducible.

# ============================================================
# 25.24 PERFORMANCE
# ============================================================

# Monitor:
#
# - response time
# - API latency
# - database latency
# - AI response time
# - queue time
# - error rate
# - uptime
#
# Performance problems should generate operational alerts.

# ============================================================
# 25.25 OBSERVABILITY
# ============================================================

# Production monitoring should cover:
#
# APPLICATION
# DATABASE
# API
# AI
# SECURITY
# INFRASTRUCTURE
# PAYMENTS
# INTEGRATIONS
#
# Each critical service should have health checks.

# ============================================================
# 25.26 ERROR MANAGEMENT
# ============================================================

# Errors should be:
#
# - captured
# - classified
# - logged
# - correlated
# - monitored
# - escalated where required
#
# Users should receive safe error messages.
#
# Sensitive internal details must not be exposed unnecessarily.

# ============================================================
# 25.27 AI FAILURE HANDLING
# ============================================================

# If an AI service fails:
#
# 1. Detect failure.
# 2. Stop unsafe execution.
# 3. Retry only where safe.
# 4. Log failure.
# 5. Escalate where required.
# 6. Use human/manual fallback where available.
#
# AI failure must never silently create a false successful
# transaction.

# ============================================================
# 25.28 BACKUP STRATEGY
# ============================================================

# Critical data should be backed up regularly.
#
# Backups should be:
#
# - encrypted
# - access-controlled
# - monitored
# - tested
# - recoverable
#
# Backup existence alone is not sufficient.
#
# Recovery must be periodically tested.

# ============================================================
# 25.29 DISASTER RECOVERY
# ============================================================

# Maintain documented procedures for:
#
# - database failure
# - cloud outage
# - AI provider outage
# - payment provider outage
# - security incident
# - accidental deletion
# - deployment failure
# - credential compromise
#
# Define:
#
# RTO = target recovery time
# RPO = acceptable data loss window
#
# Exact targets should be established according to business
# criticality and cost.

# ============================================================
# 25.30 SECURITY INCIDENT RESPONSE
# ============================================================

# If a serious security event occurs:
#
# DETECT
# ↓
# CONTAIN
# ↓
# ASSESS
# ↓
# PROTECT
# ↓
# RECOVER
# ↓
# REVIEW
#
# Appropriate legal/regulatory notification must be considered
# where required.

# ============================================================
# 25.31 THIRD-PARTY INTEGRATIONS
# ============================================================

# Every external integration should have:
#
# - documented purpose
# - owner
# - credentials
# - permissions
# - data exchanged
# - failure behaviour
# - monitoring
# - replacement/fallback plan
#
# Avoid unnecessary vendor lock-in.

# ============================================================
# 25.32 PAYMENT INTEGRATION
# ============================================================

# Payment systems should be integrated through secure,
# approved providers.
#
# The platform must:
#
# - verify payment status
# - record transaction references
# - reconcile payments
# - detect failures
# - log events
#
# Never store sensitive payment credentials unnecessarily.

# ============================================================
# 25.33 DOCUMENT STORAGE
# ============================================================

# Business documents should have:
#
# - unique identifier
# - document type
# - owner
# - linked transaction
# - creation date
# - version
# - status
# - access permissions
#
# Important documents should not depend on a single person's
# local computer.

# ============================================================
# 25.34 DATA RETENTION
# ============================================================

# Data retention policies must consider:
#
# - legal requirements
# - contractual requirements
# - accounting requirements
# - privacy requirements
# - operational requirements
#
# Data should not be retained indefinitely without purpose.

# ============================================================
# 25.35 PRIVACY
# ============================================================

# The system should apply privacy-by-design principles.
#
# Collect only information required for legitimate business
# purposes.
#
# Restrict access.
#
# Protect personal information.
#
# Provide appropriate retention and deletion procedures.
#
# Applicable privacy laws must be assessed before launch.

# ============================================================
# 25.36 TESTING
# ============================================================

# Required testing categories:
#
# - unit tests
# - integration tests
# - API tests
# - authentication tests
# - authorisation tests
# - workflow tests
# - AI evaluation
# - security testing
# - performance testing
# - end-to-end testing
#
# Critical business workflows require automated regression
# testing where practical.

# ============================================================
# 25.37 AI EVALUATION
# ============================================================

# AI systems must be tested against representative scenarios.
#
# Evaluate:
#
# - accuracy
# - consistency
# - hallucination rate
# - policy compliance
# - tool-use correctness
# - escalation behaviour
# - financial calculation accuracy
# - document accuracy
#
# AI should fail safely.

# ============================================================
# 25.38 PRODUCTION READINESS CHECK
# ============================================================

# BEFORE PRODUCTION:
#
# [ ] Build succeeds
# [ ] Tests pass
# [ ] Environment variables verified
# [ ] Secrets secured
# [ ] Authentication works
# [ ] Authorisation works
# [ ] Database secured
# [ ] Backups configured
# [ ] Monitoring configured
# [ ] Error handling verified
# [ ] AI permissions verified
# [ ] CEO approval gates verified
# [ ] Audit logging verified
# [ ] Payment workflows tested
# [ ] Rollback tested
# [ ] Critical routes tested
# [ ] HTTPS verified
# [ ] Production smoke test passed

# ============================================================
# 25.39 ARCHITECTURAL RULE
# ============================================================

# NEVER BUILD TECHNOLOGY FOR TECHNOLOGY'S SAKE.
#
# Every component must answer:
#
# 1. What business problem does this solve?
# 2. Who uses it?
# 3. What data does it require?
# 4. What permission does it need?
# 5. What happens if it fails?
# 6. How is it monitored?
# 7. Can it be replaced?
#
# If the answer is unclear, do not deploy it.

# ============================================================
# 25.40 FINAL TECHNOLOGY PRINCIPLE
# ============================================================

# TriLink Global should feel like one integrated company,
# not a collection of disconnected applications.
#
# Platformsify provides the technology operating layer.
#
# The Platformsify CEO Dashboard provides executive control.
#
# AI provides scalable operational intelligence.
#
# Secure APIs connect the business systems.
#
# Reliable data powers decisions.
#
# Human authority remains above automated execution.
#
# ============================================================
# END OF PART 25
# ============================================================


# ============================================================
# PART 26 — AI OPERATIONS, CONTROL, SECURITY & EXECUTION
# ============================================================

# 26.1 SINGLE EXECUTIVE CONTROL CENTRE
#
# Platformsify CEO Dashboard is the SINGLE executive control
# centre for TriLink Global.
#
# The dashboard must provide the Founder / Chairman / CEO with
# one unified view of:
#
# - company performance
# - revenue
# - costs
# - cash position
# - trade activity
# - logistics activity
# - energy activity
# - customers
# - suppliers
# - shipments
# - orders
# - invoices
# - payments
# - contracts
# - risks
# - compliance
# - AI activity
# - alerts
# - approvals
# - strategic KPIs
#
# No separate CEO dashboard is to be created.
#
# ============================================================
# 26.2 AI OPERATING MODEL
# ============================================================
#
# AI acts as the operational workforce for approved workflows.
#
# AI responsibilities include:
#
# - lead qualification
# - customer communication
# - supplier communication
# - quotation preparation
# - document preparation
# - shipment coordination
# - logistics updates
# - invoice preparation
# - payment reconciliation
# - market intelligence
# - pricing analysis
# - risk detection
# - compliance checks
# - reporting
# - forecasting
# - KPI monitoring
# - operational scheduling
# - exception detection
#
# AI must never silently bypass permissions.
#
# ============================================================
# 26.3 HUMAN APPROVAL GATES
# ============================================================
#
# The following require Founder / Chairman / CEO approval:
#
# - major contracts
# - major financial commitments
# - bank account changes
# - payment destination changes
# - new corporate entities
# - new country expansion
# - high-value transactions
# - high-risk suppliers
# - high-risk customers
# - regulatory exceptions
# - legal settlements
# - strategic partnerships
# - senior executive appointments
# - changes to AI permissions
#
# AI prepares the decision.
# CEO makes the final decision.
#
# ============================================================
# 26.4 AI AUDIT TRAIL
# ============================================================
#
# Every material AI action must be traceable.
#
# Record:
#
# - timestamp
# - AI workflow
# - user/system initiating event
# - input
# - decision
# - action
# - affected customer/supplier/order
# - financial impact
# - approval status
# - human approver where required
# - resulting system state
#
# Audit records must be tamper-resistant and retained according
# to the applicable legal and regulatory requirements.
#
# ============================================================
# 26.5 SECURITY
# ============================================================
#
# Security-by-design is mandatory.
#
# Required controls:
#
# - role-based access control
# - least-privilege permissions
# - secure authentication
# - protected secrets
# - encrypted sensitive data
# - secure API access
# - environment separation
# - audit logging
# - rate limiting
# - input validation
# - fraud detection
# - suspicious activity alerts
# - backup and recovery procedures
#
# AI must not expose secrets, credentials, payment information,
# private keys or authentication tokens.
#
# ============================================================
# 26.6 FINANCIAL CONTROL
# ============================================================
#
# AI may prepare financial operations but financial authority
# remains with the Founder / Chairman / CEO and authorised
# financial controls.
#
# The system must track:
#
# - sales
# - purchases
# - invoices
# - receivables
# - payables
# - margins
# - transaction fees
# - logistics costs
# - energy costs
# - taxes
# - cash flow
# - forecasts
#
# Financial anomalies must automatically generate CEO alerts.
#
# ============================================================
# 26.7 CUSTOMER OPERATIONS
# ============================================================
#
# AI manages the customer lifecycle:
#
# LEAD
#   ↓
# QUALIFICATION
#   ↓
# REQUIREMENT
#   ↓
# QUOTE
#   ↓
# NEGOTIATION
#   ↓
# CONTRACT
#   ↓
# ORDER
#   ↓
# FULFILMENT
#   ↓
# DELIVERY
#   ↓
# INVOICE
#   ↓
# PAYMENT
#   ↓
# AFTER-SALES
#
# Every stage must be visible from the Platformsify CEO Dashboard.
#
# ============================================================
# 26.8 SUPPLIER OPERATIONS
# ============================================================
#
# AI manages approved supplier workflows:
#
# - supplier discovery
# - supplier onboarding
# - documentation collection
# - quotation requests
# - price comparison
# - availability checks
# - compliance checks
# - purchase coordination
# - delivery monitoring
# - supplier performance
#
# High-risk or strategic supplier decisions require CEO approval.
#
# ============================================================
# 26.9 LOGISTICS INTELLIGENCE
# ============================================================
#
# The system must maintain visibility over:
#
# - origin
# - destination
# - route
# - carrier
# - shipment status
# - expected delivery
# - customs status
# - documentation
# - freight cost
# - exceptions
# - delays
#
# AI should identify delays and exceptions before they become
# major customer problems.
#
# ============================================================
# 26.10 ENERGY OPERATIONS
# ============================================================
#
# TriLink Energy must operate through the same executive control
# architecture.
#
# AI may support:
#
# - energy market intelligence
# - supplier research
# - buyer research
# - project pipeline
# - pricing analysis
# - contract preparation
# - project monitoring
# - risk analysis
# - reporting
#
# Energy transactions remain subject to applicable licensing,
# regulatory, contractual and financial requirements.
#
# ============================================================
# 26.11 CEO ALERT SYSTEM
# ============================================================
#
# The CEO Dashboard must prioritise alerts by severity.
#
# CRITICAL:
# Immediate Founder / CEO attention.
#
# HIGH:
# Requires executive review.
#
# MEDIUM:
# Operational review required.
#
# LOW:
# Informational.
#
# AI should avoid unnecessary alerts and prioritise issues that
# materially affect money, customers, compliance, security,
# reputation or strategic objectives.
#
# ============================================================
# 26.12 KPI SYSTEM
# ============================================================
#
# Core executive KPIs:
#
# - revenue
# - gross margin
# - net margin
# - cash position
# - receivables
# - payables
# - active customers
# - active suppliers
# - orders
# - shipments
# - delivery performance
# - customer acquisition
# - customer retention
# - conversion rate
# - average transaction value
# - operational costs
# - AI automation rate
# - exception rate
# - compliance status
#
# KPIs must be available through the Platformsify CEO Dashboard.
#
# ============================================================
# 26.13 AI PERFORMANCE
# ============================================================
#
# AI itself must be measured.
#
# Track:
#
# - successful workflows
# - failed workflows
# - human escalations
# - approval frequency
# - processing time
# - cost per workflow
# - error rate
# - customer response time
# - automation percentage
#
# AI performance must never be hidden from the CEO.
#
# ============================================================
# 26.14 EXCEPTION-FIRST MANAGEMENT
# ============================================================
#
# The CEO should not need to manually inspect every operation.
#
# Normal approved workflows should run automatically.
#
# The dashboard should surface:
#
# - exceptions
# - risks
# - unusual transactions
# - delays
# - missed targets
# - compliance issues
# - financial anomalies
# - customer complaints
# - supplier failures
#
# This creates an AI-first exception-management business model.
#
# ============================================================
# 26.15 BUSINESS CONTINUITY
# ============================================================
#
# TriLink must maintain:
#
# - backups
# - recovery procedures
# - operational logs
# - documented workflows
# - failure alerts
# - service monitoring
# - recovery testing
#
# Critical operations must have documented recovery procedures.
#
# ============================================================
# 26.16 GOVERNANCE PRINCIPLE
# ============================================================
#
# AI operates.
# Platformsify orchestrates.
# TriLink executes.
# The Founder / Chairman / CEO governs.
#
# FINAL AUTHORITY:
# MEHERAJ UDDIN
#
# ============================================================
# END OF PART 26
# ============================================================


# ============================================================
# PART 27 — AI SALES, CRM & REVENUE ENGINE
# ============================================================

# 27.1 PURPOSE
# ============================================================
#
# TriLink must have an AI-first commercial engine capable of
# identifying opportunities, qualifying prospects, preparing
# offers, managing relationships and tracking revenue.
#
# Platformsify CEO Dashboard remains the SINGLE executive
# control centre.
#
# No separate CEO CRM dashboard is permitted.
#
# ============================================================
# 27.2 AI SALES PIPELINE
# ============================================================
#
# Every commercial opportunity follows:
#
# DISCOVER
#   ↓
# IDENTIFY
#   ↓
# QUALIFY
#   ↓
# VERIFY
#   ↓
# UNDERSTAND REQUIREMENT
#   ↓
# SOURCE
#   ↓
# PRICE
#   ↓
# QUOTE
#   ↓
# NEGOTIATE
#   ↓
# APPROVE
#   ↓
# CONTRACT
#   ↓
# ORDER
#   ↓
# FULFIL
#   ↓
# INVOICE
#   ↓
# COLLECT PAYMENT
#   ↓
# RETAIN
#   ↓
# EXPAND
#
# ============================================================
# 27.3 AI LEAD DISCOVERY
# ============================================================
#
# AI may identify potential:
#
# - importers
# - exporters
# - distributors
# - wholesalers
# - retailers
# - manufacturers
# - logistics customers
# - energy customers
# - institutional buyers
# - strategic partners
#
# AI must prioritise legitimate commercial opportunities and
# respect applicable privacy, marketing and data-protection laws.
#
# ============================================================
# 27.4 LEAD SCORING
# ============================================================
#
# Each lead receives an AI-generated priority score based on:
#
# - business fit
# - geography
# - product requirement
# - estimated transaction value
# - purchasing capacity
# - urgency
# - reliability
# - compliance risk
# - historical interaction
# - probability of conversion
#
# Example:
#
# A — HIGH PRIORITY
# B — QUALIFIED
# C — DEVELOPING
# D — LOW PRIORITY
# E — DO NOT PURSUE
#
# CEO can override the AI classification.
#
# ============================================================
# 27.5 CUSTOMER PROFILE
# ============================================================
#
# Every approved customer record should contain:
#
# - company identity
# - country
# - industry
# - contact information
# - buyer role
# - requirements
# - transaction history
# - quotes
# - contracts
# - orders
# - invoices
# - payments
# - communication history
# - risk status
# - compliance status
# - relationship status
#
# Sensitive information must be handled according to applicable
# data-protection requirements.
#
# ============================================================
# 27.6 AI CUSTOMER COMMUNICATION
# ============================================================
#
# AI may prepare and, where permitted, send routine business
# communications including:
#
# - introductions
# - product information
# - quotation follow-ups
# - shipment updates
# - invoice reminders
# - order confirmations
# - customer-service responses
# - meeting scheduling
# - satisfaction follow-ups
#
# AI must not make unauthorised legal, financial or contractual
# commitments.
#
# ============================================================
# 27.7 AI QUOTATION ENGINE
# ============================================================
#
# AI prepares quotations using approved:
#
# - supplier prices
# - freight costs
# - customs-related costs
# - operating costs
# - transaction fees
# - target margins
# - currency rates
# - customer requirements
#
# The quotation must show:
#
# - product/service
# - quantity
# - unit price
# - total price
# - currency
# - delivery terms
# - estimated delivery
# - validity period
# - applicable conditions
#
# Pricing outside approved rules requires CEO approval.
#
# ============================================================
# 27.8 MARGIN PROTECTION
# ============================================================
#
# AI must protect minimum approved margins.
#
# Before a quotation is issued:
#
# REVENUE
# minus
# PRODUCT COST
# minus
# LOGISTICS COST
# minus
# TRANSACTION COST
# minus
# OTHER APPROVED COSTS
# equals
# EXPECTED CONTRIBUTION
#
# If the expected margin falls below the approved threshold:
#
# → STOP
# → FLAG
# → REQUEST APPROVAL
#
# AI must never knowingly create an unapproved loss-making deal.
#
# ============================================================
# 27.9 NEGOTIATION SUPPORT
# ============================================================
#
# AI may analyse:
#
# - customer requests
# - competing offers
# - supplier pricing
# - historical pricing
# - target margin
# - volume discounts
# - delivery requirements
#
# AI can recommend:
#
# - opening price
# - target price
# - minimum approved price
# - alternative products
# - alternative logistics
# - volume incentives
#
# Final authority remains with the Founder / Chairman / CEO or
# an authorised delegate.
#
# ============================================================
# 27.10 CONTRACT WORKFLOW
# ============================================================
#
# AI prepares contract drafts from approved templates.
#
# Workflow:
#
# CUSTOMER REQUIREMENT
#        ↓
# COMMERCIAL TERMS
#        ↓
# AI CONTRACT DRAFT
#        ↓
# COMPLIANCE CHECK
#        ↓
# RISK CHECK
#        ↓
# HUMAN APPROVAL
#        ↓
# SIGNATURE
#        ↓
# CONTRACT STORAGE
#        ↓
# CONTRACT MONITORING
#
# AI must never represent an unsigned draft as an executed
# contract.
#
# ============================================================
# 27.11 CUSTOMER RISK
# ============================================================
#
# AI should identify:
#
# - inconsistent company information
# - unusual payment requests
# - suspicious changes
# - high-risk jurisdictions
# - sanctions/compliance concerns
# - unusual transaction patterns
# - identity inconsistencies
# - excessive credit exposure
#
# Risk flags must be escalated according to the compliance
# policy.
#
# ============================================================
# 27.12 CUSTOMER CREDIT CONTROL
# ============================================================
#
# Where credit is offered, the system must track:
#
# - approved credit limit
# - outstanding balance
# - overdue invoices
# - payment history
# - exposure
# - risk rating
#
# AI must alert the CEO when exposure exceeds approved limits.
#
# ============================================================
# 27.13 REVENUE FORECASTING
# ============================================================
#
# AI forecasts:
#
# - pipeline revenue
# - expected orders
# - contracted revenue
# - recurring revenue
# - expected collections
# - gross margin
# - cash requirements
#
# Forecasts must distinguish between:
#
# CONFIRMED
# PROBABLE
# POSSIBLE
# SPECULATIVE
#
# AI must never present speculative revenue as guaranteed revenue.
#
# ============================================================
# 27.14 CUSTOMER RETENTION
# ============================================================
#
# After successful delivery, AI should monitor:
#
# - repeat purchase probability
# - customer satisfaction
# - unresolved issues
# - reorder timing
# - cross-sell opportunities
# - contract renewal dates
#
# The objective is to turn individual transactions into
# long-term commercial relationships.
#
# ============================================================
# 27.15 AI CROSS-SELLING
# ============================================================
#
# Where commercially appropriate, AI may recommend:
#
# - additional products
# - logistics services
# - sourcing services
# - trade services
# - energy opportunities
# - repeat orders
# - bundled services
#
# Recommendations must be relevant to the customer's actual
# business requirements.
#
# ============================================================
# 27.16 SALES PERFORMANCE
# ============================================================
#
# CEO Dashboard KPIs:
#
# - leads
# - qualified leads
# - active opportunities
# - quotations
# - quotation value
# - conversion rate
# - average deal value
# - gross margin
# - sales cycle
# - new customers
# - repeat customers
# - lost opportunities
# - pipeline value
# - forecast revenue
#
# ============================================================
# 27.17 LOST DEAL INTELLIGENCE
# ============================================================
#
# When a deal is lost, AI records:
#
# - reason
# - competitor
# - price issue
# - product issue
# - delivery issue
# - timing
# - customer decision
# - compliance issue
# - other relevant reason
#
# AI identifies recurring patterns and recommends improvements.
#
# ============================================================
# 27.18 REVENUE ALERTS
# ============================================================
#
# CEO alerts should include:
#
# - major new opportunity
# - unusually large deal
# - margin below target
# - major customer lost
# - major customer at risk
# - overdue high-value invoice
# - pipeline falling below target
# - sudden conversion-rate decline
# - abnormal pricing activity
#
# ============================================================
# 27.19 COMMERCIAL AUTOMATION PRINCIPLE
# ============================================================
#
# The objective is:
#
# FEWER MANUAL TASKS
# + FASTER RESPONSE
# + BETTER INFORMATION
# + BETTER MARGINS
# + BETTER CUSTOMER EXPERIENCE
# + STRONGER CONTROL
#
# AI handles repeatable work.
#
# Humans handle judgement, accountability and high-impact
# decisions.
#
# ============================================================
# 27.20 CEO COMMAND MODEL
# ============================================================
#
# The Founder / Chairman / CEO should be able to see:
#
# "What is happening?"
# "What needs my attention?"
# "What is making money?"
# "What is losing money?"
# "Which customers matter?"
# "Which suppliers are risky?"
# "What is likely to happen next?"
# "What decisions require my approval?"
#
# The Platformsify CEO Dashboard must answer these questions
# without requiring the CEO to inspect individual systems.
#
# ============================================================
# 27.21 FINAL COMMERCIAL PRINCIPLE
# ============================================================
#
# AI finds opportunities.
# AI qualifies opportunities.
# AI prepares commercial work.
# AI protects approved margins.
# AI monitors customers.
# AI forecasts revenue.
#
# Platformsify orchestrates the workflow.
#
# TriLink executes the transaction.
#
# Founder / Chairman / CEO retains final authority.
#
# ============================================================
# END OF PART 27
# ============================================================


# ============================================================
# PART 28 — AI FINANCE, ACCOUNTING & TREASURY ENGINE
# ============================================================

# 28.1 PURPOSE
# ============================================================
#
# TriLink requires a central AI-assisted financial operating
# system covering revenue, costs, invoices, payments, cash,
# margins, forecasting, reconciliation and financial risk.
#
# Platformsify CEO Dashboard remains the SINGLE executive
# financial control centre.
#
# No separate CEO finance dashboard is permitted.
#
# ============================================================
# 28.2 FINANCIAL COMMAND CENTRE
# ============================================================
#
# The CEO Dashboard must provide a consolidated view of:
#
# - cash
# - revenue
# - expenses
# - gross profit
# - net profit
# - receivables
# - payables
# - invoices
# - overdue balances
# - transaction pipeline
# - committed expenditure
# - forecasts
# - financial risks
# - approved budgets
#
# ============================================================
# 28.3 AI BOOKKEEPING SUPPORT
# ============================================================
#
# AI may assist with:
#
# - transaction categorisation
# - invoice data extraction
# - purchase matching
# - expense classification
# - reconciliation preparation
# - payment matching
# - financial summaries
# - anomaly detection
# - management reporting
#
# AI-generated accounting records must remain reviewable and
# auditable.
#
# ============================================================
# 28.4 ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE
# ============================================================
#
# The system tracks:
#
# - issued invoices
# - invoice dates
# - due dates
# - amounts
# - currencies
# - payment status
# - overdue days
# - customer exposure
#
# AI may automatically:
#
# - identify overdue invoices
# - prepare reminders
# - prioritise collection
# - forecast collections
# - flag high-risk receivables
#
# Material disputes or sensitive collection decisions require
# human review.
#
# ============================================================
# 28.5 ACCOUNTS PAYABLE
# ============================================================
#
# The system tracks:
#
# - supplier invoices
# - purchase orders
# - payment terms
# - due dates
# - amounts
# - currencies
# - approval status
# - payment status
#
# AI must compare:
#
# PURCHASE ORDER
# +
# GOODS/SERVICE RECEIPT
# +
# SUPPLIER INVOICE
#
# before recommending payment.
#
# ============================================================
# 28.6 PAYMENT CONTROL
# ============================================================
#
# AI may prepare payment instructions.
#
# AI must NOT independently change:
#
# - bank account ownership
# - payment destinations
# - beneficiary details
# - payment limits
# - authorised signatories
#
# High-value payments require the appropriate human approval.
#
# ============================================================
# 28.7 PAYMENT FRAUD PROTECTION
# ============================================================
#
# AI must identify suspicious payment patterns including:
#
# - sudden beneficiary changes
# - duplicate invoices
# - unusual amounts
# - unusual currencies
# - unusual payment timing
# - mismatched supplier information
# - unexpected bank-detail changes
# - duplicate payment requests
#
# Suspicious transactions must be stopped or held according to
# the configured risk policy.
#
# ============================================================
# 28.8 CASH-FLOW MANAGEMENT
# ============================================================
#
# AI continuously analyses:
#
# CASH AVAILABLE
# +
# EXPECTED COLLECTIONS
# -
# EXPECTED PAYMENTS
# -
# COMMITTED COSTS
# =
# PROJECTED CASH POSITION
#
# Forecast periods:
#
# - 7 days
# - 30 days
# - 90 days
# - 6 months
# - 12 months
#
# ============================================================
# 28.9 CASH-FLOW ALERTS
# ============================================================
#
# CEO alerts must be generated for:
#
# - projected cash shortfall
# - unusually high expenditure
# - delayed collections
# - major unexpected cost
# - liquidity risk
# - large unapproved commitment
# - unusual transaction activity
#
# ============================================================
# 28.10 MULTI-CURRENCY CONTROL
# ============================================================
#
# Because TriLink operates internationally, the financial
# system must support multiple currencies.
#
# Track:
#
# - transaction currency
# - reporting currency
# - exchange rate
# - exchange-rate source
# - conversion value
# - currency exposure
#
# AI should identify significant foreign-exchange exposure.
#
# ============================================================
# 28.11 PROFITABILITY ENGINE
# ============================================================
#
# Profitability must be measurable by:
#
# - customer
# - product
# - service
# - transaction
# - route
# - supplier
# - country
# - business division
#
# Core calculation:
#
# REVENUE
# -
# DIRECT COSTS
# -
# LOGISTICS COSTS
# -
# TRANSACTION COSTS
# -
# OTHER APPROVED COSTS
# =
# CONTRIBUTION
#
# ============================================================
# 28.12 CUSTOMER PROFITABILITY
# ============================================================
#
# AI identifies:
#
# - highest-value customers
# - highest-margin customers
# - low-margin customers
# - loss-making relationships
# - customers with high service costs
# - customers with high payment risk
#
# CEO receives strategic recommendations.
#
# ============================================================
# 28.13 SUPPLIER COST INTELLIGENCE
# ============================================================
#
# AI compares suppliers using:
#
# - purchase price
# - quality
# - reliability
# - delivery performance
# - payment terms
# - minimum order quantity
# - logistics impact
# - historical performance
#
# AI recommends commercially stronger supplier options.
#
# ============================================================
# 28.14 BUDGETING
# ============================================================
#
# TriLink must maintain budgets for:
#
# - operations
# - sales
# - marketing
# - technology
# - logistics
# - energy projects
# - staffing
# - professional services
# - expansion
#
# AI compares:
#
# ACTUAL
# vs
# BUDGET
# vs
# FORECAST
#
# ============================================================
# 28.15 VARIANCE ANALYSIS
# ============================================================
#
# AI automatically identifies significant differences between
# budget and actual performance.
#
# Examples:
#
# - revenue below target
# - logistics cost above target
# - supplier cost increase
# - marketing overspend
# - unexpected operational cost
# - margin deterioration
#
# Each major variance should include:
#
# WHAT HAPPENED
# WHY IT HAPPENED
# FINANCIAL IMPACT
# RECOMMENDED ACTION
#
# ============================================================
# 28.16 TAX & COMPLIANCE SUPPORT
# ============================================================
#
# AI may organise and prepare information needed for tax and
# statutory reporting.
#
# AI must not replace qualified professional advice where
# required.
#
# The system should track:
#
# - tax-related transactions
# - invoices
# - supporting documents
# - applicable reporting deadlines
# - relevant jurisdictions
# - required records
#
# ============================================================
# 28.17 FINANCIAL DOCUMENT CONTROL
# ============================================================
#
# Store and associate:
#
# - invoices
# - receipts
# - purchase orders
# - contracts
# - payment confirmations
# - credit notes
# - statements
# - financial reports
#
# Documents must be linked to the relevant transaction.
#
# ============================================================
# 28.18 FINANCIAL CLOSE
# ============================================================
#
# AI assists with the financial close process:
#
# 1. Collect transactions
# 2. Match invoices
# 3. Reconcile accounts
# 4. Identify missing documents
# 5. Identify anomalies
# 6. Calculate management metrics
# 7. Prepare reports
# 8. Flag unresolved items
# 9. Human review
# 10. Finalisation
#
# ============================================================
# 28.19 CEO FINANCIAL REPORT
# ============================================================
#
# The CEO Dashboard should provide:
#
# DAILY:
# - cash position
# - urgent payments
# - major transactions
# - critical alerts
#
# WEEKLY:
# - revenue
# - margin
# - collections
# - expenditure
# - pipeline
#
# MONTHLY:
# - profit and loss summary
# - cash-flow summary
# - balance-sheet indicators
# - budget variance
# - customer profitability
# - supplier costs
#
# ============================================================
# 28.20 FINANCIAL SCENARIO ENGINE
# ============================================================
#
# AI should support scenarios such as:
#
# BASE CASE
# UPSIDE
# DOWNSIDE
# STRESS CASE
#
# Analyse effects on:
#
# - revenue
# - margins
# - cash
# - working capital
# - operational capacity
# - expansion plans
#
# ============================================================
# 28.21 INVESTMENT & EXPANSION ANALYSIS
# ============================================================
#
# Before major expansion, AI prepares:
#
# - estimated investment
# - expected revenue
# - expected costs
# - expected margin
# - payback analysis
# - key risks
# - regulatory considerations
# - scenario analysis
#
# CEO makes the final investment decision.
#
# ============================================================
# 28.22 FINANCIAL RISK SCORE
# ============================================================
#
# AI maintains an internal financial-risk view based on:
#
# - liquidity
# - customer concentration
# - supplier concentration
# - credit exposure
# - currency exposure
# - margin pressure
# - overdue receivables
# - unexpected expenditure
#
# High-risk conditions trigger CEO review.
#
# ============================================================
# 28.23 FINANCIAL DATA PRINCIPLE
# ============================================================
#
# Every material financial number must have:
#
# SOURCE
# TIME
# CURRENCY
# STATUS
# OWNER
# TRACEABILITY
#
# AI must distinguish:
#
# ACTUAL
# ESTIMATE
# FORECAST
# SCENARIO
#
# ============================================================
# 28.24 FINANCE AUTOMATION PRINCIPLE
# ============================================================
#
# AI prepares.
# AI reconciles.
# AI analyses.
# AI forecasts.
# AI detects anomalies.
#
# Authorised humans approve material financial decisions.
#
# ============================================================
# 28.25 FINAL FINANCIAL CONTROL PRINCIPLE
# ============================================================
#
# Platformsify provides the financial intelligence layer.
#
# The TriLink CEO Dashboard provides executive visibility.
#
# AI manages approved financial workflows.
#
# Human authority remains responsible for material financial
# decisions and statutory accountability.
#
# ============================================================
# END OF PART 28
# ============================================================


# ============================================================
# PART 29 — AI GOVERNANCE, SAFETY, AUDIT & HUMAN CONTROL
# ============================================================
#
# PURPOSE
# This section defines how TriLink's AI-operated business remains
# controlled, auditable, secure and commercially responsible.
#
# 29.1 — SINGLE AI OPERATING MODEL
#
# Platformsify CEO Dashboard is the single executive technology
# control centre for TriLink.
#
# All approved AI agents, workflows, alerts, reports and operational
# automations must report through the TriLink CEO Dashboard.
#
# Do not build an independent competing CEO dashboard.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 29.2 — AI AGENT STRUCTURE
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# The system may contain specialised AI agents operating under the
# CEO Dashboard.
#
# CORE AGENTS:
#
# 1. CEO Intelligence Agent
#    - executive summaries
#    - KPI monitoring
#    - strategic alerts
#    - decision support
#
# 2. Trade Agent
#    - buyer/supplier discovery
#    - quotation preparation
#    - trade documentation support
#    - trade opportunity analysis
#
# 3. Logistics Agent
#    - shipment coordination
#    - route analysis
#    - delivery monitoring
#    - logistics exception alerts
#
# 4. Energy Agent
#    - energy opportunity discovery
#    - supplier/project research
#    - commercial analysis
#    - project pipeline management
#
# 5. Finance Agent
#    - revenue monitoring
#    - cost analysis
#    - invoice tracking
#    - cash-flow reporting
#    - financial anomaly alerts
#
# 6. Compliance Agent
#    - document checks
#    - regulatory workflow support
#    - sanctions/compliance screening support
#    - risk alerts
#
# 7. Customer Agent
#    - enquiries
#    - CRM updates
#    - customer communication drafts
#    - follow-up management
#
# 8. Supplier Agent
#    - supplier research
#    - supplier comparison
#    - quotation collection
#    - supplier performance tracking
#
# 9. Marketing Agent
#    - content planning
#    - campaign preparation
#    - market research
#    - lead generation support
#
# 10. Security Agent
#     - suspicious activity detection
#     - access monitoring
#     - security alerts
#     - incident escalation
#
# All agents operate within permissions defined by the CEO Dashboard.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 29.3 — AI PERMISSION LEVELS
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# LEVEL 0 — OBSERVE
# AI can read approved information and produce analysis.
#
# LEVEL 1 — RECOMMEND
# AI can recommend an action but cannot execute it.
#
# LEVEL 2 — DRAFT
# AI can prepare emails, documents, quotations and workflows for
# approval.
#
# LEVEL 3 — CONTROLLED EXECUTION
# AI may execute predefined low-risk operational tasks.
#
# LEVEL 4 — EXECUTIVE APPROVAL
# Human approval is mandatory before execution.
#
# LEVEL 5 — FOUNDER ONLY
# Only Founder / Chairman / CEO can authorise the action.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 29.4 — MANDATORY HUMAN APPROVAL
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# AI MUST NOT independently approve or execute:
#
# - major bank changes
# - ownership changes
# - share transfers
# - major borrowing
# - major investments
# - major contracts
# - legally binding strategic agreements
# - acquisition of another company
# - disposal of major company assets
# - changes to corporate control
# - high-value payments above configured limits
# - new-country expansion
# - high-risk suppliers
# - high-risk customers
# - regulatory submissions requiring director approval
# - employment decisions involving senior executives
# - changes to AI authority itself
#
# These actions must be escalated to the Founder / Chairman / CEO.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 29.5 — AI DECISION LOG
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# Every material AI action must generate an audit record containing:
#
# - timestamp
# - agent identity
# - user/request identity
# - action requested
# - information used
# - decision/recommendation
# - confidence where available
# - permission level
# - approval status
# - final action
# - resulting system state
# - exception/error information
#
# Audit records must be tamper-resistant and retained according to
# applicable legal and business requirements.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 29.6 — EXECUTIVE ALERT SYSTEM
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# CEO Dashboard must prioritise alerts using:
#
# CRITICAL
# Immediate executive attention.
#
# HIGH
# Action required soon.
#
# MEDIUM
# Management review required.
#
# LOW
# Informational.
#
# EXAMPLES:
#
# CRITICAL:
# - suspected fraud
# - major payment anomaly
# - security breach
# - shipment loss
# - serious regulatory issue
# - major customer failure
#
# HIGH:
# - delayed shipment
# - supplier failure
# - significant margin deterioration
# - overdue receivable
# - high-value opportunity requiring approval
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 29.7 — AI CONFIDENCE & ESCALATION
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# AI must not present uncertain information as confirmed fact.
#
# Where confidence is insufficient, the system should:
#
# 1. identify the uncertainty
# 2. request additional information
# 3. escalate to an appropriate human
# 4. record the escalation
#
# High-impact decisions require stronger validation than ordinary
# administrative tasks.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 29.8 — DATA GOVERNANCE
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# TriLink data must be separated into appropriate categories:
#
# PUBLIC
# - approved public marketing information
#
# INTERNAL
# - ordinary operational information
#
# CONFIDENTIAL
# - contracts
# - pricing
# - supplier/customer information
# - financial information
#
# RESTRICTED
# - credentials
# - security information
# - sensitive personal information
# - payment/security secrets
#
# AI agents receive only the minimum data required for their task.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 29.9 — SECRET MANAGEMENT
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# NEVER place:
#
# - API keys
# - passwords
# - private keys
# - payment secrets
# - database credentials
# - OAuth secrets
#
# directly into source code or the master plan.
#
# Use secure environment variables or an appropriate secrets manager.
#
# Rotate credentials when required.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 29.10 — BACKUP & RECOVERY
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# Critical business data must have:
#
# - regular backups
# - tested restoration procedures
# - version history
# - disaster recovery procedures
# - documented recovery ownership
#
# A backup is not considered valid until restoration has been tested.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 29.11 — BUSINESS CONTINUITY
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# If an AI service becomes unavailable:
#
# 1. preserve existing transactions
# 2. prevent unsafe duplicate execution
# 3. alert the CEO Dashboard
# 4. queue recoverable tasks
# 5. provide manual fallback procedures
#
# AI failure must not automatically become business failure.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 29.12 — FINANCIAL CONTROLS
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# Financial automation must use configurable approval thresholds.
#
# Example:
#
# LOW VALUE
# AI may execute if explicitly pre-authorised.
#
# MEDIUM VALUE
# AI prepares the transaction and requests approval.
#
# HIGH VALUE
# Founder / Chairman / CEO approval required.
#
# EXCEPTIONAL VALUE
# Executive review plus appropriate financial/legal controls.
#
# Exact thresholds must be configured according to the company's
# banking, accounting, risk and legal requirements.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 29.13 — ANTI-FRAUD CONTROLS
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# AI should identify anomalies including:
#
# - unusual payment requests
# - changed supplier bank details
# - duplicate invoices
# - unusual pricing
# - unusual customer behaviour
# - abnormal refunds
# - suspicious account activity
# - unusual login activity
#
# Suspicious financial activity must be escalated rather than
# automatically approved.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 29.14 — SUPPLIER CONTROLS
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# Before material commercial engagement, TriLink should evaluate:
#
# - legal identity
# - ownership where appropriate
# - country/jurisdiction
# - sanctions/compliance status where applicable
# - business credentials
# - references where appropriate
# - pricing
# - capacity
# - quality
# - delivery performance
# - payment terms
# - contractual risk
#
# AI may prepare the assessment.
# Final approval remains subject to configured authority levels.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 29.15 — CUSTOMER CONTROLS
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# Customer onboarding should evaluate:
#
# - identity/business legitimacy
# - location
# - intended transaction
# - payment risk
# - commercial fit
# - compliance requirements
# - credit risk where applicable
#
# High-risk customers must be escalated.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 29.16 — DOCUMENT CONTROL
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# Documents generated by AI must include appropriate metadata where
# useful, including:
#
# - document type
# - version
# - date
# - owner
# - approval state
#
# AI-generated drafts must not automatically be represented as
# legally approved documents.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 29.17 — COMMUNICATION CONTROL
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# AI may draft:
#
# - emails
# - quotations
# - proposals
# - customer responses
# - supplier requests
# - internal reports
#
# Automated sending is permitted only where the workflow has been
# explicitly authorised.
#
# High-risk, legally significant or commercially sensitive messages
# require human approval.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 29.18 — PERFORMANCE MONITORING
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# CEO Dashboard should monitor:
#
# REVENUE
# GROSS MARGIN
# NET CONTRIBUTION
# CASH POSITION
# PIPELINE
# ORDERS
# SHIPMENTS
# DELIVERY PERFORMANCE
# CUSTOMER RETENTION
# SUPPLIER PERFORMANCE
# AI TASK SUCCESS RATE
# AI ERROR RATE
# ESCALATION RATE
# SECURITY EVENTS
# COMPLIANCE EXCEPTIONS
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 29.19 — AI QUALITY CONTROL
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# AI workflows must be tested before production activation.
#
# Minimum testing:
#
# - normal scenario
# - invalid input
# - missing data
# - duplicate request
# - failed external service
# - permission failure
# - security failure
# - incorrect AI recommendation
# - human override
# - recovery after failure
#
# Production deployment requires successful testing of critical
# workflows.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 29.20 — HUMAN OVERRIDE
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# Founder / Chairman / CEO must have the ability to:
#
# - pause an AI agent
# - disable a workflow
# - revoke permissions
# - require manual approval
# - stop automated communication
# - stop financial automation
# - review audit history
#
# Emergency stop procedures must be documented.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 29.21 — NO BLIND AUTONOMY
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# "100% AI-run" means maximum practical AI automation of approved
# operations — NOT removal of legal, financial, regulatory or
# executive accountability.
#
# TriLink remains controlled by its authorised human leadership.
#
# AI is the operating engine.
# Platformsify is the technology layer.
# The CEO Dashboard is the executive control centre.
# Founder / Chairman / CEO retains ultimate authority.
#
# ============================================================
# END PART 29
# ============================================================


# ============================================================
# PART 30 — AI OPERATING ARCHITECTURE & CEO COMMAND SYSTEM
# ============================================================
#
# PURPOSE
# Define the technical operating model that connects TriLink's
# business operations to the Platformsify CEO Dashboard.
#
# CORE ARCHITECTURE
#
#                 FOUNDER / CHAIRMAN / CEO
#                           |
#                           v
#                TRILINK CEO DASHBOARD
#                           |
#             +-------------+-------------+
#             |             |             |
#             v             v             v
#        AI CONTROL     BUSINESS DATA   ALERT ENGINE
#             |
#       +-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+
#       |     |     |     |     |     |
#       v     v     v     v     v     v
#     TRADE LOGISTICS ENERGY FINANCE CRM COMPLIANCE
#       |     |     |     |     |     |
#       +-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+
#                           |
#                           v
#                    EXTERNAL SERVICES
#
# Platformsify remains the technology operating layer.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 30.1 — CEO DASHBOARD HOME
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# The CEO Dashboard must provide a single executive view of:
#
# - today's priorities
# - revenue
# - orders
# - active shipments
# - trade opportunities
# - energy opportunities
# - cash position
# - receivables
# - payables
# - customer activity
# - supplier activity
# - AI activity
# - alerts
# - pending approvals
# - compliance exceptions
# - system health
#
# The Founder / Chairman / CEO should be able to understand the
# current state of the business without opening multiple management
# systems.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 30.2 — EXECUTIVE COMMAND CENTRE
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# The CEO Dashboard should support natural-language commands such as:
#
# "Give me today's business briefing."
#
# "Show me all high-value opportunities."
#
# "What shipments are delayed?"
#
# "Which customers owe us money?"
#
# "Which suppliers are underperforming?"
#
# "Show me this month's revenue and margin."
#
# "Find the biggest risks in the business."
#
# "Prepare a report for tomorrow's management review."
#
# "Show me everything waiting for my approval."
#
