# AI interprets the request, retrieves authorised information and
# returns an executive response.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 30.3 — COMMAND EXECUTION MODEL
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# Every command follows:
#
# REQUEST
#   |
# VALIDATE USER AUTHORITY
#   |
# IDENTIFY INTENT
#   |
# CHECK DATA ACCESS
#   |
# CHECK AI PERMISSION
#   |
# EXECUTE OR PREPARE
#   |
# VALIDATE RESULT
#   |
# LOG ACTION
#   |
# REPORT RESULT
#
# No privileged action should bypass permission validation.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 30.4 — BUSINESS EVENT ENGINE
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# TriLink should operate around business events.
#
# EXAMPLES:
#
# LEAD_CREATED
# CUSTOMER_ONBOARDED
# QUOTE_REQUESTED
# QUOTE_CREATED
# QUOTE_ACCEPTED
# ORDER_CREATED
# PAYMENT_REQUESTED
# PAYMENT_RECEIVED
# SHIPMENT_CREATED
# SHIPMENT_DISPATCHED
# SHIPMENT_DELAYED
# SHIPMENT_DELIVERED
# INVOICE_CREATED
# INVOICE_OVERDUE
# SUPPLIER_ALERT
# CUSTOMER_ALERT
# COMPLIANCE_ALERT
# AI_EXCEPTION
# APPROVAL_REQUESTED
# APPROVAL_GRANTED
# APPROVAL_REJECTED
#
# Each event can trigger authorised AI workflows.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 30.5 — AI WORKFLOW ENGINE
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# Workflow format:
#
# TRIGGER
#    ->
# CONDITIONS
#    ->
# AI ANALYSIS
#    ->
# DECISION
#    ->
# ACTION
#    ->
# VALIDATION
#    ->
# AUDIT
#
# Example:
#
# SHIPMENT_DELAYED
#    ->
# identify affected customer
#    ->
# calculate commercial impact
#    ->
# prepare customer communication
#    ->
# notify authorised user
#    ->
# update CRM
#    ->
# record audit event
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 30.6 — APPROVAL QUEUE
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# The CEO Dashboard must contain one consolidated approval queue.
#
# APPROVAL TYPES:
#
# - contracts
# - high-value quotations
# - payments
# - refunds
# - supplier onboarding
# - customer onboarding
# - exceptional discounts
# - high-risk transactions
# - strategic partnerships
# - major operational changes
#
# Each approval should show:
#
# - requested action
# - requester/AI agent
# - value
# - reason
# - supporting information
# - risk level
# - recommended decision
# - approve button
# - reject button
# - request changes option
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 30.7 — AI MEMORY MODEL
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# AI should distinguish between:
#
# SESSION CONTEXT
# Information required for the current task.
#
# OPERATIONAL MEMORY
# Approved business facts needed for ongoing workflows.
#
# EXECUTIVE MEMORY
# Strategic priorities, approved policies and business objectives.
#
# AUDIT HISTORY
# Immutable or appropriately protected records of previous actions.
#
# AI must not treat unverified assumptions as permanent business facts.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 30.8 — SOURCE OF TRUTH
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# Each important business object must have one authoritative record.
#
# EXAMPLES:
#
# CUSTOMER
# One authoritative customer record.
#
# SUPPLIER
# One authoritative supplier record.
#
# ORDER
# One authoritative order record.
#
# SHIPMENT
# One authoritative shipment record.
#
# INVOICE
# One authoritative invoice record.
#
# AI may create views and reports but must not create conflicting
# parallel versions of the same business object.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 30.9 — DATA FLOW
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# External data
#      |
#      v
# ingestion / validation
#      |
#      v
# business data layer
#      |
#      +------> AI agents
#      |
#      +------> CEO Dashboard
#      |
#      +------> reporting
#      |
#      v
# audit/event layer
#
# External information should be validated before being used for
# consequential decisions.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 30.10 — INTEGRATION PRINCIPLE
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# External systems may be connected where commercially necessary.
#
# Possible categories:
#
# - banking/payment systems
# - accounting
# - CRM
# - logistics providers
# - shipping/tracking
# - email
# - communications
# - document storage
# - identity/authentication
# - analytics
# - AI services
#
# Integrations must be permission-scoped and auditable.
#
# Platformsify CEO Dashboard remains the executive control layer.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 30.11 — API GATEWAY PRINCIPLE
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# External integrations should use controlled interfaces.
#
# Each integration should define:
#
# - authentication
# - permissions
# - rate limits
# - input validation
# - output validation
# - error handling
# - logging
# - retry behaviour
# - failure behaviour
#
# Never allow unrestricted external access to internal business data.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 30.12 — ERROR HANDLING
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# AI workflows must distinguish:
#
# TEMPORARY FAILURE
# Retry safely.
#
# DATA FAILURE
# Request correction or additional information.
#
# PERMISSION FAILURE
# Stop and escalate.
#
# EXTERNAL SERVICE FAILURE
# Queue/retry where safe.
#
# BUSINESS RULE FAILURE
# Stop and request review.
#
# SECURITY FAILURE
# Stop, protect the system and escalate immediately.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 30.13 — DUPLICATE PROTECTION
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# Critical workflows must be idempotent where possible.
#
# The system must prevent accidental duplication of:
#
# - orders
# - invoices
# - payments
# - shipments
# - customer records
# - supplier records
# - notifications
#
# AI retrying a failed workflow must not automatically create a second
# financial or commercial transaction.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 30.14 — REAL-TIME OPERATIONS
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# Where supported, the CEO Dashboard should surface important events
# as they occur.
#
# Priority events:
#
# - payment received
# - payment failed
# - shipment dispatched
# - shipment delayed
# - shipment delivered
# - major lead created
# - major quotation accepted
# - major customer issue
# - security event
# - compliance exception
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 30.15 — DAILY AI CEO BRIEFING
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# The system should be capable of producing a daily executive briefing
# containing:
#
# 1. Revenue
# 2. Orders
# 3. Cash
# 4. Pipeline
# 5. Shipments
# 6. Customers
# 7. Suppliers
# 8. Energy pipeline
# 9. Risks
# 10. Approvals
# 11. AI activity
# 12. Recommended priorities
#
# The briefing must distinguish:
#
# FACT
# CONFIRMED BUSINESS DATA
#
# ANALYSIS
# AI interpretation
#
# RECOMMENDATION
# Suggested action
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 30.16 — WEEKLY EXECUTIVE REVIEW
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# AI should prepare a weekly review covering:
#
# - performance against targets
# - revenue trend
# - margin trend
# - pipeline growth
# - conversion rate
# - logistics performance
# - customer retention
# - supplier performance
# - outstanding receivables
# - operational risks
# - compliance issues
# - AI efficiency
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 30.17 — MONTHLY BOARD/FOUNDER REVIEW
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# Monthly reporting should provide:
#
# FINANCIAL PERFORMANCE
# COMMERCIAL PERFORMANCE
# OPERATIONAL PERFORMANCE
# CUSTOMER PERFORMANCE
# SUPPLIER PERFORMANCE
# ENERGY PROJECT PIPELINE
# RISK REGISTER
# COMPLIANCE STATUS
# TECHNOLOGY PERFORMANCE
# AI AUTOMATION PERFORMANCE
#
# The Founder / Chairman / CEO uses this information for strategic
# decision-making.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 30.18 — AI KPI SYSTEM
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# AI performance should itself be measured.
#
# KPI EXAMPLES:
#
# - tasks completed
# - successful tasks
# - failed tasks
# - escalations
# - human overrides
# - average processing time
# - cost per workflow
# - automation percentage
# - error percentage
# - customer response time
# - financial anomaly detection
#
# High automation is not considered successful if quality or control
# deteriorates.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 30.19 — CEO DASHBOARD HEALTH
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# The dashboard must display system health including:
#
# - AI service status
# - database status
# - integration status
# - authentication status
# - payment integration status
# - notification status
# - background workflow status
# - recent errors
# - queued jobs
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 30.20 — EMERGENCY COMMANDS
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# The Founder / Chairman / CEO must have access to emergency controls
# such as:
#
# PAUSE ALL AI AUTOMATION
# PAUSE FINANCIAL AUTOMATION
# PAUSE OUTBOUND COMMUNICATION
# PAUSE SPECIFIC AGENT
# REVOKE AGENT PERMISSION
# REQUIRE HUMAN APPROVAL
# VIEW ACTIVE JOBS
# VIEW SECURITY EVENTS
#
# Emergency controls must be protected by strong authentication and
# appropriate authorisation.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 30.21 — EXECUTIVE PRINCIPLE
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# TriLink should feel like one integrated company rather than a
# collection of disconnected software tools.
#
# The Founder / Chairman / CEO sees one command centre.
#
# The AI agents operate behind that command centre.
#
# Business data flows through controlled workflows.
#
# Exceptions rise to the CEO Dashboard.
#
# Decisions remain auditable.
#
# Human authority remains protected.
#
# ============================================================
# END PART 30
# ============================================================


# ============================================================
# PART 31 — COMPLETE CEO DASHBOARD MASTER SPECIFICATION
# ============================================================
#
# PURPOSE
# Define the complete executive interface for TriLink Global.
#
# CORE RULE:
# Platformsify CEO Dashboard is the SINGLE executive control centre.
#
# TriLink must NOT create a second competing CEO dashboard.
#
# The dashboard is the executive command layer connecting:
#
# FOUNDER / CHAIRMAN / CEO
#          |
#          v
# PLATFORMSIFY CEO DASHBOARD
#          |
#          v
# AI OPERATING SYSTEM
#          |
#   +------+------+------+------+------+
#   |      |      |      |      |      |
# TRADE LOGISTICS ENERGY FINANCE CRM
#          |
#          v
#     BUSINESS DATA
#
# ============================================================
# 31.1 — EXECUTIVE DASHBOARD OBJECTIVE
# ============================================================
#
# The Founder / Chairman / CEO must be able to understand the
# entire company from one secure executive environment.
#
# The dashboard must answer:
#
# - What is happening?
# - What changed?
# - What needs my attention?
# - What is making money?
# - What is losing money?
# - What opportunities exist?
# - What risks exist?
# - What is the AI doing?
# - What requires my approval?
# - What should I do next?
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 31.2 — DASHBOARD DESIGN PRINCIPLE
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# The interface should be:
#
# - simple
# - fast
# - executive-focused
# - mobile-friendly
# - responsive
# - secure
# - data-driven
# - AI-first
# - action-oriented
#
# Avoid unnecessary screens and duplicated information.
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 31.3 — PRIMARY NAVIGATION
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# CEO Dashboard navigation should include:
#
# 1. COMMAND CENTRE
# 2. AI ASSISTANT
# 3. BUSINESS OVERVIEW
# 4. TRADE
# 5. LOGISTICS
# 6. ENERGY
# 7. CUSTOMERS
# 8. SUPPLIERS

# 13. ANALYTICS

# 16. ALERTS

# 31.4 — COMMAND CENTRE

# The Command Centre is the default CEO landing page.
#
# It should display:
#
# TODAY
# - date
# - operational status
# - top priorities
#
# FINANCIAL SNAPSHOT
# - revenue
# - gross margin
# - cash position
# - receivables
# - payables
#
# COMMERCIAL SNAPSHOT
# - active customers
# - open opportunities
# - quotations
# - orders

#

# - exceptions

#
# AI

# - completed tasks
# - failed tasks
# - escalations
#
# EXECUTIVE ACTIONS
# - pending approvals
# - critical alerts
# - recommended actions
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 31.5 — CEO PRIORITY PANEL
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# AI should generate a ranked list:
#
# 1. CRITICAL
# 2. HIGH
# 3. MEDIUM
# 4. LOW
#
# Each item should contain:
#

# - financial impact where known
# - business impact
# - urgency
# - AI recommendation
# - required decision
# - action button
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 31.6 — AI EXECUTIVE ASSISTANT
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# The CEO Dashboard must provide a natural-language AI interface.
#
# Example commands:
#
# "Give me today's CEO briefing."
#
# "What are my biggest risks?"
#
# "Show me the best opportunities."
#
# "Which customers are overdue?"
#
# "Which shipments need attention?"
#
# "Show me this month's revenue."
#
# "Compare revenue with last month."
#
# "Which suppliers are underperforming?"
#
# "Prepare tomorrow's executive report."
#
# "Show me everything waiting for approval."
#
# "What should I focus on today?"
#
# The AI must clearly distinguish:
#
# FACT
# CONFIRMED DATA
#
# ANALYSIS
# AI INTERPRETATION
#
# RECOMMENDATION
# SUGGESTED ACTION
#
# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 31.7 — AI COMMAND SAFETY
# ------------------------------------------------------------
#
# Natural-language commands must pass through:
#
# AUTHENTICATION
# ->
# AUTHORISATION
# ->
# INTENT DETECTION
# ->
# RISK CHECK
# ->
# DATA ACCESS CHECK
# ->
# EXECUTION OR APPROVAL
# ->
# AUDIT LOG
#
# AI must never assume permission merely because the command was
# written in natural language.

#
# 1. Open Command Centre.
# 2. Review AI CEO briefing.
# 3. Review critical alerts.
# 4. Review approvals.
# 5. Review financial position.

# 31.28 — CEO DASHBOARD PRINCIPLE

# INTELLIGENCE
#      +
# APPROVALS
#      +
# AUTOMATION
#      +

# ------------------------------------------------------------
#

# CEO can:
#
# - see the company's current state
# - understand financial performance
# - see active operations
# - see opportunities
# - see risks
# - communicate with AI
# - approve important actions
# - control AI agents
# - review audit history
# - respond to critical alerts
#
# without requiring a separate competing CEO management dashboard.
#
# ============================================================
# END PART 31
# ============================================================


# ============================================================
# PART 32 — AI GOVERNANCE, SECURITY & EXECUTIVE CONTROL
# ============================================================

# PURPOSE
# This section defines how TriLink's AI-operated business remains
# secure, controlled, auditable and ultimately accountable to the
# Founder / Chairman / CEO.

# ============================================================
# 32.1 SINGLE EXECUTIVE CONTROL CENTRE
# ============================================================

# The Platformsify CEO Dashboard is the SINGLE executive control
# centre for TriLink Global.

# The CEO Dashboard must provide one consolidated view of:

# - company performance
# - revenue
# - costs
# - profit
# - cash position
# - active deals
# - customers
# - suppliers
# - shipments
# - logistics
# - energy projects
# - trade opportunities
# - AI activity
# - alerts
# - risks
# - compliance
# - contracts
# - approvals
# - tasks
# - business intelligence
# - strategic recommendations

# No separate TriLink CEO management dashboard should be created.

# ============================================================
# 32.2 AI OPERATING MODEL
# ============================================================

# AI should operate TriLink through controlled workflows.

# AI may:
# - monitor business activity
# - classify information
# - prepare reports
# - analyse opportunities
# - identify risks
# - prepare quotations
# - prepare draft communications
# - match buyers and suppliers
# - track shipments
# - identify delays
# - prepare invoices
# - reconcile approved operational information
# - produce forecasts
# - generate management recommendations
# - prioritise operational tasks
# - escalate exceptions
# - maintain operational records

# AI must NOT independently override Founder authority.

# ============================================================
# 32.3 HUMAN APPROVAL GATES
# ============================================================

# HUMAN APPROVAL REQUIRED FOR:

# - major contracts
# - new banking arrangements
# - movement of significant funds
# - changes to ownership
# - new shareholders
# - major investments
# - loans
# - guarantees
# - legal settlements
# - regulatory submissions where executive approval is required
# - high-risk jurisdictions
# - major suppliers
# - strategic customers
# - acquisitions
# - mergers
# - new subsidiaries
# - major energy projects
# - material changes to company strategy

# AI prepares.
# CEO reviews.
# CEO approves.
# System records the decision.

# ============================================================
# 32.4 AI PERMISSION LEVELS
# ============================================================

# LEVEL 0 — OBSERVE
# AI can read approved information and produce analysis.

# LEVEL 1 — RECOMMEND
# AI can recommend actions but cannot execute them.

# LEVEL 2 — DRAFT
# AI can prepare documents, messages, quotations and workflows.

# LEVEL 3 — EXECUTE APPROVED OPERATIONS
# AI can execute predefined low-risk workflows within approved limits.

# LEVEL 4 — EXECUTIVE APPROVAL
# AI prepares the complete decision package for CEO approval.

# LEVEL 5 — FOUNDER AUTHORITY
# Only Founder / Chairman / CEO can authorise exceptional,
# strategic or ownership-level decisions.

# Default AI permission:
# LEVEL 2.

# Approved operational automation may use LEVEL 3.

# ============================================================
# 32.5 AUDIT TRAIL
# ============================================================

# Every material AI action must record:

# - timestamp
# - user/system identity
# - AI workflow
# - action taken
# - information used
# - decision/recommendation
# - approval status
# - approving executive where applicable
# - resulting system change
# - exception or error information

# Audit records must be tamper-resistant and retained according
# to applicable legal and business requirements.

# ============================================================
# 32.6 SECURITY PRINCIPLES
# ============================================================

# TriLink must use:

# - least-privilege access
# - role-based permissions
# - strong authentication
# - encrypted secrets
# - environment separation
# - secure API keys
# - secret rotation
# - logging
# - monitoring
# - backup procedures
# - incident response
# - dependency/security scanning
# - controlled production deployment

# NEVER place:
# - passwords
# - API keys
# - payment credentials
# - private tokens
# - database secrets
# - authentication secrets

# directly inside public source code.

# ============================================================
# 32.7 AI SAFETY RULE
# ============================================================

# AI must never fabricate:
# - customers
# - suppliers
# - prices
# - contracts
# - shipments
# - payments
# - regulatory approvals
# - financial results
# - company credentials
# - completed transactions

# If information is unavailable:
# AI must clearly state that it is unavailable.

# If confidence is insufficient:
# AI must escalate to a human.

# ============================================================
# 32.8 EXECUTIVE ALERT SYSTEM
# ============================================================

# The CEO Dashboard should prioritise alerts by severity.

# CRITICAL
# Immediate Founder/CEO attention required.

# HIGH
# Executive review required soon.

# MEDIUM
# Operational management review.

# LOW
# Routine monitoring.

# Example critical alerts:
# - suspected fraud
# - major payment failure
# - major shipment failure
# - serious compliance issue
# - cyber-security incident
# - major customer dispute
# - major supplier failure
# - significant financial anomaly

# ============================================================
# 32.9 BUSINESS CONTINUITY
# ============================================================

# TriLink must maintain recovery procedures for:

# - application failure
# - database failure
# - API failure
# - cloud outage
# - payment failure
# - supplier failure
# - logistics disruption
# - cyber incident
# - AI service outage

# AI automation must fail safely.

# If the AI layer becomes unavailable:
# critical human-controlled business functions must remain
# recoverable.

# ============================================================
# 32.10 CEO DECISION LOOP
# ============================================================

# TRILink operating loop:

# DATA
#   ↓
# AI ANALYSIS
#   ↓
# OPPORTUNITY / RISK DETECTION
#   ↓
# AI RECOMMENDATION
#   ↓
# APPROVAL GATE
#   ↓
# EXECUTION
#   ↓
# VERIFICATION
#   ↓
# AUDIT LOG
#   ↓
# CEO DASHBOARD
#   ↓
# CONTINUOUS IMPROVEMENT

# ============================================================
# 32.11 FINAL GOVERNANCE PRINCIPLE
# ============================================================

# TriLink is AI-first, not AI-uncontrolled.

# AI provides:
# speed
# automation
# analysis
# scale
# monitoring
# operational intelligence

# Founder / Chairman / CEO provides:
# ownership
# authority
# accountability
# strategy
# final judgement

# Platformsify provides the technology operating layer.

# The TriLink CEO Dashboard remains the SINGLE executive
# command centre.

# ============================================================
# END PART 32
# ============================================================


# ============================================================
# PART 33 — DATA ARCHITECTURE, MASTER RECORDS & AI MEMORY
# ============================================================

# PURPOSE
# This section defines the single source of truth for TriLink data.
# The AI must operate from controlled, structured business records
# rather than disconnected spreadsheets, messages or duplicated
# databases.

# ============================================================
# 33.1 SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH
# ============================================================

# TriLink must maintain one authoritative business data layer.

# Platformsify CEO Dashboard is the executive interface.

# The underlying approved data services remain the system of record.

# No department may create an independent competing source of truth
# for core company information.

# ============================================================
# 33.2 CORE MASTER RECORDS
# ============================================================

# The business data model must support:

# COMPANY
# - legal name
# - trading name
# - company identifiers
# - jurisdiction
# - business status
# - directors
# - ownership structure

# PEOPLE
# - employees
# - contractors
# - executives
# - authorised users
# - roles
# - permissions

# CUSTOMERS
# - customer identity
# - contact information
# - country
# - sector
# - verification status
# - commercial status
# - account owner

# SUPPLIERS
# - supplier identity
# - country
# - products/services
# - verification status
# - commercial terms
# - risk status

# PRODUCTS
# - product identity
# - category
# - specifications
# - origin
# - pricing
# - availability
# - compliance information

# TRADE DEALS
# - buyer
# - supplier
# - product
# - quantity
# - origin
# - destination
# - price
# - currency
# - Incoterms
# - status
# - margin
# - approval status

# SHIPMENTS
# - shipment ID
# - linked trade
# - origin
# - destination
# - carrier
# - milestones
# - ETA
# - status
# - exceptions

# PAYMENTS
# - transaction reference
# - linked customer/supplier
# - amount
# - currency
# - status
# - approval
# - reconciliation status

# CONTRACTS
# - contract ID
# - parties
# - effective date
# - expiry date
# - obligations
# - approval status
# - document location

# ENERGY PROJECTS
# - project ID
# - location
# - project type
# - capacity
# - development stage
# - partners
# - financial model
# - permits
# - risks
# - approval status

# ============================================================
# 33.3 UNIQUE IDENTIFIERS
# ============================================================

# Every important business object must have a unique identifier.

# Examples:

# CUSTOMER-ID
# SUPPLIER-ID
# PRODUCT-ID
# DEAL-ID
# SHIPMENT-ID
# PAYMENT-ID
# CONTRACT-ID
# PROJECT-ID
# TASK-ID
# INCIDENT-ID

# IDs must remain stable throughout the lifecycle of the record.

# ============================================================
# 33.4 DATA RELATIONSHIPS
# ============================================================

# Records must be linked rather than duplicated.

# CUSTOMER
#   ↓
# DEAL
#   ↓
# PRODUCT
#   ↓
# SUPPLIER
#   ↓
# SHIPMENT
#   ↓
# PAYMENT
#   ↓
# PROFITABILITY

# This allows the AI to understand the complete commercial chain.

# ============================================================
# 33.5 DATA VALIDATION
# ============================================================

# AI and applications must validate important data before saving it.

# Validate:
# - required fields
# - formats
# - dates
# - currencies
# - quantities
# - duplicate records
# - invalid identifiers
# - inconsistent statuses
# - impossible values

# Financial and legal records receive stricter validation.

# ============================================================
# 33.6 DUPLICATE PREVENTION
# ============================================================

# The system should detect potential duplicates.

# Examples:
# - duplicate customers
# - duplicate suppliers
# - duplicate products
# - duplicate deals
# - duplicate invoices
# - duplicate payments

# AI may recommend merging records.

# High-impact merges require authorised approval.

# ============================================================
# 33.7 DATA QUALITY SCORE
# ============================================================

# Important records should receive a data-quality score.

# HIGH
# Complete and verified.

# MEDIUM
# Mostly complete but requiring review.

# LOW
# Missing or uncertain information.

# CRITICAL DATA
# Must not be used for automated high-impact decisions until
# sufficiently verified.

# ============================================================
# 33.8 AI BUSINESS MEMORY
# ============================================================

# AI memory must be structured and controlled.

# AI may remember:
# - approved company policies
# - approved operating procedures
# - approved commercial rules
# - customer preferences where legitimately recorded
# - supplier capabilities
# - historical business decisions
# - recurring operational patterns
# - approved strategic objectives

# AI must not treat an unverified conversation or prediction as
# authoritative business fact.

# ============================================================
# 33.9 SOURCE PRIORITY
# ============================================================

# When information conflicts, the AI follows this hierarchy:

# 1. AUTHORITATIVE LEGAL/FINANCIAL RECORD
# 2. APPROVED SYSTEM OF RECORD
# 3. VERIFIED BUSINESS DOCUMENT
# 4. AUTHORISED EXECUTIVE INSTRUCTION
# 5. VERIFIED EXTERNAL DATA
# 6. AI INFERENCE

# AI inference must never silently override authoritative records.

# ============================================================
# 33.10 DATA CHANGE CONTROL
# ============================================================

# Material changes must preserve history.

# The system should record:

# - previous value
# - new value
# - timestamp
# - actor
# - reason
# - approval where required

# Critical financial, legal and ownership records must maintain
# a complete audit history.

# ============================================================
# 33.11 DOCUMENT MANAGEMENT
# ============================================================

# Business documents must be linked to their relevant records.

# Examples:

# CUSTOMER
# └── contracts
# └── verification
# └── correspondence

# DEAL
# └── quotation
# └── purchase order
# └── contract
# └── invoice

# SHIPMENT
# └── shipping documents
# └── customs documents
# └── delivery evidence

# PROJECT
# └── feasibility
# └── permits
# └── agreements
# └── financial models

# ============================================================
# 33.12 AI RETRIEVAL
# ============================================================

# AI should retrieve relevant business records before making
# recommendations.

# AI should prefer:
# - current records
# - verified records
# - authorised documents
# - relevant historical records

# AI should identify the source of important information.

# ============================================================
# 33.13 DATA RETENTION
# ============================================================

# Retention periods must follow applicable:

# - company law
# - tax requirements
# - accounting requirements
# - contractual requirements
# - privacy requirements
# - regulatory requirements

# Records must not be deleted merely because they are old if a
# legal or audit requirement requires retention.

# ============================================================
# 33.14 PRIVACY
# ============================================================

# Personal data must be handled according to applicable privacy law.

# Collect only information necessary for legitimate business
# purposes.

# Access must be restricted according to role and business need.

# Sensitive information must receive additional protection.

# ============================================================
# 33.15 BACKUP & RECOVERY
# ============================================================

# Critical business data must be backed up.

# Recovery procedures must be tested periodically.

# Backup strategy should protect against:

# - accidental deletion
# - corruption
# - system failure
# - ransomware
# - infrastructure outage

# ============================================================
# 33.16 CEO DATA VIEW
# ============================================================

# The Platformsify CEO Dashboard should provide the Founder /
# Chairman / CEO with:

# - real-time KPIs
# - financial overview
# - active deals
# - shipment status
# - customer pipeline
# - supplier pipeline
# - energy portfolio
# - AI activity
# - alerts
# - unresolved exceptions
# - approval queue
# - strategic recommendations

# The dashboard should show both:
# CURRENT STATE
# and
# WHY IT CHANGED.

# ============================================================
# 33.17 AI DECISION CONTEXT
# ============================================================

# Before making a material recommendation, AI should consider:

# - current data
# - historical performance
# - financial impact
# - operational impact
# - customer impact
# - supplier impact
# - compliance considerations
# - risk
# - confidence level

# Recommendations should include the reasoning summary and
# relevant supporting records where appropriate.

# ============================================================
# 33.18 FINAL DATA PRINCIPLE
# ============================================================

# ONE BUSINESS
# ONE AUTHORITATIVE DATA MODEL
# ONE EXECUTIVE CONTROL CENTRE
# ONE AUDITABLE AI OPERATING LAYER

# Platformsify CEO Dashboard = executive control interface.
# Approved backend/data services = system of record.
# AI = controlled intelligence and automation.
# Founder / Chairman / CEO = final authority.

# ============================================================
# END PART 33
# ============================================================


# ============================================================
# PART 34 — AI COMMERCIAL ENGINE & REVENUE SYSTEM
# ============================================================

# PURPOSE
# This section defines how TriLink AI identifies, qualifies,
# develops and manages commercial opportunities while keeping
# financial and strategic authority with the Founder / Chairman /
# CEO.

# ============================================================
# 34.1 COMMERCIAL ENGINE
# ============================================================

# TriLink commercial workflow:

# MARKET INTELLIGENCE
#        ↓
# OPPORTUNITY DISCOVERY
#        ↓
# BUYER IDENTIFICATION
#        ↓
# SUPPLIER IDENTIFICATION
#        ↓
# VERIFICATION
#        ↓
# DEAL MATCHING
#        ↓
# PRICING
#        ↓
# MARGIN ANALYSIS
#        ↓
# RISK ANALYSIS
#        ↓
# CEO APPROVAL WHERE REQUIRED
#        ↓
# QUOTATION
#        ↓
# CONTRACT
#        ↓
# ORDER
#        ↓
# LOGISTICS
#        ↓
# PAYMENT
#        ↓
# DELIVERY
#        ↓
# PROFITABILITY
#        ↓
# CUSTOMER RETENTION

# ============================================================
# 34.2 AI MARKET INTELLIGENCE
# ============================================================

# AI should continuously analyse approved market information.

# Monitor:
# - product demand
# - pricing
# - supply availability
# - freight conditions
# - currency movements
# - trade routes
# - customer demand
# - supplier capacity
# - market competition
# - regulatory developments
# - energy opportunities

# AI must distinguish verified information from estimates.

# ============================================================
# 34.3 OPPORTUNITY SCORING
# ============================================================

# Every material opportunity should receive a score.

# Evaluate:

# MARKET DEMAND
# SUPPLIER RELIABILITY
# BUYER QUALITY
# EXPECTED MARGIN
# CAPITAL REQUIREMENT
# LOGISTICS COMPLEXITY
# PAYMENT RISK
# COUNTRY RISK
# COMPLIANCE RISK
# COMPETITIVE POSITION
# EXECUTION DIFFICULTY
# STRATEGIC VALUE

# Example classification:

# A — HIGH PRIORITY
# Strong commercial opportunity with acceptable risk.

# B — PROMISING
# Worth developing with additional verification.

# C — WATCH
# Insufficient evidence or weak economics.

# D — REJECT
# Unacceptable risk or poor economics.

# ============================================================
# 34.4 BUYER ACQUISITION
# ============================================================

# AI may support:

# - buyer discovery
# - lead research
# - lead qualification
# - company profiling
# - outreach drafting
# - follow-up scheduling
# - CRM updates
# - meeting preparation
# - quotation preparation
# - opportunity scoring

# AI must not misrepresent TriLink's capabilities or credentials.

# ============================================================
# 34.5 SUPPLIER ACQUISITION
# ============================================================

# AI may identify and compare suppliers based on:

# - product capability
# - capacity
# - pricing
# - quality
# - certifications
# - delivery history
# - location
# - payment terms
# - export capability
# - reputation
# - risk

# Suppliers must undergo appropriate verification before being
# treated as approved commercial partners.

# ============================================================
# 34.6 COUNTERPARTY VERIFICATION
# ============================================================

# Before material transactions, the system should verify,
# where applicable:

# - legal identity
# - company registration
# - beneficial ownership information where required
# - business address
# - contact details
# - sanctions screening
# - applicable trade restrictions
# - relevant licences
# - certifications
# - banking/payment information

# Verification requirements depend on transaction size,
# jurisdiction and risk.

# ============================================================
# 34.7 DEAL MATCHING
# ============================================================

# AI should match:

# BUYER
# +
# PRODUCT
# +
# SUPPLIER
# +
# ROUTE
# +
# PRICE
# +
# LOGISTICS
# +
# PAYMENT TERMS

# The system should calculate an estimated commercial outcome.

# ============================================================
# 34.8 PRICING ENGINE
# ============================================================

# Pricing should consider:

# supplier price
# + transport
# + insurance
# + duties/taxes where applicable
# + handling
# + warehousing
# + payment costs
# + platform/technology costs
# + contingency
# + target margin
# = proposed selling price

# Pricing must identify assumptions.

# AI should never hide material costs to make a deal appear
# more profitable.

# ============================================================
# 34.9 MARGIN PROTECTION
# ============================================================

# Every deal should calculate:

# REVENUE
# DIRECT COST
# GROSS PROFIT
# GROSS MARGIN
# ESTIMATED OPERATING COST
# ESTIMATED NET CONTRIBUTION

# AI should flag:

# - negative margin
# - unusually low margin
# - unrealistic costs
# - missing costs
# - unusual discounts
# - unexpected currency exposure

# ============================================================
# 34.10 DEAL APPROVAL MATRIX
# ============================================================

# LOW-RISK / PRE-APPROVED
# AI may execute within defined limits.

# STANDARD DEAL
# AI prepares complete commercial package.

# HIGH-VALUE DEAL
# CEO approval required.

# HIGH-RISK DEAL
# CEO approval plus appropriate specialist review.

# EXCEPTIONAL DEAL
# Founder / Chairman / CEO decision required.

# No AI workflow may bypass the configured approval matrix.

# ============================================================
# 34.11 QUOTATION ENGINE
# ============================================================

# AI may generate draft quotations containing:

# - quotation number
# - customer
# - product
# - quantity
# - specifications
# - unit price
# - total price
# - currency
# - delivery terms
# - payment terms
# - validity period
# - assumptions
# - exclusions
# - applicable taxes/duties
# - contact information

# Final issuance must follow the approval policy.

# ============================================================
# 34.12 CONTRACT WORKFLOW
# ============================================================

# AI may prepare contract drafts from approved templates.

# Workflow:

# DEAL
# ↓
# COMMERCIAL TERMS
# ↓
# CONTRACT DRAFT
# ↓
# RISK REVIEW
# ↓
# LEGAL REVIEW WHERE REQUIRED
# ↓
# CEO APPROVAL
# ↓
# SIGNATURE
# ↓
# CONTRACT RECORD
# ↓
# OBLIGATION TRACKING

# AI must not provide legal advice as a substitute for qualified
# professional legal review where such review is required.

# ============================================================
# 34.13 SALES PIPELINE
# ============================================================

# Pipeline stages:

# LEAD
# ↓
# QUALIFIED
# ↓
# DISCOVERY
# ↓
# VERIFIED OPPORTUNITY
# ↓
# COMMERCIAL DISCUSSION
# ↓
# QUOTED
# ↓
# NEGOTIATION
# ↓
# CONTRACTED
# ↓
# ORDERED
# ↓
# DELIVERED
# ↓
# COMPLETED
# ↓
# REPEAT BUSINESS

# Lost opportunities must record a reason.

# ============================================================
# 34.14 CUSTOMER RELATIONSHIP ENGINE
# ============================================================

# AI should monitor:

# - customer activity
# - order frequency
# - order value
# - payment behaviour
# - complaints
# - delivery performance
# - repeat purchases
# - churn risk
# - expansion opportunities

# AI may recommend:

# - follow-up
# - reorder
# - new products
# - improved terms
# - retention actions
# - account escalation

# ============================================================
# 34.15 REVENUE FORECASTING
# ============================================================

# CEO Dashboard should display:

# - current revenue
# - contracted revenue
# - pipeline value
# - weighted pipeline
# - expected monthly revenue
# - expected quarterly revenue
# - expected annual revenue
# - gross profit forecast
# - cash collection forecast

# Forecasts must show assumptions and confidence.

# ============================================================
# 34.16 COMMERCIAL KPIs
# ============================================================

# PRIMARY KPIs:

# Revenue
# Gross Profit
# Gross Margin
# Net Contribution
# Active Deals
# Conversion Rate
# Average Deal Value
# Customer Acquisition Cost
# Customer Retention
# Repeat Purchase Rate
# Days to Close
# Days to Collect
# Pipeline Value

# ============================================================
# 34.17 AI COMMERCIAL ALERTS
# ============================================================

# AI should alert the CEO when:

# - a major deal changes materially
# - margin falls below threshold
# - customer payment is late
# - supplier price changes
# - logistics costs increase
# - expected delivery changes
# - customer risk increases
# - opportunity value changes
# - forecast materially changes

# ============================================================
# 34.18 COMMERCIAL FRAUD CONTROLS
# ============================================================

# AI should flag:

# - duplicate invoices
# - unusual payment requests
# - changed bank details
# - unusual discounts
# - suspicious counterparties
# - unexpected beneficiary changes
# - inconsistent documentation
# - abnormal transaction patterns

# Bank/payment changes require independent verification and
# appropriate human approval.

# ============================================================
# 34.19 COMMERCIAL LEARNING LOOP
# ============================================================

# After every completed transaction:

# RESULTS
# ↓
# ACTUAL COSTS
# ↓
# ACTUAL REVENUE
# ↓
# ACTUAL MARGIN
# ↓
# DELIVERY PERFORMANCE
# ↓
# CUSTOMER FEEDBACK
# ↓
# SUPPLIER PERFORMANCE
# ↓
# AI LEARNING
# ↓
# IMPROVED FUTURE QUOTATIONS

# Historical results must improve future recommendations without
# corrupting authoritative records.

# ============================================================
# 34.20 CEO COMMERCIAL CONTROL
# ============================================================

# Platformsify CEO Dashboard must allow the Founder / Chairman /
# CEO to see:

# - best opportunities
# - worst risks
# - highest-value customers
# - highest-value suppliers
# - most profitable deals
# - deals requiring approval
# - deals at risk
# - revenue forecast
# - margin forecast
# - cash collection
# - commercial recommendations

# ============================================================
# 34.21 FINAL COMMERCIAL PRINCIPLE
# ============================================================

# TriLink does not chase every transaction.

# AI identifies opportunities.
# AI analyses economics.
# AI identifies risk.
# AI prepares the commercial package.
# Approved automation executes routine workflows.
# Founder / Chairman / CEO controls material decisions.

# The objective is:

# PROFITABLE
# REPEATABLE
# VERIFIED
# SCALABLE
# AUDITABLE
# AI-OPERATED COMMERCE

# ============================================================
# END PART 34
# ============================================================


# ============================================================
# PART 35 — AI OPERATIONS, GOVERNANCE & FINAL CONTROL LAYER
# ============================================================

# PURPOSE
#
# This section defines the final operating rules for TriLink Global's
# AI-first business model.
#
# The system must operate through the Platformsify CEO Dashboard.
#
# Platformsify CEO Dashboard = SINGLE EXECUTIVE CONTROL CENTRE.
#
# No separate CEO dashboard may be created for TriLink.
#
# ============================================================
# 35.1 AI OPERATING MODEL
# ============================================================
#
# AI is responsible for approved operational workflows including:
#
# - lead processing
# - customer enquiries
# - supplier discovery
# - supplier comparison
# - quotation preparation
# - document preparation
# - shipment workflow preparation
# - logistics coordination
# - invoice preparation
# - payment status monitoring
# - customer communication drafts
# - supplier communication drafts
# - market intelligence
# - risk detection
# - KPI monitoring
# - management reporting
# - exception detection
# - workflow automation
# - internal knowledge retrieval
#
# AI must not assume unrestricted authority.
#
# AI authority is permission-based.
#
# ============================================================
# 35.2 FOUNDER / CHAIRMAN / CEO AUTHORITY
# ============================================================
#
# Founder / Chairman / CEO:
# Meheraj Uddin
#
# Ultimate authority remains with the Founder / Chairman / CEO.
#
# CEO approval is required for:
#
# - major contracts
# - major financial commitments
# - bank account changes
# - payment-method changes
# - major supplier approval
# - major customer approval
# - high-risk transactions
# - new-country expansion
# - strategic partnerships
# - ownership changes
# - senior executive appointments
# - AI permission escalation
# - regulatory exceptions
# - material legal decisions
#
# AI may recommend.
#
# AI may prepare.
#
# AI may analyse.
#
# AI may execute only where the Founder / Chairman / CEO has
# previously authorised the workflow and risk threshold.
#
# ============================================================
# 35.3 SINGLE DASHBOARD PRINCIPLE
# ============================================================
#
# Platformsify CEO Dashboard must provide the executive view of:
#
# - revenue
# - pipeline
# - customers
# - suppliers
# - shipments
# - logistics
# - energy opportunities
# - finance
# - invoices
# - payments
# - risk
# - compliance
# - AI activity
# - exceptions
# - KPIs
# - alerts
# - strategic opportunities
#
# TriLink must not build an independent competing CEO dashboard.
#
# TriLink-specific modules should integrate into the Platformsify
# CEO Dashboard operating layer.
#
# ============================================================
# 35.4 AI ACTION LOG
# ============================================================
#
# Every material AI action must produce an auditable record.
#
# Record:
#
# - timestamp
# - AI agent
# - user/account
# - workflow
# - action
# - data used
# - decision/recommendation
# - authority level
# - result
# - exception status
#
# Logs must be searchable by authorised management.
#
# ============================================================
# 35.5 APPROVAL MATRIX
# ============================================================
#
# LEVEL 0 — INFORMATION
# AI may operate automatically.
#
# Examples:
# - dashboard summaries
# - market research
# - KPI calculations
# - internal reporting
#
# LEVEL 1 — LOW RISK OPERATIONS
# AI may operate automatically within predefined limits.
#
# Examples:
# - customer acknowledgement
# - routine follow-up
# - document classification
# - internal workflow updates
#
# LEVEL 2 — COMMERCIAL PREPARATION
# AI prepares the action for approval.
#
# Examples:
# - quotations
# - supplier offers
# - contract drafts
# - commercial proposals
#
# LEVEL 3 — MATERIAL TRANSACTION
# CEO approval required.
#
# Examples:
# - major purchase
# - major sale
# - major shipment commitment
# - substantial payment
#
# LEVEL 4 — STRATEGIC / HIGH RISK
# Founder / Chairman / CEO approval required.
#
# Examples:
# - new country
# - major partnership
# - bank changes
# - ownership changes
# - major legal/regulatory decision
#
# ============================================================
# 35.6 EXCEPTION MANAGEMENT
# ============================================================
#
# AI must identify exceptions instead of silently continuing.
#
# Exceptions include:
#
# - unusual pricing
# - missing documentation
# - supplier inconsistency
# - customer risk
# - payment failure
# - shipment delay
# - compliance concern
# - suspicious activity
# - unusual transaction value
# - conflicting data
# - system failure
#
# When an exception occurs:
#
# 1. Detect
# 2. Classify
# 3. Record
# 4. Stop or contain the affected workflow
# 5. Notify the appropriate authority
# 6. Recommend next action
# 7. Resume only when authorised
#
# ============================================================
# 35.7 BUSINESS CONTINUITY
# ============================================================
#
# The business must continue safely if an AI service becomes
# unavailable.
#
# Required controls:
#
# - database backups
# - configuration backups
# - source-code version control
# - audit logs
# - recovery procedures
# - service health monitoring
# - documented emergency procedures
# - manual CEO override
#
# AI failure must never equal business failure.
#
# ============================================================
# 35.8 SECURITY PRINCIPLE
# ============================================================
#
# Security must follow least privilege.
#
# AI receives only the permissions required for its assigned task.
#
# Never expose:
#
# - secret keys
# - passwords
# - payment credentials
# - private authentication tokens
# - unnecessary customer information
# - unnecessary supplier information
#
# Secrets must remain in secure environment configuration or
# approved secret-management infrastructure.
#
# ============================================================
# 35.9 DATA GOVERNANCE
# ============================================================
#
# TriLink must maintain clear separation between:
#
# - customer data
# - supplier data
# - financial data
# - logistics data
# - employee data
# - operational data
# - AI-generated information
#
# AI-generated information must be distinguishable from verified
# source information where appropriate.
#
# Critical business decisions must be based on verified data.
#
# ============================================================
# 35.10 AI QUALITY CONTROL
# ============================================================
#
# AI outputs must be evaluated for:
#
# - accuracy
# - completeness
# - consistency
# - commercial reasonableness
# - regulatory suitability
# - source reliability
#
# High-impact decisions require human approval.
#
# ============================================================
# 35.11 CEO DAILY CONTROL VIEW
# ============================================================
#
# The Platformsify CEO Dashboard should present a concise executive
# control view containing:
#
# TODAY
# - revenue
# - orders
# - active shipments
# - cash/payment status
# - urgent exceptions
#
# PIPELINE
# - qualified opportunities
# - expected value
# - conversion rate
#
# OPERATIONS
# - shipments
# - supplier status
# - customer status
# - delays
#
# RISK
# - compliance alerts
# - financial alerts
# - operational alerts
#
# AI
# - actions completed
# - actions awaiting approval
# - exceptions
# - system health
#
# STRATEGY
# - major opportunities
# - expansion recommendations
# - management decisions required
#
# ============================================================
# 35.12 CEO DECISION QUEUE
# ============================================================
#
# The CEO Dashboard must clearly separate:
#
# APPROVED
# PENDING CEO APPROVAL
# BLOCKED
# FAILED
# COMPLETED
#
# Nothing requiring CEO approval should be hidden inside normal
# operational activity.
#
# ============================================================
# 35.13 AI AGENT STRUCTURE
# ============================================================
#
# Recommended logical AI agents:
#
# 1. CEO Intelligence Agent
# 2. Sales Agent
# 3. Customer Service Agent
# 4. Supplier Agent
# 5. Trade Agent
# 6. Logistics Agent
# 7. Energy Agent
# 8. Finance Agent
# 9. Compliance Agent
# 10. Risk Agent
# 11. Data Agent
# 12. Reporting Agent
#
# These are logical AI functions.
#
# They must remain controlled through the Platformsify technology
# operating layer and CEO Dashboard.
#
# ============================================================
# 35.14 NO UNCONTROLLED AUTONOMY
# ============================================================
#
# AI must never:
#
# - invent transactions
# - invent customers
# - invent suppliers
# - fabricate documents
# - approve restricted transactions without authority
# - change ownership
# - change banking information
# - bypass security
# - bypass compliance
# - conceal failures
# - delete audit evidence
#
# ============================================================
# 35.15 FINAL BUSINESS PRINCIPLE
# ============================================================
#
# TRI LINK GLOBAL
# =
# HUMAN STRATEGIC CONTROL
# +
# AI OPERATIONAL EXECUTION
# +
# PLATFORMSIFY TECHNOLOGY
# +
# AUDITABLE GOVERNANCE
#
# Founder / Chairman / CEO:
# Meheraj Uddin
#
# Technology Control Centre:
# Platformsify CEO Dashboard
#
# SINGLE EXECUTIVE CONTROL CENTRE:
# PLATFORMSIFY CEO DASHBOARD
#
# ============================================================
# END PART 35
# ============================================================

# PURPOSE
#
# This section converts the TriLink master plan into a controlled
# implementation and deployment checklist.
#
# The objective is NOT to create another management platform.
#
# Platformsify CEO Dashboard remains the SINGLE executive control
# centre for TriLink Global.
#

# 36.1 FINAL SYSTEM ARCHITECTURE
# ============================================================
#
# FOUNDER / CHAIRMAN / CEO
#          |
#          v
# PLATFORMSIFY CEO DASHBOARD
#          |
#          +----------------------+
#          |                      |
#          v                      v
#      AI CONTROL            BUSINESS DATA

#                     v

#                     |
#       +-------------+-------------+

#

# Infrastructure
#

#

#

#

#
# Stage 10:

#

# 36.3 PRE-DEPLOYMENT CHECKLIST
# ============================================================
#

# [ ] Git repository clean
# [ ] Production branch identified
# [ ] Environment variables documented
# [ ] Secrets removed from source code
# [ ] Authentication configured
# [ ] Authorisation configured

# [ ] Database backup verified

# [ ] Error handling tested

# 36.4 CEO DASHBOARD CHECKLIST
# ============================================================
#

# [ ] Executive KPIs load

# [ ] Pipeline view works
# [ ] Customer view works
# [ ] Supplier view works
# [ ] Shipment view works
# [ ] Energy view works
# [ ] Finance view works
# [ ] Risk view works
# [ ] Compliance view works
# [ ] AI activity view works
# [ ] Approval queue works
# [ ] Exception queue works
# [ ] System-health view works
#
# ============================================================
# 36.5 AI SAFETY CHECKLIST
# ============================================================
#
# [ ] AI has minimum required permissions
# [ ] AI cannot access unnecessary secrets
# [ ] AI actions are logged
# [ ] AI recommendations are identifiable
# [ ] High-risk actions require approval
# [ ] Payment actions require correct authority
# [ ] Banking changes require CEO approval
# [ ] Legal actions require human approval
# [ ] Compliance exceptions stop workflows
# [ ] AI cannot delete audit records
# [ ] AI cannot bypass authentication
# [ ] AI cannot escalate its own permissions
#
# ============================================================
# 36.6 COMMERCIAL READINESS
# ============================================================
#
# BEFORE accepting live commercial transactions:
#
# [ ] Legal company structure verified
# [ ] Required registrations verified
# [ ] Required licences verified
# [ ] Trade documentation verified
# [ ] Supplier onboarding process ready
# [ ] Customer onboarding process ready
# [ ] Pricing methodology ready
# [ ] Quotation process ready
# [ ] Contract process ready
# [ ] Invoice process ready
# [ ] Payment process ready
# [ ] Shipment documentation ready
# [ ] Dispute process ready
# [ ] Refund/cancellation process ready
#
# ============================================================
# 36.7 DATA QUALITY GATE
# ============================================================
#
# No production AI workflow should rely on uncontrolled data.
#
# Required:
#
# [ ] Customer records validated
# [ ] Supplier records validated
# [ ] Product records validated
# [ ] Pricing records validated
# [ ] Currency data validated
# [ ] Shipment data validated
# [ ] Financial data reconciled
# [ ] Duplicate records identified
# [ ] Missing critical fields identified
# [ ] Data ownership defined
#
# ============================================================
# 36.8 TEST ENVIRONMENT
# ============================================================
#
# All new AI workflows should first operate in a test/staging
# environment where possible.
#
# Test:
#
# - successful transactions
# - failed transactions
# - missing data
# - duplicate data
# - invalid permissions
# - payment failures
# - supplier rejection
# - customer cancellation
# - shipment delay
# - API failure
# - AI failure
# - database failure
#
# ============================================================
# 36.9 PRODUCTION RELEASE GATE
# ============================================================
#
# A feature may move to production only when:
#
# [ ] Development complete
# [ ] Tests passed
# [ ] Security reviewed
# [ ] Permissions reviewed
# [ ] Logging verified
# [ ] Error handling verified
# [ ] CEO Dashboard verified
# [ ] Rollback procedure available
# [ ] CEO approval obtained where required
#
# ============================================================
# 36.10 ROLLBACK PLAN
# ============================================================
#
# Every production deployment must have a rollback path.
#
# Required:
#
# - previous stable version
# - database recovery procedure
# - configuration backup
# - deployment history
# - incident log
# - responsible decision-maker
#
# If a deployment creates unacceptable risk:
#
# 1. Stop affected workflow
# 2. Protect customer/financial data
# 3. Disable affected AI action
# 4. Roll back where necessary
# 5. Investigate
# 6. Correct
# 7. Test
# 8. Re-authorise deployment
#
# ============================================================
# 36.11 PRODUCTION MONITORING
# ============================================================
#
# Monitor continuously:
#
# - uptime
# - response times
# - API failures
# - authentication failures
# - database health
# - AI errors
# - workflow failures
# - payment failures
# - shipment exceptions
# - unusual activity
# - security events
#
# Critical alerts must reach the appropriate management channel.
#
# ============================================================
# 36.12 BUSINESS KPI CONTROL
# ============================================================
#
# Core CEO KPIs:
#
# REVENUE
# - gross revenue
# - net revenue
# - revenue growth
#
# SALES
# - leads
# - qualified opportunities
# - conversion
# - average transaction value
#
# CUSTOMERS
# - active customers
# - repeat customers
# - customer acquisition
#
# SUPPLIERS
# - active suppliers
# - fulfilment reliability
# - pricing competitiveness
#
# LOGISTICS
# - active shipments
# - on-time delivery
# - delay rate
# - exception rate
#
# FINANCE
# - receivables
# - payables
# - cash position
# - transaction margins
#
# AI
# - automated workflows
# - successful AI actions
# - failed AI actions
# - pending approvals
# - exceptions
#
# ============================================================
# 36.13 AI PERFORMANCE REVIEW
# ============================================================
#
# AI performance must be measured.
#
# Track:
#
# - accuracy
# - completion rate
# - exception rate
# - human override rate
# - cost per workflow
# - time saved
# - customer response quality
# - commercial impact
#
# Poor-performing AI workflows must be reviewed or disabled.
#
# ============================================================
# 36.14 FOUNDER CONTROL
# ============================================================
#
# The Founder / Chairman / CEO must always be able to:
#
# - view the business
# - view AI activity
# - approve actions
# - reject actions
# - pause workflows
# - disable AI agents
# - review exceptions
# - review financial activity
# - review strategic recommendations
#
# The AI system must never make the Founder / Chairman / CEO
# operationally irrelevant.
#
# AI increases executive leverage.
#
# It does not replace ultimate corporate authority.
#
# ============================================================
# 36.15 FINAL GO-LIVE CHECK
# ============================================================
#
# [ ] TriLink business structure confirmed
# [ ] Founder / Chairman / CEO confirmed
# [ ] Platformsify CEO Dashboard confirmed as single control centre
# [ ] No competing CEO dashboard exists
# [ ] Core business modules identified
# [ ] AI agents defined
# [ ] Permission model defined
# [ ] Approval model defined
# [ ] Audit model defined
# [ ] Security model defined
# [ ] Data model defined
# [ ] Test environment available
# [ ] Production environment available
# [ ] Backups available
# [ ] Rollback available
# [ ] Monitoring available
# [ ] CEO approval workflow available
# [ ] Commercial readiness verified
# [ ] Regulatory requirements verified
# [ ] Final production test completed
#
# ============================================================
# 36.16 GO-LIVE RULE
# ============================================================
#
# TriLink Global must not be considered fully production-ready
# merely because the software deploys successfully.
#
# FULL GO-LIVE requires:
#
# TECHNOLOGY
# +
# SECURITY
# +
# DATA
# +
# OPERATIONS
# +
# COMMERCIAL READINESS
# +
# COMPLIANCE
# +
# HUMAN GOVERNANCE
#
# ============================================================
# 36.17 FINAL OPERATING MODEL
# ============================================================
#
# FOUNDER / CHAIRMAN / CEO
# MEHERAJ UDDIN
#
#              |
#              v
#
# PLATFORMSIFY CEO DASHBOARD
#

#
# ============================================================
# 36.18 MASTER PRINCIPLE
# ============================================================
#
# ONE BUSINESS
# ONE EXECUTIVE CONTROL CENTRE
# ONE AI OPERATING MODEL
# ONE GOVERNANCE FRAMEWORK
# ONE AUDIT TRAIL
#
# Platformsify CEO Dashboard is the executive technology layer.
#
# TriLink Global remains the business.
#
# The Founder / Chairman / CEO remains the ultimate authority.
#
# AI operates the approved workflows.
#

# ============================================================
# PART 38 — IMPLEMENTATION CONTROL & BUILD EXECUTION
# ============================================================

# PURPOSE
#
# This section defines how the approved TriLink master plan is
# converted into the working production system.
#
# The implementation must follow the architecture already defined.
#
# NO SECOND CEO DASHBOARD.
#
# Platformsify CEO Dashboard remains the SINGLE executive control
# centre.
#
# ============================================================
# 38.1 IMPLEMENTATION ORDER
# ============================================================
#
# The build must proceed in the following order:
#
# 01. Repository verification
# 02. Application verification
# 03. Environment verification
# 04. Database verification
# 05. Authentication
# 06. Authorisation
# 07. Core business data
# 08. Platformsify CEO Dashboard integration
# 09. AI read-only intelligence
# 10. AI recommendation workflows
# 11. Low-risk automation
# 12. Approval workflows
# 13. Trade workflows
# 14. Logistics workflows
# 15. Energy workflows
# 16. Finance workflows
# 17. Risk/compliance workflows
# 18. Audit logging
# 19. Production monitoring
# 20. End-to-end testing
# 21. Controlled production release
#
# ============================================================
# 38.2 REPOSITORY VERIFICATION
# ============================================================
#
# Confirm:
#
# [ ] Correct repository
# [ ] Correct branch
# [ ] Working tree status
# [ ] Git remote
# [ ] Latest commit
# [ ] No accidental untracked secrets
# [ ] Master plan present
#
# Recommended commands:
#
# git status
# git branch --show-current
# git remote -v
# git log -1 --oneline
#
# ============================================================
# 38.3 APPLICATION VERIFICATION
# ============================================================
#
# Confirm:
#
# [ ] Node version
# [ ] npm version
# [ ] package.json
# [ ] package-lock.json
# [ ] Build scripts
# [ ] Development server
# [ ] Production build
#
# Required principle:
#
# Development success does not equal production readiness.
#
# ============================================================
# 38.4 ENVIRONMENT CONTROL
# ============================================================
#
# Production secrets must NEVER be committed to Git.
#
# Verify:
#
# [ ] Environment variables documented
# [ ] Local development environment configured
# [ ] Production environment configured
# [ ] Secrets stored securely
# [ ] Public variables separated from private variables
# [ ] API keys restricted
# [ ] Database credentials protected
#
# Never place real secrets inside this master plan.
#
# ============================================================
# 38.5 DATABASE CONTROL
# ============================================================
#
# The production database must have:
#
# [ ] Schema
# [ ] Authentication relationship
# [ ] Customer records
# [ ] Supplier records
# [ ] Product records
# [ ] Orders
# [ ] Shipments
# [ ] Invoices
# [ ] Payments
# [ ] AI activity
# [ ] Audit events
# [ ] Exceptions
#
# Every important record must have:
#
# - unique identifier
# - creation timestamp
# - update timestamp
# - ownership/access rules
# - status
#
# ============================================================
# 38.6 AUTHENTICATION
# ============================================================
#
# Required roles:
#
# FOUNDER_CEO
# ADMIN
# OPERATIONS
# FINANCE
# SALES
# LOGISTICS
# COMPLIANCE
# AI_AGENT
#
# Roles must not automatically receive unrestricted access.
#
# ============================================================
# 38.7 AUTHORISATION
# ============================================================
#
# Authorisation must be enforced server-side.
#
# Never rely only on frontend controls.
#
# Verify:
#
# [ ] User identity
# [ ] Role
# [ ] Resource ownership
# [ ] Action permission
# [ ] Approval requirement
#
# ============================================================
# 38.8 PLATFORMSIFY CEO DASHBOARD INTEGRATION
# ============================================================
#
# The TriLink system must feed the executive information required
# by the Platformsify CEO Dashboard.
#
# Executive information includes:
#
# - revenue
# - pipeline
# - customers
# - suppliers
# - orders
# - shipments
# - finance
# - energy opportunities
# - risk
# - compliance
# - AI activity
# - approvals
# - exceptions
#
# TriLink must not build a replacement executive dashboard.
#
# ============================================================
# 38.9 AI READ-ONLY PHASE
# ============================================================
#
# The first production AI phase should be READ-ONLY.
#
# AI may:
#
# - analyse
# - summarise
# - identify trends
# - detect anomalies
# - prepare recommendations
# - prepare reports
#
# AI must not initially execute material business actions.
#
# This phase validates:
#
# [ ] Data quality
# [ ] AI accuracy
# [ ] Dashboard integration
# [ ] Reporting
# [ ] Auditability
#
# ============================================================
# 38.10 AI RECOMMENDATION PHASE
# ============================================================
#
# Once read-only intelligence is validated:
#
# AI may recommend:
#
# - suppliers
# - pricing
# - customer follow-ups
# - logistics actions
# - trade opportunities
# - energy opportunities
# - risk responses
#
# Recommendations must include:
#
# - reason
# - supporting data
# - confidence where appropriate
# - expected impact
# - risk
# - required approval
#
# ============================================================
# 38.11 LOW-RISK AUTOMATION PHASE
# ============================================================
#
# Approved low-risk workflows may then be automated.
#
# Examples:
#
# - acknowledgements
# - routine notifications
# - internal task creation
# - document classification
# - status updates
# - scheduled reporting
#
# All automated actions remain logged.
#
# ============================================================
# 38.12 APPROVAL WORKFLOW
# ============================================================
#
# For actions requiring approval:
#
# AI PREPARES
#       |
#       v
# CEO DASHBOARD
#       |
#       v
# FOUNDER / AUTHORISED USER
#       |
#    +--+--+
#    |     |
# APPROVE REJECT
#    |     |
#    v     v
# EXECUTE STOP
#
# The system must retain the decision record.
#
# ============================================================
# 38.13 TRADE BUILD
# ============================================================
#
# Trade workflow:
#
# LEAD
# ->
# CUSTOMER VERIFICATION
# ->
# PRODUCT
# ->
# SUPPLIER
# ->
# PRICING
# ->
# QUOTATION
# ->
# APPROVAL
# ->
# CONTRACT
# ->
# ORDER
# ->
# PAYMENT
# ->
# SHIPMENT
# ->
# DELIVERY
# ->
# INVOICE/RECONCILIATION
# ->
# COMPLETION
#
# Exceptions must branch into the risk/approval workflow.
#
# ============================================================
# 38.14 LOGISTICS BUILD
# ============================================================
#
# Logistics workflow:
#
# ORDER
# ->
# SHIPMENT CREATION
# ->
# DOCUMENTATION
# ->
# CARRIER / TRANSPORT
# ->
# TRACKING
# ->
# BORDER / COMPLIANCE
# ->
# DELIVERY
# ->
# CONFIRMATION
# ->
# CLOSE
#
# AI may monitor status and identify delays.
#
# Material exceptions require escalation.
#
# ============================================================
# 38.15 ENERGY BUILD
# ============================================================
#
# Energy workflow:
#
# OPPORTUNITY
# ->
# PROJECT SCREENING
# ->
# COUNTERPARTY VERIFICATION
# ->
# TECHNICAL / COMMERCIAL ANALYSIS
# ->
# RISK REVIEW
# ->
# PROPOSAL
# ->
# CEO APPROVAL
# ->
# CONTRACT
# ->
# EXECUTION
# ->
# MONITORING
#
# Energy must use the same central governance system.

# ============================================================
# PART 39 — AI GOVERNANCE, SECURITY & AUDIT
# ============================================================

# 39.1 AI GOVERNANCE
#
# TriLink AI must operate under defined permissions.
# AI does not receive unlimited authority.
#
# Every AI workflow must have:
# - defined purpose
# - defined inputs
# - defined outputs
# - permission scope
# - spending limits where applicable
# - escalation rules
# - audit logging
# - human override
#
# ============================================================
# 39.2 AI PERMISSION LEVELS
# ============================================================
#
# LEVEL 0 — OBSERVE
# AI can analyse information and generate recommendations.
#
# LEVEL 1 — ASSIST
# AI can prepare documents, messages, quotations and reports.
#
# LEVEL 2 — OPERATE
# AI can execute approved routine operational workflows.
#
# LEVEL 3 — EXECUTIVE RECOMMENDATION
# AI prepares recommendations for CEO approval.
#
# LEVEL 4 — HUMAN APPROVAL REQUIRED
# Major financial, legal, contractual or strategic actions require
# Founder / Chairman / CEO approval.
#
# AI must never silently escalate its own permissions.
#
# ============================================================
# 39.3 HIGH-RISK ACTIONS
# ============================================================
#
# Human approval is required for:
# - major payments
# - bank account changes
# - ownership changes
# - major contracts
# - legal commitments
# - regulatory submissions
# - high-value procurement
# - exceptional discounts
# - new-country launches
# - major partnerships
# - senior executive appointments
# - changes to AI authority
#
# ============================================================
# 39.4 AUDIT TRAIL
# ============================================================
#
# Every material AI action must record:
# - timestamp
# - workflow
# - AI agent
# - user/system initiating action
# - input
# - decision
# - output
# - approval status
# - financial impact where applicable
# - escalation status
#
# Audit records must be searchable from the Platformsify CEO Dashboard.
#
# ============================================================
# 39.5 SECURITY
# ============================================================
#
# Security requirements:
# - least-privilege access
# - role-based permissions
# - secure authentication
# - encrypted sensitive information
# - protected secrets
# - environment separation
# - production access controls
# - activity logging
# - backup and recovery procedures
#
# Secrets must never be hard-coded into source files.
#
# ============================================================
# 39.6 AI SAFETY
# ============================================================
#
# AI must:
# - identify uncertainty
# - avoid fabricating commercial facts
# - distinguish verified data from estimates
# - escalate ambiguous high-risk situations
# - preserve customer and supplier confidentiality
# - respect regulatory requirements
# - maintain an auditable decision history
#
# ============================================================
# 39.7 CEO OVERRIDE
# ============================================================
#
# The Founder / Chairman / CEO must have an emergency override
# capability through the Platformsify CEO Dashboard.
#
# CEO controls include:
# - pause AI workflow
# - disable agent
# - revoke permission
# - suspend transaction
# - require human approval
# - review audit trail
# - restore approved workflow
#
# ============================================================
# 39.8 GOVERNANCE OBJECTIVE
# ============================================================
#
# TriLink should be highly automated without becoming uncontrolled.
#
# PRINCIPLE:
#
# AI runs approved operations.
# The CEO controls authority.
# The dashboard provides visibility.
# The audit system provides accountability.
#
# ============================================================


# ============================================================
# PART 40 — FINAL AI OPERATING MODEL & LAUNCH READINESS
# ============================================================

# 40.1 FINAL BUSINESS OPERATING MODEL
#
# TriLink Global operates as an AI-first international business.
#
# CORE BUSINESS FUNCTIONS:
#
# 1. STRATEGY

#

# 4. PRIORITISE
# 5. EXECUTE APPROVED TASKS
# 6. ESCALATE EXCEPTIONS
# 7. RECORD ACTIONS
# 8. REPORT RESULTS

# HIGH

# LOW
